# S6 staging API second re-pin packet (REPIN-2)

Packet ID: **S6-STAGING-API-REPIN-2**. Status: **REVIEWED; INSTALLED REVISION 1; NOT RUN; NO GO**. Revision 1. Nothing in this packet is a GO.

**Derived from revision 8.** This packet is derived from packet S6-STAGING-API-REPIN-1 revision 8 (installed file `docs/S6_STAGING_API_REPIN_PACKET_2026-10-28.md`, LF blob sha256 `ea6b463f`). Revision 8 section 2.4 says that a second re-pin needs its own packet; this is that packet. Revision 8 and its Root rulings R1 to R34 are the base: where this packet says "as revision 8 section N", that text applies unchanged with the family names of this packet, and the sections copied below keep their revision 8 numbers. The revision 1 to 8 change lists and the revision 7 and 8 build histories are not copied; they stay in the revision 8 file. The revision 8 file stays byte-frozen, because its bytes are the run-time contract of the sealed RA(r8) of section 4A. Basis: the REPIN-2 change map of 30 October 2026 (Shape R, chosen by the owner) and Root rulings RP1 to RP10 of 30 October 2026 (RP3 amends RP1 for one minimal A1 code change, which RP4 accepts as built and RP7 accepts after the independent tester run; the A1 rebuild copies that build by value; section 6.1 item 3). Contract sentence: revision 1 of this packet, after installation as `docs/S6_STAGING_API_REPIN_2_PACKET_2026-10-30.md` (Root ruling RP2, E7), is the build contract of the REPIN-2 tools; revision 8 stays the contract of RA(r8). `PACKET_SHA` is the sha256 of this file's final LF blob; Root records it in the tool pins and the acceptance record, never in this file.

After an accepted RA receipt, or such an A2 receipt, R5a, R4, R4H, RM and RC are valid again with their own rules; RX needs a fresh job ID and manifest; the families retired by this packet stay retired. ## 1. Purpose and limits

Purpose: put the QR-2 boundary record (the registry of `5ac648b`, the per-Bot reason note of `6817c0a` and the values commit), together with the content of revision 8 (the QR-2 refusal fix `281110e`, the panel hint `30cd728` with the hint changes of ruling L2, and the bar-coverage degrade D), on the staging API at pin P2, after the RA(r8) of section 4A has returned the staging API to `ba1a433`, while the trading worker keeps its `ba1a433` tree and its process.

Limits:
- Spot/Paper only; Live hard-locked; no change to Pine, Risk Manager, the research flags or any budget value.
- Production units, the production database and the loopback quant bridge are untouched and identity-checked before and after each effect, against the r6 a02 baseline (combined digest `b5789332`, as re-read at c6) or the latest Root-reviewed receipt that carries it.
- One manager reload in A2 and one in RA; at most two in either job with an in-run unwind; no other reload by the jobs of this packet; RA(r8) (section 4A) is a revision 8 job with the same limit. Each reload is host-wide and shared with the production units. A1, A2M and AR run no reload.
- The trading worker, the market stream, the fallback collector service, the fallback collector timer and the research worker get no control verb.
- The QR-2 flag stays effective 1 from the P5a flag layer after A2 and after RA.
- No migration, no database write by the tools, no dump.
- One-use runner pattern of main packet section 7: manifest, consumed marker, nonce, receipt, envelope, retained output, 120-second Root argv GO window.
- Host work only by the single designated operations worker, serially, outside any fan-out.
- Private values stay out of tracked files.

## 2. Pin rule, pair test and re-map rule

### 2.1 Pin (value `75c7a5b336ca6653c0e748cf2b88e1e0ff3b4ac5`; placeholder `[pin]` in the GO templates)

The pin P2 is one exact commit: `75c7a5b336ca6653c0e748cf2b88e1e0ff3b4ac5` (`75c7a5b`, the values commit "feat(qr2): add the staging QR-2 boundary record from deployment evidence"). Root sets it (change map step 5); this installation text carries the full commit (Root ruling RP9). The GO templates of section 14 keep `[pin]`, which Root fills with that commit when it writes each GO request.
- **Pin:** the integrated, reviewed and CI-green values commit that carries the staging boundary record, on top of `5ac648b` and `6817c0a`, and so also everything of `33e5a8c` (F, the hint changes of ruling L2 and D). Every GO line states "includes the QR-2 422 range refusal and panel hint: yes; includes the QR-2 bar-coverage degrade D: yes; includes the QR-2 boundary record: yes".
- **Values commit shape.** The values commit changes the registry entry (values from the accepted BR result), a sanitized tracked BR result record named as the record's `evidence`, the golden digest U4, tests and the RC-Q46 and RC-Q47 contract text. It must not touch `public/`, `src/config.js`, `src/postgres/server.js` or any environment read. A change to `public/` moves the cache tokens beyond `qr2o10` (case 5 again, with new test pins); a change to any of the others is re-map case 1, and this design is void.
- **BR dependency.** The record values come from the read-only boundary read of packet S6-QR2-BOUNDARY-READ-1 (job BR, its own GO-BR). That packet is separate. This packet's chain starts only after Root accepts the BR result and the values commit is integrated. There is no fallback pin: without an accepted BR result there is no P2 and no GO of this packet. Done: BR a01 ended BR_PASS on 30 October 2026 and Root accepted it; result record `docs/S6_QR2_BOUNDARY_READ_1_A01_RESULT_2026-10-30.md` and evidence `docs/evidence/S6_QR2_BOUNDARY_READ_1_A01_RESULT_2026-10-30.json`, which the record `STAGING_QL_P2_PROTOTYPE_20261030` of `75c7a5b` names as its `evidence`.
- **Ancestry.** `ba1a433` must be an ancestor of P2, and `a877733` must not be (the A1 tool constants `REQUIRED_ANCESTOR` and `FORBIDDEN_ANCESTOR` stay).

Before the pin is set, Root re-reads, from the pushed revision, exact-revision CI with every check of the named set completed with success (9 of 9 today), and the Root acceptance records for every slice between `ba1a433` and the pin that touches `src`, `public`, `scripts`, `migrations` or the package files. `ba1a433` must be an ancestor of the pin, and `a877733` must not be. Artifacts are built from Git blobs of the pin only. Every revision-dependent constant (tree name, manifest, served list, cache tokens, auth-route set, engine identities, startup-probe inputs) is derived from the pin, never copied. Root writes the A0 neutrality record at pin time (section 4).

**Pin set.** P2 = `75c7a5b336ca6653c0e748cf2b88e1e0ff3b4ac5` (`75c7a5b`). Root's records of exact-revision CI and of the slice acceptances at that revision (paragraph above) are the basis; this packet does not restate them.

Every GO line shows the pin and both revisions: the staging API at the pin, the trading worker at `ba1a433`. Any change of the pin after a GO voids that GO (ruling D1).

### 2.2 Expected facts for P2 (the A0' record is the fact of record)

Under Shape R the running API tree at this packet's A2 is `ba1a433` (after RA(r8)), so the API side is compared with `ba1a433`, not with `33e5a8c` (change map G10). The worker side is compared with `ba1a433` as before. The values below are expectations from the read of 30 October 2026 at `6817c0a`; the A0' record, from Git blobs of P2 with two independent derivations, is the fact of record.
- **Runtime difference `ba1a433..P2`:** the 7 files of the revision 8 A0 record (`public/i18n.js`, `public/index.html`, `public/paper-performance.js`, `src/portfolio/qr2-coverage-public.js`, `src/portfolio/qr2-coverage-snapshot.js`, `src/portfolio/qr2-coverage.js`, `src/postgres/qr2-coverage-reader.js`), plus the new `src/portfolio/qr2-boundary-records.js`, `src/portfolio/qr2-response.js` (the first-failure reason crosses the response, enum-filtered), and `src/postgres/qr2-routes.js` and `src/postgres/qr2-owner-route.js` (import and dependency default only). No migration, no package or lock change, no Python input change.
- **Cache tokens:** in `public/index.html` the tokens of `i18n.js` and `paper-performance.js` move from `qr2o7` (the `ba1a433` value) to `qr2o10`; the CSS token stays `qr2o7`.
- **Trading worker closure** from `src/postgres/worker-main.js`: 39 files at both revisions, list sha256 `fd1ce5e3`, no QR-2 file, no file differs.
- **API closure** from `src/postgres/server.js`: 148 files (146 JavaScript files and 2 SQL files; 147 at `33e5a8c`), so the Node syntax-check set grows from 145 to 146. Root derives every count from P2.
- **Paper write-path closure, method version 2:** the union is byte-equal between `ba1a433` and P2 (no Paper write entry imports a changed file); A0' proves it by the closure scan.
- **N2:** `src/postgres/server.js`, `src/postgres/worker-main.js`, `src/config.js`, `src/postgres/schema.sql`, `migrations/` and the package files are byte-equal. The registry validates its records when the module loads. That is not a startup assert in the N2 sense: it adds no `verifySchema`, fence, lock, environment key, route or worker setting. A bad record throws at import, and the API then fails to start (residual, section 12). A0' states this reasoning explicitly.
- **N3:** package files and Python inputs blob-equal; the dependency archive is the P1 a02 archive.
- **N4:** every changed runtime file is in `public/` or in the QR-2 read path. The new module's importers are `qr2-routes.js` and `qr2-owner-route.js` only, which `src/postgres/server.js` imports.

### 2.3 General pair test

The API and the trading worker move as a pair when, between the trading worker's running tree and the pin, any of these differ: a file in the worker's runtime import closure; `schema.sql` or any migration; the fence module; the database layer; the form of a table that one unit writes and the other reads; an environment key read by the worker closure. Otherwise the API may move alone. The main packet section 4 rule ("move as a pair") rests on the `40e3b6b` fence crossing and is scoped to it.

### 2.4 Re-map rule for a later or different pin

Compare the pin with `ba1a433` for the trading worker closure, and with the running API tree for the API. Apply the first matching case, most restrictive first:
1. **N2 change** (migration, SQL file, startup assert, environment key, route, worker setting): this design is void; the main packet full form applies.
2. **N1 change, or N1 unknown:** move to the pair variant. That is a new packet revision, with the counter baseline and the new-rows refusal rules back.
3. **N3 change** (package files or Python inputs): rebuild and review the dependency archive; the design is otherwise unchanged.
4. **A request range that newly computes on staging** (ruling R3 item 5; A-P3-6, S-P3-8): any change, in any file, that lets a request compute that the running tree refuses, including a budget change in `src/portfolio/qr2-coverage-limits.js`. Stays API-only, but A0 records which ranges newly compute under the pin and Root's estimate of compute memory and CPU on the shared host. The A2 gate re-checks the API memory headroom with that estimate in place of 300 MB when it is larger. When the staging API has no memory limit, the bound is the host available-memory floor: the host's available memory must exceed that floor by at least the estimate. A guard value is never lowered to fit. For P2, against the running API tree `ba1a433` after RA(r8), case 4 applies to the D ranges as revision 8 section 2.2 lists them and, by analogy, to the reconciliation compute of the boundary record (section 2.5).
5. **`public/` change:** rebuild the served list and cache tokens.
6. **Docs-only change:** rebuild the constants.
7. **QR-2 read-path source change, N4 holds:** stays API-only; rebuild the manifest and constants.

Cases 4 to 7 combine: a pin with changes of more than one of these kinds takes each matching case. P2 takes cases 4 (by analogy for the reconciliation compute), 5, 6 and 7.

A pin change after a GO voids that GO. A further re-pin while this packet's re-pin layer is live needs its own packet: its expected list is this packet's post-A2 list, and the layer it supersedes is this packet's layer. This packet answers revision 8 section 2.4 by Shape R: RA(r8) first, so its expected list is the post-P5a list again (section 4A).

### 2.5 Re-map record for P2 (A0' content; Root ruling RP1, Q-E)

- **Trees compared:** `ba1a433` for the API (the running tree after RA(r8)) and for the trading worker.
- **Case 5:** the served list and the cache tokens are rebuilt (`qr2o10` for `i18n.js` and `paper-performance.js`; CSS `qr2o7`).
- **Case 6:** docs, the contract rows RC-Q46 and RC-Q47 and the golden digest; constants rebuilt.
- **Case 7:** the new `src/portfolio/qr2-boundary-records.js`, `src/portfolio/qr2-response.js`, `src/postgres/qr2-routes.js` and `src/postgres/qr2-owner-route.js` (import and default only), with the QR-2 read-path files of revision 8; manifest and constants rebuilt.
- **Case 4, by analogy.** By its letter (a range that the running tree refuses), case 4 applies again to the D ranges, because the running tree is `ba1a433`: they are the ranges of revision 8 section 2.2. By analogy (compute load, not range admission), it applies to the boundary record: newly computing work is the full reconciliation and financial compute for accounts that halted at `EPOCH_UNKNOWN` under `UNRECORDED`, in every range (Week, Month, Year, All Time, Custom), on the per-Bot route; the owner route is unchanged in effect (503 without M6). The snapshot read is the same (read limits unchanged); the compute per request grows: every fill replayed, lots, the NAV grid, TWR and MWR, drawdown.
- **Bound, unchanged:** one compute per API process (single slot, refused, not queued); worker heap caps old generation 256 MB, young generation 32 MB, stack 4 MB; deadline 10000 ms; the read limits. No guard value is lowered.
- **Estimate: 384 MB of memory**, kept unless the A0' local measurement exceeds it; then Root sets a new estimate, never a lowered guard. Basis: the revision 8 basis (revision 8 section 2.2), and A2M a01 of revision 8: API resident memory 176 MiB after the owner's requests, with Bots 2 and 3 halting at their first fill; host available memory about 6.9 GB; the staging API has no memory limit, so the A2 gate checks the host's available memory above the host available-memory floor by at least 384 MB.
- **Local measurement** (A0' item, before Root accepts the REPIN-2 tools): a rehearsal from P2 with a fixture sized from the BR result (fills per account, earliest and latest instants, the record's epochs) that records the worker's peak heap and resident memory and the All Time compute time against the 10000 ms deadline. If the time is near the deadline (over 5000 ms, the finding margin of Root ruling RP2, E3), Root rules before GO-RA(r8): the owner would see a compute timeout instead of a report (residual, section 12).
- **Per-Bot expectation** (A0' item): for each Bot with Paper fills, Root records from the tracked BR result either "reconciles" or "`EPOCH_UNKNOWN` at a named fill class" (a fill before E0, or a fill in the 12.248-second switch gap). Root maps each Bot to its BR scope by `scopes[].slot` (the UI Bot n); if a slot has more than one BR scope, Root rules before GO-A2 (audit d2 P3-2). Root derives the class per scope from `scopes[].preE0`, `scopes[].preE0LastAt`, `scopes[].hasP2Signal` and `scopes[].slot` (BR Q1), `edges[].at` (BR Q2) and the scope x bucket `cells[]` (BR Q3; buckets `PRE_E0` and `GAP`) (Root ruling RP2, E4; audit d1 P3-3; audit d2 P3-1). It also records the expected parity counts of section 6.9 item 3c per Bot: `scopes[].fills`; `lots[].lots` (BR Q5); the sum of `cells[].n` with marker ABSENT over every bucket of the scope; the sum of `cells[].n` whose marker disagrees with the bucket epoch (0 under an accepted BR); the BR read time `db.observedAtMs`; and any `COST_CLAMPED` that the Q5 price classes predict. The accepted BR a01 result (`docs/evidence/S6_QR2_BOUNDARY_READ_1_A01_RESULT_2026-10-30.json`, BR_PASS; record `docs/S6_QR2_BOUNDARY_READ_1_A01_RESULT_2026-10-30.md`) has two scopes. Bot slot 3 (`scopeOrd` 2): 24 fills (12 buys, 12 sells) from 2026-10-03T12:02:02.172Z to 2026-10-08T02:05:07.815Z, `preE0` 0, `hasP2Signal` true, cells only in bucket E0 (BUY 12, SELL 12, marker ABSENT), 12 lots: expectation "reconciles", with the parity counts `fill_count` 24 and AGREES 0, each plus only fills after `db.observedAtMs` (2026-10-30T14:16:19.956Z); `lot_count` 12 plus only lots opened after it; ABSENT 24 and DISAGREES 0 exactly. Bot slot 2 (`scopeOrd` 1): 3 fills (2 buys, 1 sell), all before E0 `from` (`preE0` 3; first 2026-09-26T11:37:02.310Z, `preE0LastAt` 2026-09-27T00:34:02.322Z), `hasP2Signal` false, cells only in bucket `PRE_E0` (BUY 2, SELL 1, marker ABSENT), 2 lots: expectation "`EPOCH_UNKNOWN` at class pre-E0", with `failed_from` expected at its first fill, before E0 `from`, the parity counts `fill_count` 3 and `lot_count` 2, and expected marker counts 0, because no slot 2 cell has its `maxAt` before that first fill. The BR read refuted the E0 start for slot 2 only (U1) and found an earlier E0 start NOT PROVEN; the owner chose to keep the proven record, so Bot 2 is the one Bot predicted `EPOCH_UNKNOWN`. No scope has a `GAP` cell or any fill at or after the gap. In Q5 every lot pair is a pair with fee, with no tie, no missing quote and no quantity difference. The record instants of `75c7a5b` (E0 from 2026-10-01T16:16:00Z to 2026-10-14T22:17:41.152Z, E1 from 2026-10-14T22:17:53.400Z to 2026-10-26T04:49:37.825Z, E2 open from there) equal the BR manifest instants, so the BR Q9 rule for moved edges does not apply. This expectation is the input of Root's checks and of the owner check (section 6.9).

## 3. Jobs

| Job | Effect | Requires | Units touched | GO | Stop point |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| A0 (Root, local) | None. Pin facts, the A0' neutrality record, re-map cases, CI, acceptance records, local rehearsal and measurement, per-Bot expectation | Accepted BR result; the values commit integrated, reviewed and CI-green; P2 set | none | none | A0' record written and hashed |
| RA(r8) (revision 8 job, section 4A) | Archive the revision 8 re-pin layer; API back on `ba1a433` with the flag effective 1 | Every REPIN-2 tool accepted; the RA(r8) manifest from the reviewed A2 a01 receipt; the sealed-tool identity check; dry admission | staging API only | GO-RA(r8) (owner-typed) | Accepted RA receipt (section 4A), or another revision 8 end outcome |
| AR | None. Read-only reference reading: a01 required after the accepted RA(r8) receipt and before GO-A1; later only after a drift at an A1 or A2 gate or at the A1 end reading, or before GO-A2 when more than 6 hours pass after AR a01 (section 6.12) | AR tool accepted (built with the A2 tool package); for a01, the accepted RA(r8) receipt | none | GO-AR | Classes, process digests, restart counts and configuration digests returned |
| A1 | Transport one package; stage one new API tree, inert; read the layer-name boolean; export the process references | A0' record; A1 tool accepted; A2, A2M, RA and AR tool accepted; accepted RA(r8) receipt; reference: the Root-accepted AR a01 record or a later Root-accepted AR record (reference kind `AR_RECORD`) | none | GO-A1 | Tree staged exact; boolean and references returned; all units unchanged (after a difference at the end reading: the staged tree, then GO-AR; section 5.1) |
| A2 | API switch with immediate and delayed checks; RC-Q36 memory numbers before and after the switch | A1 accepted with the boolean true (no exception); reference: the accepted A1 record or a later Root-accepted AR record; A2, A2M, RA and AR tool accepted; retirement inventory written and pinned; U-4 carry recorded; local rehearsal and measurement recorded | staging API only | GO-A2 (owner-typed) | Delayed check passed (or a market stop code of section 6.4) |
| A2M | None. Read-only memory job after the owner's browser steps | A2 receipt reviewed; owner browser steps reported; Root's timing record written | none | GO-A2 (A2M clause) | Numbers and booleans returned |
| RA | Archive this packet's re-pin layer; API back on `ba1a433` with the flag effective 1 | RA accepted (built with A2) | staging API only | GO-RA | API ready on the post-P5a configuration (or another end outcome of section 8) |

**Families.** `S6-STAGING-API-REPIN-2-A1`, `-A2`, `-A2M`, `-AR` and `-RA`. RA(r8) is a job of the revision 8 family `S6-STAGING-API-REPIN-1-RA` and follows revision 8. Each effect GO is separate because the risks differ: A1 writes files only; A2 changes the code that serves the owner; RA withdraws it. A2M and AR have no effect on any unit; A2M runs under GO-A2 and AR under its own GO-AR. Each job has its own job ID (attempt a01 first). No retry under any GO; contingency job IDs exist only after a pre-effect stop with no effect, and, for RA only, under the rollback-family rule of section 8 (after an RA attempt that did not end with the API ready on `ba1a433`, except after `RA_UNWIND_FAILED` and `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE`, as section 8 states; a production-identity difference at the RA gate also ends `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE`, so the next RA needs Root's recorded review in its manifest although that stop had no effect; ruling R4 item 1; ruling R5 items 2 and 3; ruling R6 item 4); after a lost receipt the next job ID of the same family waits 1335 seconds (every family of this packet: A1, A2, A2M, AR and RA; section 11.1).

Each tool pins this packet and also checks that the main packet still hashes to `e8a18a90d047a43d46213d00f46a981842da8ef0c58779030c730e8df7cf8dc4`, the follow-up packet to `ebc707c8e7b75602dbac80a325b5cae2bcb1d1b02d157e7d90615c60462d8c50` and the collector packet to `a296989ee0194107498680698194b93fed6f60515d6f7e3121e8d9d8b1b0a741` (sha256 of the working-copy bytes at HEAD `79934a1`). Root re-hashes the LF Git blobs before any tool pins them and records the values in the tool pin.

## 4. A0: Root, local, read only (the A0' record for P2)

Root does A0 in the checkout at pin time, from Git blobs only (never working copies), and writes one ignored local A0 record under `.qa-local/`. Root pins the sha256 of that record in the A1, AR, A2 (with A2M) and RA manifests. A0 has no host step. In this packet the A0 record is the A0' record for P2; its baseline is `ba1a433` for both the API and the trading worker (section 2.2; change map G10).

**Neutrality record.** A pin P is neutral against the trading worker's tree `ba1a433` when all of these hold:
- **N1. Worker and Paper write closure unchanged.** No file in the transitive static import closure of the trading worker entry, and no file in the Paper write-path closure defined below, differs between `ba1a433` and P. The scan follows relative `import ... from`, `export ... from`, bare `import`, literal `import()`, `new URL(..., import.meta.url)` and `.sql` reads. A non-literal dynamic import in any closure file makes N1 unknown, and unknown counts as not neutral.
- **N2. No migration or startup change.** No migration module, SQL file, migration or rollback script, `src/config.js`, startup assert (`verifySchema`, `requirePaperLedgerFence`, the runtime and maintenance locks), environment key read, route table or worker setting differs.
- **N3. Dependencies unchanged.** `package.json`, the lock file and the Python inputs are field-equal (the P1 dependency rule), and the dependency archive built from the pin is byte-equal to the one staged by P1 attempt a02.
- **N4. Changes only in public or QR-2 read files.** Every changed runtime file is in `public/` or in the QR-2 read path (modules imported only by the QR-2 routes, readers and compute worker).

**Paper write-path closure, method version 2** (ruling R2, P2-2; ruling R3 item 4, A-P2-3). Derived from Git at both `ba1a433` and P with this rule:
1. **Paper write table set W, by name only, wherever the table is created:** every table whose name begins with `paper_` (for example funding, cash journal, snapshots, the fence and capital models), `ledger_` or `pine_bridge_`, plus `fills`, `positions`, `signals`, `daily_stats`, `bot_sessions` and `bot_session_archive`, matched schema-qualified or not. The creation site is not a condition. (Method version 1 required creation in `src/postgres/schema.sql` or in a `.sql` file of the API closure; that left out the fence table, which a JavaScript template string creates, and the capital-model table, whose `.sql` file is not in the API closure because a migration module reads it through a non-literal URL helper.)
2. **Entry modules:** every JavaScript module of the API closure whose source contains a write statement (`INSERT INTO`, `UPDATE ... SET`, `DELETE FROM`, `TRUNCATE`, `MERGE INTO` or `COPY ... FROM`) that names a table of W, schema-qualified or not; every module of the API closure with a write statement whose target is not a literal table name (included, never dropped); and, by path, `src/postgres/pine-bridge-receiver.js`, `src/postgres/paper-ledger-fence.js`, `src/postgres/ledger.js`, `src/paper-accounting.js` and `src/postgres/db.js`.
3. **`src/postgres/server.js`** is not an entry, because its closure is the whole API. It must be byte-equal between `ba1a433` and P; its own route handlers are then unchanged, and its imports of changed modules are covered by N4.
4. **Closure:** the union of the transitive closures of the entry modules at both revisions, under the scan rules of N1.

Derive-b of the A0 record (Opus 5.5 / high, independent, from Git) is the independent re-derivation of the N1 counts and list hashes that A1 acceptance needs, because A0 is the legal basis of the API-only design (ruling R10 item 3(c)).

**Contents of the A0 record:**
1. The pin, `ba1a433`, and the method version of the closure scan (version 2).
2. N1: the worker closure file count and the SHA-256 of the sorted path list at both revisions; the result of `git diff --quiet ba1a433 <pin> -- <closure files>`; the same for the Paper write-path closure, with the table set W, the entry-module list and its SHA-256, and the `src/postgres/server.js` unchanged-bytes result; the count of non-literal dynamic imports (must be 0).
3. N2: the path list checked and an empty difference over it.
4. N3: the field-equality result and the dependency archive digest against the P1 attempt a02 digest.
5. N4: each changed runtime file with its class (`public/` or QR-2 read path) and, for each QR-2 file, its importer set. The request ranges and compute that newly run under the pin (case 4: the D ranges of revision 8 section 2.2 and, by analogy, the reconciliation compute of section 2.5) and Root's estimate of compute memory and CPU on the shared host, with the bound it is checked against (the API memory limit headroom, or the host available-memory floor when the API has no memory limit).
6. The re-map case or cases taken (section 2.4), which for a neutral pin is case 4, 5, 6 or 7 (cases 4, 5, 6 and 7 together for P2, case 4 by analogy), or case 3 with a reviewed archive.
7. Exact-revision CI: the check set, each conclusion and the revision read; the acceptance record of each runtime slice.
8. The local rehearsal (ruling D34 form, not a staging check), from P2 Git blobs, with the flag 1 and a session: (a) the revision 8 items still show (C7 "not checked" in English and in Thai; the generic 422 hint without the `timestamp_rows` and `union_minutes` lines, ruling L1 Q6); (b) with a fixture sized from the BR result, the accounts the BR result names as recordable reconcile with financial totals, and an account with a fill of a named class shows `RECONCILIATION_FAILED` with reason `EPOCH_UNKNOWN` and the note: heading "Why reconciliation failed" and Thai "สาเหตุที่กระทบยอดไม่ผ่าน", the `EPOCH_UNKNOWN` sentence and the line "Reason code EPOCH_UNKNOWN", in English and in Thai; (c) the measurement of section 2.5 (worker peak heap and resident memory, All Time compute time against 10000 ms); (d) the served page carries the `qr2o10` tokens. The record names every item that could not be shown locally.
9. The per-Bot expectation from the tracked BR result, per Bot by `scopes[].slot`, from `scopes[].preE0`, `scopes[].preE0LastAt`, `scopes[].hasP2Signal`, `edges[].at` and the scope x bucket `cells[]`, with the expected parity counts of section 6.9 item 3c (section 2.5).

Any N item that fails or is unknown stops this design at A0 (section 2.4).

**Status for P2.** Not done (revision 1). Root writes the A0' record after it sets the pin. Derive-b of the record (Opus 5.5 / high, independent, from Git) re-derives N1 before A1 acceptance (ruling R10 item 3(c)). Root pins the record's sha256 in the A1, AR, A2 (with A2M) and RA manifests of this packet. The revision 8 A0 record (sha256 `89c5c7a1`) is the record of `33e5a8c` and is not this packet's record.

## 4A. Prelude: RA(r8) before the REPIN-2 chain (Shape R)

**Why.** Staging today (state S-r8) runs pin `33e5a8c` under the revision 8 re-pin layer. Revision 8 section 2.4 says that a second re-pin needs its own packet with the post-A2 list as its expected list. The owner chose Shape R: run the sealed and accepted RA of revision 8 first, so that staging returns to `ba1a433` with the post-P5a configuration (state S-0), and then run this packet's chain as a first re-pin again (state S-P2 after A2). This keeps every REPIN-2 tool a constants-only rebuild: the P5a flag layer stays the anchor of the layer-name function and of the two-form pattern. It also needs no rebuilt RA of revision 8 (Root ruling RP1, Q-B). The trading worker runs `ba1a433` in every state, and production is untouched in every step.

**Job.** RA(r8) attempt a01 of the revision 8 family `S6-STAGING-API-REPIN-1-RA`, under revision 8 section 8, with its own GO-RA(r8) (section 14), reversing revision 8 A2 job a01. The tool is the sealed RA of revision 8, unchanged in every byte; its contract is the installed revision 8 file. This packet changes nothing of RA(r8): its gate, layer-state table, failure cases, outcomes, bounds and the five fixed sentences S0 to S4 are those of revision 8 sections 8, 9 and 11.

**Sealed-tool identity (Root ruling RP1, Q-H).** RA(r8) is the A2RA package that ran A2 a01 and A2M a01 of revision 8: admission `toolSourceSha` `9f691f0645e7fa4b...`, installed snapshot r9, SHA256SUMS `105066c9`, `PACKET_SHA` the revision 8 LF blob `ea6b463f`. Before admission Root re-derives `toolSourceSha` from that snapshot and admits RA(r8) only when it matches `9f691f06...`. Revision 8 section 6.1 names "snapshot r8"; that name predates the r9 re-seal and does not apply here. Root also requires the SHA256SUMS file of A2RA snapshot r9 to hash to `105066c9` with every line OK, and the core and runner in the RA(r8) execution folder to be byte-equal to the r9 `ra-core.mjs` and `ra-runner.mjs` (audit d1 P3-4).

**Order rule.** Root requests GO-RA(r8) only after all of these: the BR result accepted; the values commit integrated with exact-revision CI 9 of 9; P2 set; the A0' record written, with derive-b done and accepted (second opinion d1 P2-3); this packet installed and audited; the REPIN-2 lane, A1 and A2RA rebuilt, reviewed and accepted (A1 as a copy by value of the accepted family build of Root ruling RP4; section 6.1 item 3); the retirement inventory and the U-4 carry written; dry admissions passed (change map steps 1 to 8; before RA(r8) the A1 and A2 dry admissions are form-only, with a fake reference, because the `AR_RECORD` facts exist only after AR a01; audit d1 P3-5); the scan rule of section 10 holds (no stray file carries the revision 8 RA family token; audit d1 P2-7). Reasons: rollback readiness before any effect (revision 8 section 6.1 item 2, carried forward), and a 503 window (below) that holds no build or review.

**RA(r8) preparation (Root, local).**
1. The RA(r8) manifest from the reviewed revision 8 A2 a01 receipt, by revision 8 section 6.13 (Root ruling RP1, Q-D): `ra.records` empty (attempt a01), the production baseline from CHECK r6 a02 (Root ruling RP5, Q3: Root's read found that the A2 a01 retained output's production block holds only an equality and a digest, no per-unit rows, so the revision 8 fallback applies; the section 6.13 baseline row stays), and `resetFailedGranted` false unless the GO-RA(r8) carries the reset-failed clause, which Root adds only if the dry admission shows a failed unit that needs it (Root ruling RP2, E6).
2. A pin-drift check of every file the sealed tool pins: the installed revision 8 file (`ea6b463f`), the main, follow-up and collector packets, and the pinned A0, retirement and U-4 records of revision 8. Any drift kills RA(r8) at run time (`PIN_MISMATCH` or a pin check). No pinned file is edited (residual, section 12).
3. A dry admission with the sealed build and the real binding right before the GO request, run from the real RA(r8) execution folder under `.qa-local` with no `qaRoot` override (second opinion d2 P3-7), and the payload-size run with the real manifest. The dry admission also proves the scan: no unlisted `manifest*.json` or `*GO_CONSUMED*.json` file under `.qa-local` outside the skipped directories carries the revision 8 RA family token (section 10). Root removes every leftover dry-admission manifest outside its own execution folder, or lists it as the scan requires (second opinion d1 P3-1). Root also recounts the entries under `.qa-local` and checks the design and review folders of section 10 before the admission (Root ruling RP5, Q4; audit d2 P3-5).
4. A fresh usage reading under main ruling D8 that covers RA(r8), AR a01, A1, A2 with its delayed check and A2M, so that they can run back to back.
5. **Optional readiness read** (second opinion d1 P3-6). Root may weigh one read-only reading of production identity and unit states before GO-RA(r8), under the unused revision 8 AR family with its own GO-AR of revision 8. A production change since 29 October 2026 makes RA(r8) stop before any effect, and that stop can lead to the Q-B case of the stop states below.

**Root-only reads (execution folders)** (second opinion d1 P2-5; Root ruling RP2, E9). Only Root reads these values, from the execution folders; children audit field names only and never list or open an execution folder: the A1 a01 private list digest; the A2 a01 gate inventory line; the A2 a01 receipt fields that the RA(r8) manifest binds (Root confirmed the A2 job ID against the A2 a01 root manifest, and the retained output's production block holds no per-unit rows; Root ruling RP5, Q1 and Q3); the AR a01 values; the restart counts of the receipt chain; the open contingency job IDs; the production unit rows.

**Success state S-0 and the checks that prove it.** Root accepts RA(r8), terminal for the revision 8 RA family, only when all of these hold (revision 8 sections 8 and 9):
1. End code `RA_API_READY`; or `RA_STOP_MARKET_AFTER_READY` or `RA_STOP_AT_CHECK` after Root's review first.
2. End row LR-n from start row LL-n (the terminal rule allows only the end rows L0-n, LU-n, LR-n and LQ-n).
3. Loaded configuration: the drop-in list equal to the post-P5a list (list digest equal to the accepted revision 8 A1 a01 record, which equals the c1 `pairDropInDigests` value for the staging API); every drop-in file digest equal; the admission-off layer last; the QR-2 flag effective 1 from the P5a flag layer only; the five research flags 0.
4. Working directory the `ba1a433` tree and the start command the P4 argv; the lane walk of the `ba1a433` tree exact.
5. Immediate checks passed: served URLs byte-equal to the `ba1a433` served list (tokens `qr2o7`); protected routes refuse without a session; health 200 in Paper mode; no Live signal; the trading worker the same process with a fresh heartbeat; the research worker stopped; the market units as revision 8 allows; production equal to the baseline.
6. Disk: exactly one archive of the revision 8 re-pin layer (RA archive form) with the layer digest; no live or pending revision 8 re-pin file; every other file of the drop-in directory equal in count and digest to the A2 a01 gate inventory line.
7. Root's review of the receipt and a tracked result record; the revision 8 RA family consumed and terminal.

**After acceptance.**
- R5a, R4, R4H, RM and RC are valid again with their own rules. RX needs a fresh job ID (revision 8 section 9); Root writes a new RX tripwire marker for this packet's layer before the GO-A2 request (section 6.1 item 12; second opinion d1 P3-2).
- The revision 8 families A1, A2, A2M, AR and RA are retired by marker lines (section 10).
- AR a01 of this packet is required before GO-A1. RA(r8) restarts the staging API, so the c1 process facts of the staging API no longer describe it, and the A1 reference kind is `AR_RECORD` (change map G4; RP1, Q-C).
- Under Shape R the c1 configuration and drop-in references of the staging API are valid again (change map G5). Root confirms them at AR a01 and A1 acceptance; a difference is a drift on which Root rules.
- The archive of the revision 8 layer stays on disk. It never matches this packet's two-form pattern (its pin prefix is `33e5a8c3`, not the prefix of P2), so this packet's A2 gate records it among the other files of its inventory line, and this packet's RA compares it there (change map G1 and G2; sections 5.2 and 7).

**Stop states.**
- **Stop before any effect** (a gate refusal, `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE` at the gate, `PRE_TERMINATED`, or a no-command end): staging stays S-r8 (`33e5a8c` with the revision 8 layer live), and no 503 window opens. A next RA(r8) attempt follows the revision 8 rollback-family rule under a new GO-RA(r8), after Root's review.
- **Any end without the API proved ready on `ba1a433`** (`RA_UNWOUND`, `RA_UNWIND_RELOAD_FAILED`, `RA_UNWIND_START_FAILED`, `RA_API_NOT_READY`, `RA_API_STOPPED`, `RA_BUDGET_STOP`, `RA_STOP_NOT_PROVED`, `RA_STOP_MARKET_API_NOT_READY` or a termination): the revision 8 outcome tables and rollback-family rule apply. The staging API may be stopped, or running on either tree. Root reports, and the owner decides.
- **`RA_UNWIND_FAILED`, or `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE` after an effect:** excluded as in revision 8. Where revision 8 names a packet revision, that revision is a new file, because the revision 8 bytes stay frozen while RA(r8) may still run. The same holds for every RA(r8) end for which revision 8 names a packet revision, or a Root ruling that changes the RA contract: a production change that needs a new source of unit rows, a lost receipt with an unreadable progress record, a stuck transitioning state and a repeated case 4 (section 12, rollback-family rule). Because the sealed RA(r8) pins the revision 8 bytes, any such revision means a rebuilt RA for the revision 8 layer, and Q-B reopens: Root plans a constants-only RA build for the revision 8 layer against the new revision file. This packet's RA cannot act on the revision 8 layer (it treats that layer as another file; change map G1), and RX is barred while that layer is live, so until then the staging API may stay stopped.
- **No job of this packet** (AR, A1, A2, A2M or RA) starts until an accepted RA(r8) receipt exists. Shape R does not return from S-0 to S-r8.

**The 503 window, for the owner.** From the RA(r8) API start until this packet's A2 API start, staging serves `ba1a433`. During that window the Main Bot Month report answers 503 `QR2_REPORT_FAILED` again, as before revision 8 A2 a01, and the bar-coverage "not checked" result and the 422 hint are gone. The owner accepted this window (Shape R) and confirms item F of section 13, with the cases below, already at GO-RA(r8) (Root ruling RP6; second opinion d2 P2-2). **Window end** (second opinion d2 P3-1): the Main Bot Month serves again at this packet's A2 API start; the window closes only at Root's acceptance of A2. A market stop or a failed owner step keeps it open for the owner's decision. **Normal path** (second opinion d2 P2-2; Root ruling RP8, G1), counted from the RA(r8) API start: about 1 to 2 hours on the normal path when the owner answers each GO promptly; longer if any GO waits, and the stop cases below can make it days. It depends on the owner's GO-AR, GO-A1 and GO-A2 (and GO-AR a02 when more than 6 hours pass between AR a01 and GO-A2) and on Root's receipt reviews with the independent linkage reviewer; each wait for a GO, each receipt review and an AR a02 under the 6-hour rule add to it, and a standing authority for GO-AR and GO-A1 shortens it. Root keeps it short: all builds and the packet review are done before GO-RA(r8), and RA(r8), AR a01, A1 and A2 with A2M run back to back on one usage reading. Root's local steps inside the window are the RA(r8) receipt review with the independent linkage check (section 17), the revision 8 marker lines (section 10), the AR a01 acceptance, the real A1 dry admission, the A1 acceptance and the A2 receipt review; their times are not measured, and Root records them. Root may defer the tracked result records until after A2, but never the receipt reviews (second opinion d1 P2-3). The window can last days (Root ruling RP3; second opinion d1 P1-1). Cases that make it longer, each with its route:
- a job of this packet stops before its effect: the window lasts until a next attempt under a contingency job ID with its own GO;
- this packet's A2 stops after an effect and unwinds to S-0 (`A2_UNWOUND` or another unwind code): a next A2 needs a packet revision or Root ruling that defines its job ID, a new owner-typed GO-A2 and possibly a GO-AR (section 9);
- A2 passes, but the owner's browser step or Root's parity rows find a boundary suspect (section 6.9): GO-RA of this packet returns staging to S-0, and the fix needs a new BR or values commit, a new pin, a packet revision and rebuilds;
- A1 fails after an effect (a partial stage, or a tree that is not exact): there is no contingency job ID, and the next step is a Root ruling or a new pin;
- RA(r8) ends `RA_STOP_MARKET_AFTER_READY` or with a market change: Root cannot accept AR a01 while the stream is not running, and a market start needs its own packet or Root ruling before A2 (section 12, market start after RM or RC);
- RA(r8) ends without the API proved ready (`RA_API_STOPPED` and the other codes of the stop states above): the whole staging UI is down, not only the Main Bot Month report;
- RA(r8) ends in a state for which revision 8 names a packet revision: Q-B reopens (stop states above), and the staging API may stay stopped meanwhile;
- a usage band change in the middle of the chain stalls it inside S-0;
- this packet's A2 stops after an effect and leaves the API stopped (its named cases, `A2_UNWIND_FAILED`, or the API not ready after a market change): the whole staging UI is down, not only the Main Bot Month report; the route is GO-RA of this packet, or a Root ruling when the files form no recognised row (section 12, rollback-family rule) (second opinion d2 P2-1);
- A1 passes, but its record is not acceptable (`layerNameFits` false, the admission-off or P5a layer not found, or the list digest not equal to the c1 `pairDropInDigests` value): A2 is not requested until a new Root ruling and packet revision (sections 5.1 and 5.2);
- AR a01 shows a difference of the c1 configuration or drop-in references: a drift on which Root rules before A1 (After acceptance above);
- the owner step fails on timing (a request of 10000 ms or more, or a compute timeout) or on a failure that is not a boundary suspect (section 6.9 item 2): Root reviews, and the owner decides to keep P2 or to request GO-RA; GO-RA returns staging to S-0 and opens the window again, and no quick fix exists when the compute is too slow.

**Window reports** (second opinion d2 P3-2). Root records the window start (the RA(r8) API start time) in its RA(r8) report. Every Root report while the window is open gives the elapsed time. Root reports to the owner when the window passes 6 hours and after any stop after an effect. A Root stop report at a usage band names the window as open, with its start time. Root may set a one-off scheduled reminder at 6 hours after the start. Root's GO-RA(r8) request carries this list and the normal-path estimate (section 14).

**Owner cost of Shape R.** Two host-wide manager reloads shared with the production units (RA(r8) and this packet's A2), plus one if this packet's RA is needed, each with the named risk of item E of section 13; one more effect GO.

**Bounds** (revision 8 section 11, by value). RA(r8): about 3 to 5 minutes, plus the timer-window wait when the timer is active (at most 420 seconds) and the settle wait when needed (at most 30 seconds); host bound 1200 seconds; API gap expected 4 to 13 seconds, bound 380 seconds; lost-receipt wait 1335 seconds.

## 5. A1: inert stage of the API tree

### 5.1 Gate and effects

Copied by value from the accepted P1 tool (r2 snapshot), with named adaptations: the targets are reduced to the API tree; the required ancestor is `ba1a433`; the dependency archive must be byte-equal to the P1 attempt a02 archive, and a difference stops the build.

- **Build, local only.** Deterministic package from Git blobs of the pin (P1 form: built twice in memory and byte-compared, exclusive-create outputs). Served list, cache tokens and auth-route set rebuilt from the pin.
- **Gate, read only.** Host health floors as in P1 (memory, load, free disk for one more API tree, no heavy job, no live payload, the expected Node major); no pending systemd job and no unit needing a reload; production identity against the baseline; the staging API, trading worker, research worker, market stream, fallback collector service and fallback collector timer as states and main processes only, against the reference that the A1 manifest names: the Root-accepted AR a01 record of this packet, read after the accepted RA(r8) receipt (section 4A), or a later Root-accepted AR record (section 6.12) (reference kind `AR_RECORD`; the c6 record and its c1 export facts no longer describe the staging API process after RA(r8); change map G4 and K9). Root copies the AR values by value into the private A1 manifest; Root's manifest prep check asserts that the reference kind is `AR_RECORD`; the tool accepts both reference kinds, and a `C6_C1_FACTS` manifest would stop `PRE_DRIFT_API` before any effect and spend the attempt ID (second opinion d1 P2-2). The build asserts the form against a fake built from the recorded form. The staging API, the trading worker and the stream must be the same main process with the same restart count as the reference; the research worker stopped; the timer active; the fallback collector service inactive with result success, or in a recorded timer-started run (the A2 rule; ruling R3, S-P3-2). A unit in automatic restart or another transitioning state refuses, with no settle wait. A fallback collector service in a timer-started run is not transitioning for this rule; it is recorded (ruling R5 item 7; A-P3-4(a)). For the staging API, the trading worker or the stream that is a process-identity drift, and the path of section 6.3 applies. For the fallback collector service, the timer or the research worker it is not a drift: it is a pre-effect stop, and the next A1 attempt gets a contingency A1 job ID with no AR (ruling R4 item 7; A-P3-6(b)).
- **Effects.** One fresh incoming directory (mode 0700) and one copy of the package files; on the host: incoming exact, archive checked against the manifest, extraction into a partial directory with exclusive create per file, tree exact (files, bad, extra, missing), `node --check` on the API entry set (Root derives the count from the pin), rename into the existing stage directory under a name derived from the pin that collides with no existing name, exact again.
- **No activation.** No drop-in file, daemon reload, start, stop, restart or reset-failed; no environment read other than the drop-in digests of section 5.2; no database connection; no delete. Old trees stay.
- **Record.** At the end reading the staging API, the trading worker and the stream equal the gate (state, main process and restart count). A collector service run started by the timer, and a change of the timer's last trigger or next elapse, are recorded, never a difference. A research worker no longer stopped, a timer no longer active or a failed fallback collector service at the end is a Root review item: Root reports it, and it does not lead to AR (ruling R5 item 7; A-P3-4(b)). A difference of the staging API, the trading worker or the stream at the end reading comes after the stage, so A1 has had effects and gets no contingency job ID. Root reports it, and AR may follow it (section 6.12). Root may then accept the staged tree, when it is exact, with the Root-accepted AR record as the A2 reference in place of the A1 reference export, with no new A1 (ruling R4 item 7; A-P3-6(a)).
- **Acceptance of the A1 record** (ruling R20 item 3, extending ruling R8 N1). Root accepts an A1 record for A2 and RA use only when all of these hold: `layerNameFits`, `admissionOffFound` and `p5aLayerFound` are all true; the admission-off digest is present; the P5a layer digest appears exactly once among the drop-in digests; the live count equals the layer count; the drop-in list digest equals the collector c1 export value (`pairDropInDigests` for the staging API); the staged tree is exact, and the end reading equals the gate. A record that misses any of these is a Root review item, and A2 is not requested. This packet has no exception: the A1 record of this packet needs `layerNameFits` true, which is expected (section 5.2). This is Root's acceptance check of the record; it is not tool behaviour, and the tool tests of section 15 do not cover it (ruling R22 item 9).
- **Reference export** (ruling R3 item 1; A-P2-2, S-P2-5). A1 exports, as read at the end and equal to the gate, the main-process digest and the restart count of the staging API, the trading worker and the market stream, in the form of the collector start c1 export: SHA-256 over the canonical JSON of the main process, its start ticks and the invocation, copied by value from the accepted collector start build. The accepted A1 record is the process reference of A2 (section 6.2), unless Root accepted the staged tree with an AR record (Record above) or a later AR record replaces it. The values stay in the private receipt; the tracked result record names only their presence.
- **Record directory.** One record directory with a progress record and a result file in the dedicated protected backup directory; the incoming directory, the staged tree and the shell's temporary here-document file that carries the tool are the only other writes.
- **Bounds.** Host bound 300 seconds, carried by value from the accepted P1 revision 2 limits (section 11.1).
- **Rollback.** None needed. The tree stays inert. Its removal, if wanted, needs its own packet.

### 5.2 The layer-name boolean (ruling R1 item 6)

A1 reads the staging API drop-in names and the SHA-256 of each drop-in file inside the host-side process, never returning a name or content. The P4, P5a and admission-off layers, and possibly earlier release layers, carry environment assignments, so hashing their bytes is an environment-source read under ruling D4; only digests, counts and the one boolean leave that process; never a name or content (ruling R3, S-P3-3). It identifies the admission-off layer by its recorded name (the layer must be live and sort last among the live layers, otherwise it is not found) and the P5a flag layer by its recorded name and its recorded digest (exactly the live file with that name must have that digest, otherwise it is not found, which guards against a renamed or edited layer). Both names come in through the private A1 binding and never leave the host-side process (ruling R16 item 2). It applies the single reviewed name-derivation function that A2 also uses (copied by value into both tools), with a fixed candidate order: the 100 candidates of revision 7, unchanged and in the same order, then one last candidate (revision 8, ruling R30 item 1; below). Copying that function by value is not enough: the composite rule below is applied by A1 and, in the same form, by the A2 gate (section 6.2). It returns:
- one boolean, `layerNameFits`, true only under the composite rule (ruling R8 item 3, N6): the P5a flag layer and the admission-off layer are both found among the live layers; no live layer other than the admission-off layer sorts after the P5a flag layer; and the name-derivation function returns a valid name, one that matches the accepted layer-name form, sorts after every live layer including the P5a flag layer, sorts before the admission-off layer, and collides with no file (the derivation checks only the candidate name and that name followed by a dot; a pending or archived file of the other form is caught by the A2 gate with `PRE_LAYER_FORM_PRESENT`; section 7). In every other case it is false;
- counts: live layers, live layers that sort after the P5a flag layer other than the admission-off layer (expected 0), and archived files.

**Private digest export** (ruling R16 item 2(d)). A1 also exports, in its private receipt and retained output only and never a name: the content digest of the admission-off layer; the sorted list of the content digests of every live drop-in file; the drop-in list digest over the base names, in the P5a form, computed in the host-side process from the live paths; and the live count. The tracked result names only that these exports are present. After Root accepts the A1 record, the A2 and RA manifests take their drop-in digests from it (section 6.13); Root checks that the list digest equals the c1 `pairDropInDigests` value for the staging API, and a difference is a drift on which Root rules.

**Shape rule** (ruling R20 item 2). More than 64 live drop-ins is `PRE_DROPIN_SHAPE`, which keeps the returned frame under the output limit. Duplicate live content is not a shape refusal, because names identify the two layers; the acceptance conditions of section 5.1 (acceptance of the A1 record) cover the gap that this leaves.

**The last candidate** (revision 8; ruling R30 items 1 to 3). The name-derivation function keeps its 100 candidates, unchanged and in the same order, and tries one more candidate last: the recorded name of the P5a flag layer without its `.conf` suffix (its stem), then `0-s6-repin-`, then the first eight hex characters of the pin, then `.conf`. The A1 binding already carries the P5a flag layer name. The A2 and RA bindings carry the P5a layer digest, not its name (ruling R17), and the tools find the layer name on disk by that digest (section 7), so no new private value is needed; no real layer name is written in this packet.
- **Why it fits between the two layers.** The byte `0` (0x30) sorts after the `.` (0x2E) of `.conf`, so the candidate sorts right after the P5a flag layer. It shares the P5a name up to the first byte where the P5a name and the admission-off name differ, so it sorts before the admission-off layer whenever that byte lies inside the P5a stem. The recorded names differ at the byte after the two-digit prefix and its dash (revision 8 section 2.5), which lies inside the stem.
- **Same checks as every candidate.** It matches the accepted layer-name form (at most 80 characters after the two digits and the dash), sorts after every live layer other than the admission-off layer, sorts before the admission-off layer, and collides with no live, pending or archived name of either form of section 7. When it fails any check, the function returns null and the boolean stays false.
- **Copy by value.** The name-derivation function is the revision 8 function, unchanged. This packet rebuilds the A2RA generator with constants only (packet ID, job families, packet file and `PACKET_SHA`). The A1 tool is a copy by value of the accepted family build of Root ruling RP4 with the same constants edited only; that build carries one minimal code change outside the name-derivation function: the consumed-marker name and the admission-scan pattern carry the job family token (section 6.1 item 3; Root rulings RP3 and RP4). The A2RA generator copies the function from the new A1 snapshot with a `sourceSha256` check, in the re-seal order ADP, A1, A2RA (change map G7).

No name leaves the host-side process. A false boolean blocks the A2 request; the order rule changed by ruling R30 and revision 8, and it changes again only by a new Root ruling and packet revision. **Expected result after RA(r8)** (change map G1 to G3). The drop-in directory holds the post-P5a list and one archive of the revision 8 layer. The archive is not a live `.conf` file, so it is not counted among the live layers or after the P5a flag layer. Its name carries the pin prefix `33e5a8c3`, so it collides with no candidate for P2 and does not match the pattern of P2. The last candidate fits, as it did for revision 8, and the boolean is expected true. The archived count rises by one; it is recorded, not compared. The directory stays far below the entry caps (4096 entries; 64 inventory entries).

## 6. A2: API switch

### 6.1 Preconditions (Root, before the GO request)

1. RA(r8) accepted with the success-state checks 1 to 7 of section 4A.
2. A1 accepted with the boolean true and the record conditions of section 5.1 (acceptance of the A1 record; no exception); the A0' record pinned; the A2 manifest names the process reference (the accepted A1 record, or a later Root-accepted AR record after a drift, after a difference at the A1 end reading or from the AR of item 11).
3. The lane, A1 and A2RA (A2, A2M, RA and AR) rebuilt, re-sealed in the order ADP, A1, A2RA, with the suites, mutants and rehearsal rerun, reviewed and accepted against this packet before GO-RA(r8) is requested (rollback readiness; section 4A order rule). **ADP and A2RA:** a new snapshot each, regenerated by its own generator, constants only: the packet ID (for the lane, the allowlist `packet` value and `PACKET_ID` in `adp-under-test.mjs`; audit d1 P3-6), the job families, the packet file and `PACKET_SHA`. **A1** (Root rulings RP3 and RP4; audit d2 P1-1): Root copies the accepted family build `s6-staging-api-repin-build-a1-2026-10-30-repin2-family` (`toolSourceSha` `ed0794a3...`) by value into a new folder `s6-staging-api-repin-build-a1-2026-10-30-repin2` and edits the same constants only; nobody runs that folder's `gen/regen.sh`, which points at the 28 October build and would restore the old marker name and pattern. The family build carries the one minimal code change of Root ruling RP3 (which amends RP1 for A1 only), built and accepted by Root ruling RP4: the consumed-marker name and the admission-scan pattern carry the job family token `FAMILY_TOKEN`, derived from `JOB_FAMILY` with "-" replaced by "_", so that the revision 8 A1 markers, which keep their names and are never touched, are ignored by family. Reason: the revision 8 A1 a01 marker sits in its execution folder under the scanned root, and the unchanged scan would refuse this packet's A1 a01 with `ADMISSION_SCAN`. Tests: a marker of A1's own family still blocks; a marker of another family is ignored; attempt a01 has empty `priorAttempts`; a REPIN-1-named file whose body names the own `JOB_FAMILY` still blocks a01 (RP4 P3-1); a test for the unanchored regex case (Root ruling RP7; audit d3 P2-1). Checks after the A1 rebuild: `FAMILY_TOKEN` present in `a1-core.mjs` and `a1-runner.mjs`; the family suite 13 of 13 plus the RP4 P3-1 test (a REPIN-1-named file whose body names the own `JOB_FAMILY` still blocks a01) and the RP7 test (the unanchored regex case); the full A1 suite green; `toolSourceSha` re-derived; `mutants.mjs` rerun with the four RP3 mutants added and sealed; and an independent A1 tester run (suites and mutant runner) on a quiet machine. A2RA is unchanged in code, because its scan already matches the family token `"jobFamily":"<family>"` with the closing quote (Root ruling RP4). Dry admissions: before RA(r8) the A1 and A2 dry admissions are form-only (a fake reference), because the `AR_RECORD` facts exist only after AR a01; a real A1 dry admission runs after the AR a01 acceptance and before GO-A1 (audit d1 P3-5); a scan-only dry admission runs right before each GO; each dry admission runs from the real execution folder under `.qa-local`, with no `qaRoot` override (second opinion d2 P3-7), because only that location proves the scan; Root removes every leftover dry-admission manifest outside its own execution folder, or lists it as the scan requires (section 10; second opinion d1 P3-1). The payload-size run passes with the real manifests. Every manifest value is derived from P2 and the new records, never copied from a revision 8 manifest. The A1 manifest reference kind is `AR_RECORD`, and Root's manifest prep check asserts it (the tool accepts both kinds; section 5.1).
4. The retirement inventory of section 10 written with its marker lines, including the revision 8 families, and pinned.
5. **Blocker (ruling R2 O-4):** the U-4 carry of section 10 recorded (the revision 8 U-4 result stands while the accepted RM and RC snapshots are unchanged); without it GO-A2 is not requested.
6. The local rehearsal and measurement recorded in A0' (section 4 item 8; section 2.5), with the items not shown locally named in the GO-A2 request, and the per-Bot expectation recorded (section 4 item 9).
7. The GO-A2 request names the restore source, the P4 x4 a02 pre-switch backup of 2026-10-26T04:49:30Z, and its age (section 9).
8. A fresh usage reading under main ruling D8 covers RA(r8), AR a01, A1, A2, its delayed check, A2M and RA (section 4A).
9. No other S6 job of any family in flight.
10. The case 4 estimate in the A2 manifest is 384, or Root's new estimate when the A0' measurement exceeded it (section 2.5).
11. **Stream restart rate and AR a02** (ruling R4 item 10; Root ruling RP1, Q-C). The GO-A2 request states the observed restart rate of the market stream from the private receipt chain (now including the RA(r8), AR a01 and A1 records), as a count over a period. AR a02 runs only after a drift, a difference at the A1 end reading, or when more than 6 hours pass between AR a01 and the GO-A2 request; then GO-AR and GO-A2 may come in one owner message, each admitted on its own: AR runs first, and A2 is admitted only after Root accepts the AR record and writes the A2 manifest that names it.
12. **RX tripwire before GO-A2** (second opinion d1 P3-2). Root writes the RX tripwire marker of this packet's layer (section 9) before the GO-A2 request, so that the fresh RX job ID issued after RA(r8) is consumed before any effect, and dry-admits RX once with both tripwire markers present, to confirm that the full-restore path still admits.

### 6.2 Fresh gate (read only, before the first effect)

Every item stops before any effect when it fails. The drift rule of main packet section 7 applies against the accepted P5a a01 record (configuration and API environment digests and the P5a flag layer digest), the accepted A1 record (the drop-in list digest, the per-file drop-in digests and the admission-off layer digest; ruling R16 item 3; the list digest is checked equal to the c1 `pairDropInDigests` value for the staging API, and a difference is a drift on which Root rules), the c1 gate of the accepted collector start a01 record (timer settings) and its c1 export facts (the trading worker's configuration and drop-in list digests; ruling R4 item 8), the process reference that the A2 manifest names (the accepted A1 record, or the latest Root-accepted AR record; ruling R3 item 1) for unit classes, main processes and restart counts, and the A1 record for the staged tree. The main-process and restart values that the tool compares sit in the private receipt chain from P4M a03 to c6, A1 and any AR record, in ignored local records; this packet names that chain and never its values.

- **Settle wait** (ruling R3 item 6; S-P3-9). A unit found in automatic restart or in another transitioning state, or with a pending job of its own, gets one read-only settle wait of up to 30 seconds in total per gate, with polls, before the gate classifies it. A unit still transitioning at the bound refuses before any effect. A unit that settles into a new main process is a process-identity drift (section 6.3). A fallback collector service in a timer-started run is not transitioning for this rule; section 6.3 records it.
- **Host.** Health floors, Node major, systemd version, the login manager's setting for killing user processes at logout (stop if on or unknown), no pending job, no unit needing a reload.
- **Production identity** against the baseline.
- **Staging API.** Active as the same main process with the same restart count as the process reference; drop-in list digest (the post-P5a list) and every drop-in digest equal to the accepted A1 record (sources in section 6.13); working directory the `ba1a433` API tree; QR-2 flag effective 1 from the P5a flag layer only; five research flags 0 from the admission-off layer; no heap-size option from any source; the QR-2 flag set in no environment file (ruling D34 source rule); memory limit, if any, at least 300 MB above resident memory, or at least Root's re-map case 4 estimate when that is larger; with no memory limit, the host's available memory above the host available-memory floor by at least that estimate (section 2.4). The resident memory and the host's available memory are recorded as numbers (RC-Q36 "before" reading).
- **Journals** (kept from the P5a gate; ruling R2, P3-5). The staging API and trading-worker journals are read for line counts only under the P5a refusal-line and line-cap rules; an unreadable journal or a P5a refusal-line stop stops A2 before any effect.
- **Environment sources** (ruling D4(b), P5a form). From every source other than the unit's own environment property, only the QR-2 flag, the five research flags and the Node options are kept, plus the database connection key for the startup probe. The unit's own environment property is held and compared as a whole inside the host-side process; only its digest without the QR-2 key is returned. The trading worker's own environment property is held inside the host-side process the same way; only its digest is returned. The database connection key, the encryption keys and the PostgreSQL client keys never leave that process and are never returned, printed or stored. Not a binding read.
- **Unit property reads** (ruling R4 item 6; A-P3-5; ruling R5 item 6, A-P3-3). Every unit property read other than the host-side environment-source read of the staging API's and the trading worker's own environment property (digest only), in the gate, the hooks-and-links reads and the later checks, for the three market units, the staging API and the trading worker, uses the "no environment" list form of the collector start build: the environment properties are removed from the read list for every role, including the collector service, which the collector start gate reads with them. The build asserts that no class, process, configuration or hooks read list holds an environment property, and asserts that the environment property is read only in the host-side process, with only its digest leaving that process. The only environment reads are the environment-source reads above, inside the host-side process.
- **Trading worker.** Same main process and restart count as the process reference, active, fresh heartbeat, environment property digest equal to the reference: the `envDigests.TRADING` value of the accepted P4 x4 a02 receipt, as compared at the P5a a01 gate (ruling R3 item 3; S-P2-4). Digest form, copied by value from the accepted P5a tool: the map parsed from the unit's environment property, canonical JSON, SHA-256, with every key kept (the API digest form removes the QR-2 key; the trading worker's does not). The value is copied into the private A2 manifest; the build asserts the form against a fake built from the recorded form. Configuration and drop-in list digests (ruling R4 item 8; S-P3-4): equal to the c1 `pairConfigDigests` and `pairDropInDigests` values for the trading worker, in the c6 form, which c6 recorded as equal; Root copies them by value into the private A2 manifest, and the build asserts the form against a fake built from the recorded form.
- **Heartbeat rows and Live** (ruling R12 item 7; ruling R14 item 6). The gate checks mode and Live on all rows, unfiltered, and refuses on a Live signal above zero (the Live signal count must be a non-negative integer, otherwise it is unreadable); it refuses with `HEARTBEAT_UNREADABLE` when any row lacks a string role. The role filter serves only the own-row and freshness rules.
- **Research worker** loaded and stopped.
- **Market units:** section 6.3.
- **Staged tree** exact against the A1 record, checked with the lane walk (the P5a walk copied by value, with the P5a EXACT rule) before the first database read: a FIFO entry, a linked root, a file over the byte cap or a count over the cap refuses before any database read and any command (ruling R13 item 10). The walk takes the P5a real-path form: the expected real path is the resolved parent of the bound root plus the tree name, the tree root itself must not be a link, and an unreadable parent fails the check; a bound root that is not a canonical string (a trailing `/`, `//`, `/./` or `/../`) is refused when the bound is validated (ruling R19 item 4(g); ruling R21 item 5(g)).
- **Layer name** derived from the live list (section 7) with the revision 8 function (the 100 candidates, then the last candidate built from the P5a layer name, which the gate finds on disk by the layer's recorded digest; sections 5.2 and 7); no pending or archived file with that name. The gate reads the staging API drop-in directory and applies the two-form re-pin layer pattern of section 7 only to files with a suffix: a pending or archived file of either form refuses before any effect with `PRE_LAYER_FORM_PRESENT`. A live file is not classified by the pattern. A live file outside the A1-recorded drop-in list refuses with `PRE_DROPIN_RECORD`, and a live file that makes the name derivation fail ends `A2_NO_LAYER_NAME`; two live re-pin layers of one of each form refuse, among the layer checks, by these codes, not by `PRE_DROPIN_SHAPE`, while two live old-form layers inside the A1-recorded list that both sort before the P5a flag layer can pass the gate and only RA refuses them (rulings R33 item 2 and R34 item 2). A live leftover of the old form that sorts before the P5a flag layer, inside the A1-recorded list, passes the gate; a live leftover of the new form always sorts after the P5a flag layer and ends `A2_NO_LAYER_NAME` (the residual of section 12; ruling R31 item 3; ruling R34 item 2). The gate re-derives the composite boolean here on every run and does not use the boolean stored in the A1 record; a false result ends `A2_NO_LAYER_NAME` before any effect. The gate states the composite layer-fit rule of section 5.2 explicitly (ruling R8 item 3, N6; ruling R9 item 3): the P5a flag layer and the admission-off layer are both found, no live layer other than the admission-off layer sorts after the P5a flag layer, and the name-derivation function returns a name; a fake with another live layer after the P5a flag layer ends `A2_NO_LAYER_NAME` before any effect. If none fits, or the composite rule fails, stop (`A2_NO_LAYER_NAME`). The gate also records the count and the SHA-256 of every other file of the staging API drop-in directory (every file other than the live drop-in list and the pending and archive forms of the derived name; for example archives of earlier packets, such as the archive of the revision 8 layer after RA(r8); change map G1), inside the host-side process and never a name or content (an environment-source read under ruling D4, as in section 5.2), for the compared file set of RA (section 8; ruling R4 item 4; ruling R5 item 9, A-P3-6(c)). Before its first effect on the staging API, A2 writes that gate inventory (the count and the SHA-256 list, sorted non-decreasing, duplicates allowed, at most 64 entries, no names) as a gate line of its host progress record, and the A2 receipt carries the same (ruling R5 item 1; A-P2-1, S-P2-2). One shared function writes the inventory in A2 and reads it in RA. With more than 64 files A2 refuses before any effect with `PRE_INVENTORY_TOO_LARGE` (ruling R9 item 8).
- **Database**, read only through the migration connection selector (ruling D4(a)): schema version 14, fence INSTALLED, research queue idle. No counter baseline.
- **Startup probe** (P4 step 1 form): a reviewed read-only probe from the staged API tree calls only `verifySchema` and `requirePaperLedgerFence` and returns booleans and counts. Any refusal stops.
- **Retirement inventory** present and pinned.
- **Time budget.** The remaining host bound covers the worst case from this point, including the timer-window wait (section 11.1).

### 6.3 Market-unit gate, hooks and links, timer window

Named adaptation of the collector-start market-unit gate (ruling D47 P2-1 precedent), with the role list passed as an argument:
- **Stream.** Active, same main process and restart count as the process reference (section 6.2), configuration digest equal to the P4M m1 a03 manifest's market configuration digest of the stream, which the m1 a03 run found equal (`configEqual` true); the P4M binding holds no digest field (ruling R3 item 3; S-P3-1; ruling R21 item 6; section 6.13). The digest is in the P4M tool's configuration digest form, copied by value. Root copies the value into the private A2 manifest at build time; the tool reads no binding on the host. The build asserts the form against a fake built from the recorded form.
- **Timer.** Active with a next elapse; trigger count, accuracy, randomized delay and persistence equal to the c1 gate of the accepted collector start a01 record: one calendar trigger, accuracy 1000 ms, randomized delay 0, persistence off (calendar text not compared).
- **Collector service.** Inactive with result success, or in a timer-started run (recorded).
- No failed or transitioning market unit after the settle wait of section 6.2; no pending job of any unit; no unit needing a reload. A failed market unit refuses A2; the owner decides on GO-RC or GO-RM, and A2 then needs the market units started again and a new process reference (a Root-accepted AR record) (ruling R3, A-P3-5). A start of the market units after GO-RC or GO-RM, before A2, needs its own packet or Root ruling, because the start families are retired before GO-A2 (section 10) and RM and RC only stop; A2 waits for it (ruling R4 item 11; A-P3-8, S-P3-7(b)).

**Recorded cadence** (collector start a01, 28 October 2026): the timer became ready at 07:33:06.933Z; its first run was 113067 ms later (07:35:00Z) and its last trigger at c6 was 413067 ms after readiness (07:40:00Z). The calendar period is about 300 seconds; the collector run at c1 completed in under one second (summary line at 624 ms).

**Process identity drift** (ruling R2, P3-9; ruling R3 item 1, A-P2-2, S-P2-5). A normal stream restart (the stream exits on a venue tick or step change and restarts), or a restart of the staging API or the trading worker, since the process reference stops A1 or A2 at its gate, before any effect, and spends that GO. The reference of A1 is the Root-accepted AR a01 record (section 4A) or a later Root-accepted AR record; the reference of A2 is the accepted A1 record or the latest Root-accepted AR record; each manifest names which. Root's path, the only one: Root reports the drift and its cost to the owner, requests GO-AR (section 6.12), reviews and accepts the AR record, and then requests a new GO for the next attempt of the stopped job under a contingency job ID (the stop had no effect), with a manifest that names that AR record. The A1 and A2 GO lines already name "the latest Root-accepted AR record", so their wording does not change. Cost: one extra read-only GO (GO-AR) and the new GO of the next attempt. The retired collector c1 and c6 families are not used. A difference of the staging API, the trading worker or the stream found at A1's end reading, after the stage, takes the same path to GO-AR (collector runs and timer trigger changes are recorded, and the other A1 end-reading items are Root review items; section 5.1), after which Root may accept the staged tree with the AR record as the A2 reference, with no new A1 (section 5.1). A transitioning collector service, timer or research worker at the A1 gate is not a drift (section 5.1). Root may also request one AR without a drift right before the GO-A2 request, only when more than 6 hours pass between AR a01 and the GO-A2 request (section 6.1 item 11; Root ruling RP1, Q-C) (ruling R4 items 7 and 10).

**Two-way hooks-and-links rule.** No stop, start or reload step of the staging API reaches a market unit, and no market unit's start, stop or failure handling names the staging API. Properties read (ruling R2, P3-4):
- **Staging API side:** `Conflicts`, `ConflictedBy`, `PropagatesStopTo`, `PropagatesReloadTo`, `BoundBy`, `ConsistsOf`, `RequiredBy`, `WantedBy`, `OnFailure`, `OnSuccess` name no market unit.
- **Market side** (each of the stream, the collector service and the collector timer): `Wants`, `Requires`, `Requisite`, `BindsTo`, `PartOf`, `Upholds`, `Conflicts`, `OnFailure`, `OnSuccess`, `StopPropagatedFrom`, `ReloadPropagatedFrom` name no staging API.

Names stay in the host-side process; only booleans and counts are returned. Paths through targets and mounts are not read (named risk).

**Timer reload behaviour, as a record** (ruling R1 item 5, U-2). The gate records the timer's persistence, accuracy, randomized delay, last trigger and next elapse. After the reload and at the delayed check the tool records them again, and whether the next elapse changed and whether a collector run started. A changed next elapse is recorded, never a stop.

**Timer-window rule.** The switch window W is the worst case from the API stop command to the end of the post-reload market re-check: stop 30 seconds, read-only grace 70 seconds, pre-write check 5 seconds, pre-write wait 15 seconds, layer write 3 seconds, reload 15 seconds, loaded-configuration gate 10 seconds, market re-check 10 seconds, margin 10 seconds; W = 168 seconds (section 11.1). The author check asserts W plus the timer accuracy plus the randomized delay below the recorded period (168 + 1 + 0 = 169 < 300), and the build asserts the same with the tool's limits; otherwise the gate could never pass and GO-A2 would be spent.
1. At the end of the gate and again right before the stop command, the timer's next elapse must be later than now plus W plus the timer accuracy and randomized delay, the collector service must not be in a run, and the job list must be empty.
2. Otherwise the tool waits, read only, re-reading the timer and the collector service, until a run that fires has ended and the next elapse is outside the window. The wait is bounded at 420 seconds (ruling R2 O-6; section 11.1). At the bound it stops before any effect (`A2_TIMER_WINDOW`).
3. After the proved API stop and before the layer write, the tool re-checks (at most 5 seconds): no collector run in progress and no pending job. A run in progress gets a read-only wait of up to 15 seconds. If it has not ended, the tool takes the unwind with no layer written (section 6.6).
4. The unwind reload, when needed, is not gated by the timer: restoring the API comes first, and the collector state is recorded.

### 6.4 Effects, in order (P5a pattern)

1. Create the record directory (progress record and result file) in the dedicated protected backup directory, and write the gate inventory line of section 6.2 into the progress record before step 2 (ruling R5 item 1).
