2. Stop the staging API and prove the stop: 30-second bound, then a read-only wait of up to 70 seconds with no second stop command.
3. Pre-write timer and job re-check (section 6.3 item 3).
4. Write the re-pin layer as a pending file (exclusive create, mode 0600, fsync) and rename it in.
5. One manager reload.
6. Loaded-configuration gate (section 6.5) and production identity.
7. Market re-check after the reload: the stream the same main process with no restart and no control-group event, the timer active, the collector service not failed.
8. Start the staging API; readiness within 40 seconds.

**Restart count at the start** (ruling R13 item 2, which amends ruling R12 item 14). n0 is the restart count read right before the start command. n1 is the count in the snapshot that ends the start poll (unit running, no job). The start is not ready unless n1 is 0 or equal to n0, and the count read after readiness must equal n1; a count that is not an integer is not ready (ruling R19 item 4(e); ruling R22 item 6). The rule must be right under both behaviours of the service manager, a start that resets the count and a start that keeps it, because Root has no host fact on the reset rule (reading one is target contact, which needs a finalized packet and a GO) and the manager version is unknown; section 12 names the residual. It applies to every start of the staging API in A2 and RA: the main start, the market-branch start, the unwind and put-back starts and the case 5 restart. The readiness poll is a named adaptation of the P5a loop, which keeps polling inside the alive window; its coarse failure tokens are NOT_READY and TIMEOUT, and the receipt keeps the adapter's failed list as the detail (ruling R13 item 7).

**Progress lines** (ruling R6 item 7; S-P3-1). Before every rename (step 4, and every archive of the unwind) and every reload (step 5, and the unwind reload), the tool writes an fsynced progress line `<step> ATTEMPTED` that carries the expected source and target form and the expected digest, never a name, and after the step a line `<step> DONE`. A write of either line that fails is a failed progress write, and any failed A2 progress write before readiness is an unwind trigger (section 6.6; ruling R7 item 3, P3-2). The digest line of a pending file before its rename (section 6.6) is such a line, and the A2 receipt carries it. **Torn line** (ruling R9 item 10l): after a failed or partial write, the writer starts the next line on a fresh line and writes a `NOTE TORN_PREVIOUS` line; the progress reader tolerates exactly that pattern, counts the torn step as `ATTEMPTED` and lets the disk decide, and refuses on any other line it cannot parse. One torn last line with no line ending, at the end of the file, is read as an `ATTEMPTED` step of unknown kind and the disk decides; a torn line anywhere else still refuses, and if the row's facts are missing because of it, row recognition refuses (ruling R12 item 15).

**Market change after the reload** (confirmed by ruling R2 O-7). If step 7 finds a market unit changed (stream not the same process, timer not active, collector failed), the API code is not the cause, and a second reload would add risk. The tool therefore does not unwind and runs no extra reload: it gives the market units no command, starts the API on the new tree, runs the immediate checks and ends `A2_STOP_MARKET_AFTER_READY` without the delayed check. The branch runs under the consumable allowance of section 6.6 (ruling R10 item 2; ruling R9a): granted when the change is seen, one start, one stop (only after a failed start or readiness, proved) and nothing else. An unreadable stream at the reload re-check is a failure (`EFF_STREAM_UNREADABLE`), not a market change (ruling R12 item 15; ruling R22 item 4: `PRE_UNIT_SNAPSHOT` is the code of the RA gate before any effect only; ruling R25 item 5, audit-r9 P3-1: `STREAM_UNREADABLE` without a prefix is only the A2 immediate-check token). Root reports; the owner decides on GO-RA, GO-RC or GO-RM. GO-RC or GO-RM before GO-RA is admissible: RA classifies the market units and gives them no command (section 8).

**Named outcomes of the market branch and of the later checks** (ruling R3 item 6; S-P3-5).
- **API not ready in the market branch.** If, after a market change at the reload, the API start or readiness fails, the tool runs no unwind and no extra reload and gives the market units no command. It stops the API if it runs and proves the stop (30 seconds, then the read-only wait of up to 70 seconds), starts nothing, and ends `A2_STOP_MARKET_API_NOT_READY` with the API stopped (or failed) on the re-pin layer. This stop is the one stop of the market allowance (section 6.6). When the start leaves the API failed with an empty job queue, the tool sends no stop and the failed state counts as stopped (packet r6, ruling R6 item 3; a failed API with a queued start job is not stopped, ruling R25 item 7, G1); the receipt records the failed state (ruling R13 item 8). When the stop is not proved, the end is `A2_UNWIND_API_STOPPED`, also though no unwind ran; the receipt carries `marketStopProved` false and `stopProved` false (the branch sent a stop and no start followed it, so the two fields are equal) and `apiAtEnd` (ruling R13 item 6; ruling R25 item 2; section 6.6). When the branch sent no stop (the start left the API failed), `marketStopProved` is absent (no key is written), and `stopProved` follows the rule of section 6.6: that class read is a stop read, so the value is true when the run sent a stop earlier, the read shows the API failed with an empty job queue and no start was attempted after the read, and absent when the run sent no stop. Root reports; the owner decides on GO-RA, GO-RC or GO-RM. RA admits that state (section 8, row LL).
- **Market change at the immediate or the delayed check.** If the immediate checks (section 6.7) or the delayed check (section 6.8) find a market unit changed, the tool gives no command to any unit, runs no unwind and no reload, leaves the API running on the new tree and ends `A2_STOP_MARKET_AT_CHECK` (at the immediate checks without the delayed check). Root reports; the owner decides on GO-RA, GO-RC or GO-RM.
- **Owner steps and A2M after a market stop** (ruling R3, A-P3-3). After `A2_STOP_MARKET_AFTER_READY` or `A2_STOP_MARKET_AT_CHECK`, Root asks the owner first whether the browser steps and A2M run or the owner decides on GO-RA, GO-RC or GO-RM. If A2M runs, its process reference is the process of the last check that ran (the immediate-check process when the delayed check did not run); when the A2 receipt holds no such reference, Root records the answer as unknown (section 6.11).

### 6.5 Loaded-configuration gate

On the staging API after the reload:
- drop-in list equal to the post-P5a list plus the re-pin layer, every earlier drop-in digest unchanged;
- working directory the staged tree;
- a single start command whose last argument is the staged tree's `src/postgres/server.js`, with the other arguments equal to the P4 form;
- QR-2 flag effective 1 from the P5a flag layer only;
- five research flags 0;
- no heap-size option from any source;
- environment property digest without the QR-2 key equal to the P5a record;
- the QR-2 flag set in no environment file.

Also after the reload: production identity; the trading worker's main process unchanged.

### 6.6 In-run unwind (before readiness)

Any failure before readiness, other than a market change after the reload (section 6.4), starts the unwind: a stop that is not proved or fails, a busy port, the pre-write re-check, the layer write, the reload, the loaded-configuration or production-identity check after the reload, the API start or readiness, a step-budget or in-run-limit stop, or a failed progress write. A step-budget or in-run-limit stop before readiness and a failed progress write are failures and unwind. A termination is not unwound: it sends no command in any phase, as the paragraph after the named cases below states (ruling R12 item 5, which withdraws the split of ruling R10 item 3(b); ruling R7 item 3, P3-2). **No effect yet** (fix round 5, ruling R24 item 4, F2): when the effect flag is false at a trigger (a budget stop, a clock refusal, a termination or a throw), A2 ends with the PRE code of that trigger, sends no command and runs no unwind. The codes that can occur are `PRE_TIME_BUDGET` (a budget stop or a clock refusal) and `PRE_TERMINATED` (a termination); the audit of the build found these the only reachable triggers (ruling R25 item 5, D28). The effect flag flips before a host-changing command is sent, and a command that fails, times out or errors after it was sent counts as an effect, so such a trigger takes the unwind below. **End form** (ruling R25 item 5; audit-r9 P3-3): an A2 end with a PRE_ code and the flag false sent no command, but its receipt still has `effectPhase` EFFECT and a made record, and the runner evaluates it as `A2_STOP_EFFECT` with `hostEffect` EFFECT; this is stricter than the facts and is accepted. Such an end needs no RA. The next step is Root's review of the receipt, confirming zero control commands, and then a fresh A2 gate under a contingency job ID with its own GO; the owner's current authority covers attempt a01 only. A port is busy when it has a LISTEN row, or any row with a nonzero inode, on the API's local port in tcp or tcp6 (ruling R12 item 11). On most failures the tool returns the API to `ba1a433` by itself:
- The tool stops the API and proves the stop.
- It archives the re-pin layer by no-clobber rename if it was written. A pending file left before the rename is archived the same way, by no-clobber rename, with its digest recorded in the progress record before the rename; nothing is deleted (ruling R4 item 9; S-P3-5; ruling R5 item 5, S-P3-2). Row LU of section 8 matches that pre-rename line. The A2 receipt carries that pre-rename line, and each archive and the unwind reload get their `ATTEMPTED` and `DONE` lines (section 6.4; ruling R6 item 7; A-P3-6, S-P3-1). If the pre-rename line cannot be written, the pending file stays in place, the tool starts nothing and ends `A2_UNWIND_PROGRESS_FAILED` (row LP-n; ruling R6 item 3; S-P3-6).
- It runs one more manager reload if the layer was renamed into place or a reload was attempted. A2 therefore runs at most two reloads.
- When the layer was written or a reload was attempted, it checks that the post-P5a configuration is loaded (the `ba1a433` tree, the QR-2 flag effective 1 from the P5a flag layer, research flags 0) and that the production identity is unchanged, and only then starts the API on it.
- When no layer was written and no reload was attempted, it starts the API after the proved stop on the unchanged configuration.

In these named cases it leaves the API stopped and Root reports:
- a stop that is still not proved after the read-only wait, or a failed API: the tool starts nothing and sends no second stop command; an error in the proof wait other than a termination (a snapshot fault or a budget stop) counts as not proved, sends no further command, and the end is `A2_UNWIND_API_STOPPED` with the API class read (ruling R13 item 3);
- a failed unwind reload, a failed loaded-configuration check after it, or a failed unwind start or readiness: the tool starts nothing more (`A2_UNWIND_API_STOPPED`; ruling R6 item 9);
- an unwind that finds the API failed with an empty job queue sends no stop to it (packet r6, ruling R6 item 3; a failed API counts as stopped only with an empty job queue, ruling R25 item 7, G1). As RA case 2 does (ruling R13 item 5), the archive rename and its reload still run, so the disk and the loaded configuration agree; no start is sent, and the run ends `A2_UNWIND_API_STOPPED` with `apiFailed` and `apiAtEnd` FAILED, the end row being the row of an archived and reloaded layer (ruling R19 item 4(d); ruling R21 item 5(d); ruling R13 item 3). An unwind stop that leaves the API failed takes the same road: the archive, the reload and no start (ruling R24 item 3, reading 3). On this road the run sets `stopProved` true with `apiFailed` true, because the stop read shows the API failed with an empty job queue, which counts as stopped (ruling R24 item 4, F5; ruling R25 items 2 and 7, G1). A failed API with a queued start job is not stopped (fix round 6, G1): the class check does not count it as stopped, the stop poll continues within its bound, sends no further command, and the end is not proved (ruling R26 item 5). A class check treats a failed API with a queued job like a running API and sends it the one stop of that step; when that stop is not proved, the end is `A2_UNWIND_API_STOPPED` in A2 and `RA_STOP_NOT_PROVED` in RA, both with `stopProved` false (audit r10 against the fix round 6 core; ruling R27 item 3, P3-3). When no layer was written and no reload ran, the end row is L0-n: nothing is renamed, no reload runs and no start is sent, and the unwind sends no stop except one stop when its class read, after the proved first stop, finds the API not stopped (a process an outside actor started); the run ends `A2_UNWIND_API_STOPPED` with the failed API recorded in `apiAtEnd` FAILED and `stopProved` true; when the first stop left the API failed (kind STOP_FAILED, or a failed stop command whose snapshot read shows the API failed with no job) this end writes no `apiFailed` key, and when the unwind read or the unwind's one stop finds the API failed it writes `apiFailed` true (ruling R27 item 3, P3-4; ruling R28 item 2; ruling R29 item 2); the next step is Root review, and RA in row L0-n, with the reset-failed grant, can recover (ruling R22 item 5). No path sends a second stop command to one API process (ruling R29 item 2);
- a failed progress write of the unwind other than the pre-rename digest line: the tool starts nothing more and ends `A2_UNWIND_API_STOPPED`, with the end row read from the files on disk and the reload label (row LP-n included; ruling R7 item 3, P3-2);
- an archived layer that cannot be confirmed, or a production-identity difference after the unwind reload: `A2_UNWIND_FAILED`, nothing started (RD-32 form).

**Failed stop command** (ruling R13 item 4, amended by ruling R14 items 2 and 3). A command timeout ends as a stop not proved (`A2_UNWIND_API_STOPPED`). Any other failure of the first stop command gets one snapshot read, and A2 applies the same rule as RA case 5 with its codes: the end is a stop-not-proved end (`A2_UNWIND_API_STOPPED`; a snapshot that shows the API failed with no job counts as stopped and ends here too, ruling R14 item 3) unless the API is running with no job and the gate's main process. In that case nothing is started, the API stays running on `ba1a433` as found, its readiness is not re-proved, and A2 ends `A2_UNWOUND`, end row L0-n (ruling R14 item 2). If the adapter cannot tell a timeout from another failure, every failure takes the snapshot rule. The receipt records `stopCommandFailed` and `apiAtEnd`; for the `A2_UNWOUND` end it carries `stopCommandFailed` true, `startSent` false and `apiAtEnd`.

**Tree check before the unwind start, no false stop and the limit read** (ruling R19 item 4(a) to (c); ruling R21 item 5(a) to (c)). Right before the unwind start on the old tree (the `ba1a433` tree), A2 checks that tree with the lane walk (the P5a walk copied by value, with the P5a EXACT rule) against its bound digest and file count. A mismatch or an unreadable tree sends no start: the run ends `A2_UNWIND_API_STOPPED` with why TREE, `startRefused` `TREE` and `startRefusedAt` `UNWIND` (`TREE_UNKNOWN` does not arise in the A2 unwind; it occurs only in RA case 5), and the API stays stopped. The walk is counted in its own path sum (section 11.1, 4 seconds in the A2 unwind path 176). No end code claims that the API stopped without a proved stop. While readiness of that start is not proved (the readiness poll and any check before readiness of that start is proved; ruling R19a; ruling R23 item 3), any throw after a start command that is not followed by a proved stop ends `A2_UNWIND_API_STOPPED` with `stopProved` false. The scoped rule applies to every start command of the run (the unwind start, the main start and the market-branch start among them; the market-branch rules of section 6.4 otherwise stand; ruling R23 item 4). After readiness is proved, the existing codes stand: the A2 immediate-check and delayed-check codes (`A2_STOP_AT_CHECK` and `A2_STOP_MARKET_AT_CHECK`, section 6.7) and the READY rule (ruling R19a; ruling R22 item 2). After the in-run limit or, while unwinding, the unwind limit (section 11) is passed, the run makes one read-only class read of the API, capped to end before the host deadline minus the receipt-write reserve, and records `apiAtEnd`; when that read cannot run in time, `apiAtEnd` is `UNKNOWN` and `apiAtEndWhy` is `LIMIT`, and no command follows. The receipt-write reserve is the 10-second timing margin, and the read needs more than its 5-second call time before the cap; `apiAtEndWhy` is `LIMIT` when the cap forbids the read, or when the read fails at or after the cap, and `READ` when the read fails otherwise (ruling R24 item 3, reading 4; ruling R25 item 5, audit-r9 P3-4). **Receipt fields**: `startSent` is run-level, true once any start command was attempted and never set back to false; a start that a check refused records `startRefused` (`TREE` or `TREE_UNKNOWN`; `TREE_UNKNOWN` occurs only in RA case 5) and `startRefusedAt` (the start it refused: `PUT_BACK`, `CASE5` or `UNWIND`); the receipt also carries `stopProved` and `marketStopProved` as defined here (ruling R25 item 2, which replaces the wording of ruling R23 item 2), `apiAtEnd`, `apiAtEndWhy` and `stopCommandFailed` (ruling R24 item 4, F5; ruling R22 item 9). **`stopProved`** is absent (the key is not written; read it as null) when the run sent no API stop command. It is true when, after the last API stop command, a stop read showed the API stopped or failed (ruling R14 item 3) and no start command was attempted after the last such read. Otherwise it is false. A stop read is either the stop's own proof poll or a stop-context read, such as the class read on the A2 failed-API road. A failed API counts as stopped only with an empty job queue, as an inactive API does (fix round 6, G1): a failed API with a queued start job is not stopped: the stop poll continues within its bound, sends no further command, and the end is not proved (ruling R26 item 5). Under G1, `apiAtEnd` can read `RUNNING` after a failed unit with a queued job. `apiAtEnd` is never proof, so a run can end with `stopProved` false and `apiAtEnd` `INACTIVE` or `FAILED`. The field is not recorded on a pass end (`A2_PASSED`, or `RA_API_READY` including the case 5 restart), because readiness of the started tree is proved there. **`marketStopProved`** is written only in the A2 market branch, and only when the branch sent its stop: true when that stop was proved, false when it was not; it is absent otherwise, and RA never writes it. When the branch sent a stop, that stop is the run's last stop and no start follows it, so both fields carry the same value; when the branch sent none, `marketStopProved` is absent and `stopProved` follows its own rule. **RA asymmetry, kept**: in RA case 5, where the stop command failed and the snapshot read shows the API stopped with no job, the run ends `RA_STOP_NOT_PROVED` with `stopProved` false, while the A2 counterpart (`A2_UNWIND_API_STOPPED`) records true; RA fails closed and follows the list of ruling R14 item 3 (section 12). The packet auditor checks both definitions at every assignment site against the fix round 6 core; where the code differs and the code is the safer reading, the text follows the code and Root records why, and otherwise Root rules a narrow code fix. RA uses the same fields, except `marketStopProved` (section 8).

**Termination** (ruling R12 item 5; ruling R14 item 4). A termination sends no command in any phase and starts nothing. Before the first effect (phase GATE) A2 ends `PRE_TERMINATED`, with no effect; after the first effect A2 ends `EFF_TERMINATED`. The first effect is the first host-changing step of the mode: a control command, a rename or archive, a pending-file write or a manager reload (ruling R15 item 3). Both ends carry the end row from the files on disk and the API state as read, and A2 never claims the API stopped; the progress record gets a `NOTE` line. **Effect flag and `effectPhase`** (ruling R24 item 3, reading 1): the receipt field `effectPhase` follows the creation of the record directory, while the snapshot, time-budget and termination codes take their `PRE_` or `EFF_` name from the effect flag, which flips before the first host-changing command is sent. Step codes are fixed names and can occur with the flag false: `EFF_PRE_ARCHIVE`, `EFF_PORT_BUSY`, `EFF_LOADED_CONFIG`, `EFF_PROGRESS_WRITE` and the `EFF_RECORD_` codes (ruling R25 item 5, audit-r9 P3-2). A termination between the record directory and the first host-changing step therefore ends `PRE_TERMINATED` with `effectPhase` EFFECT and a made record, and the runner evaluates it as a stop with an effect. RA reads the same two facts the same way (section 8).

After readiness there is no automatic rollback; a rollback needs GO-RA.

**Phases and error routing** (ruling R9 item 2). A run has four phases: GATE (no effect yet; the phase ends at the first effect, which is the first host-changing step of the mode: a control command, a rename or archive, a pending-file write or a manager reload; ruling R15 item 3), EFFECT (the first effect up to proved readiness), READY (readiness proved) and MARKET (a market change seen after the reload). In READY the tool sends no control command at all: no stop, start, reset-failed, reload, rename, archive or pending write. In MARKET the guard uses a consumable allowance, granted when the market change is seen (ruling R9a; ruling R10 item 2): the market branch's one start, its readiness poll and, after a failed start or readiness, one proved stop (in RA also one reset-failed under the optional clause, ruling R9 item 10g); nothing else is admitted in MARKET, and nothing at all in READY. This is enforced twice: in the error routing below, and by a guard in the control-command layer that refuses without sending when the ready flag is set, or when the market flag is set and the allowance for that command is spent (an internal assert that ends the run by phase). A reload is refused in MARKET and in READY. Phase START runs from the sending of the start command until readiness is proved or the start ends failed (ruling R12 item 6; ruling R14 item 12; section 8, case 7).
- A2 in READY: any throw, step-budget stop or in-run-limit stop at an immediate or delayed check ends `A2_STOP_AT_CHECK`, or `A2_STOP_MARKET_AT_CHECK` when a market change was seen. A throw in the start or readiness poll after a market change ends `A2_STOP_MARKET_API_NOT_READY` with no unwind and no extra reload.
- A termination sends no command in any phase; it ends `PRE_TERMINATED` before the first effect (phase GATE) and `EFF_TERMINATED` after it (ruling R12 item 5; ruling R14 item 4); the tool writes a progress `NOTE` line.
- The steps that section 6.4 names for the market branch itself (the start, the readiness poll and, after a failed start or readiness, the stop) are the allowance above: the guard admits each of them once and refuses every other command, also a second start or a second stop (ruling R10 item 2; revision 7 author reading (a), section 18).
- Tests inject a throw, and separately a step-budget stop and an in-run-limit stop, at every await point after readiness and after a market change, in A2 and in RA (every reachable row), and assert zero control commands after readiness together with the end code and the end row (section 15).

The market branch is not an unwind; its outcomes are named in section 6.4.

**A2 end outcomes** (ruling R6 item 9; S-P3-9). The A2 receipt records the end code and the end row (a row of the section 8 layer-state table, read from the files on disk and the staging API's need-reload flag at the end). A hard kill leaves no end code; the A2 progress record then holds the facts.

| Outcome | When | End row | API at the end | Next step |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

### 6.7 Immediate checks

Every served URL fetched and byte-equal to the pin's manifest (including `index.html` with the pin's cache tokens); protected routes, including the three QR-2 paths, refuse without a session (ruling D17: this cannot show the flag; the loaded configuration shows it); health 200 in Paper mode; no Live signal in the heartbeat rows (a Live signal above zero refuses; ruling R12 item 7), the admin health `liveEnabled` false check being the owner's step (section 6.9; ruling R12 item 17); loaded code bytes equal to the staged tree; the staging API journal shows no fence, replication-role or schema refusal line (counts only); the trading worker the same process with a fresh heartbeat; research queue idle; the market units as in section 6.4 step 7 (a change ends `A2_STOP_MARKET_AT_CHECK`, section 6.4); production identity. A throw, a step-budget stop or an in-run-limit stop at these checks sends no command and ends `A2_STOP_AT_CHECK`, or `A2_STOP_MARKET_AT_CHECK` when a market change was seen (section 6.6; ruling R9 item 2). **Served-URL rule** (ruling R19 item 4(n); ruling R21 item 5(h)): the served list takes the P5a rule, a path with an optional `?v=<token>` cache token, with three tightenings (ruling R24 item 3, reading 6): at most 1024 bytes, no `..` segment and no leading `//`. A list in the real form (`/`, a page and assets with `?v=` tokens) passes in A2 and in RA; a query other than `v=<token>`, an absolute URL, a `..` segment, a missing leading `/`, a leading `//` and a path of more than 1024 bytes each refuse. A trailing slash and a `.` segment are inside the P5a character set and pass.

### 6.8 Delayed check (same job, about 300 seconds after readiness)

Same API main process, no restarts, no control-group events, health unchanged; the trading worker the same process with a fresh heartbeat; the stream the same process with no restart; the timer active with a next elapse (re-recorded); the collector service not failed (a market change ends `A2_STOP_MARKET_AT_CHECK`, section 6.4); production identity. It reads the staging API's resident memory and the host's available memory as numbers (RC-Q36 "after switch" reading). A throw, a step-budget stop or an in-run-limit stop at this check sends no command and ends `A2_STOP_AT_CHECK`, or `A2_STOP_MARKET_AT_CHECK` when a market change was seen (section 6.6; ruling R9 item 2).

### 6.9 RC-Q36 target re-measure and the owner's browser check (required)

Contract RC-Q36 requires the target server to be measured again, with its memory headroom read, before the route is turned on anywhere. Revision 8 closed it for `33e5a8c`. The boundary record changes the compute per request (section 2.5), so this A2 measures again (ruling R2 O-2 and O-3, carried):
1. **Memory numbers:** the gate reading (before the switch), the delayed-check reading (after the switch) and the A2M reading (after the owner's requests, section 6.11), each with the API resident memory and the host's available memory. A2M also returns whether the QR-2 worker thread exists, so the reading with a computation in the process lifetime is known.
2. **Route timings.** After Root reports the job result, the owner opens the per-Bot report for Week, Month and All Time for Bots 2 and 3 (the Bots with Paper fills; these requests compute the reconciliation) and for the Main Bot, in the owner's browser, through the owner's own port forwarding, which only the owner opens. Root reads only the request timings and status codes of those requests, view only, in the owner's Chrome session, and the read and compute times the panel shows. Root records the status code beside each timing, so that a 422 timing is not read as a compute timing. Root enters no credential and changes no setting. The timing record holds only the range label, the status code and the durations; it never holds a URL, port, query string, header, cookie or identifier (ruling R3 item 6; S-P3-6). Root compares the timings with the revision 8 A2 a01 timings (about 100 to 150 ms); the reconciliation compute may be slower, and the deadline is 10000 ms. Any single report request over 5000 ms is a finding that Root reviews before acceptance; a request of 10000 ms or more, or a compute timeout, fails the step (Root ruling RP2, E3). A 503 stops the step for Root's review. A failed step: Root reviews; the owner decides to keep P2 or to request GO-RA; GO-RA opens the 503 window again (second opinion d2 P2-1).
3. **Owner check** (Root ruling RP1, Q-F).
   - **a. Served page.** Hard reload first. The served page carries `i18n.js?v=qr2o10` and `paper-performance.js?v=qr2o10`; the CSS token stays `qr2o7`.
   - **b. Bots 2 and 3, Week, Month and All Time.** Reconciliation OK and financial totals not null. Or `RECONCILIATION_FAILED` with reason `EPOCH_UNKNOWN`, which passes only when all of these hold: (i) the BR result names the account and the fill class (a fill before E0, or a fill in the 12.248-second switch gap): the A0' per-Bot expectation derives the class per scope from `scopes[].preE0`, `scopes[].preE0LastAt`, `scopes[].hasP2Signal`, `scopes[].slot`, `edges[].at` and the scope x bucket `cells[]` (`PRE_E0`, `GAP`), and per the BR result the named account's earliest unrecordable fill is of that class and comes before any fill of another class (Root ruling RP2, E4; audit d1 P2-4); (ii) the reported first failure matches that class, which Root, not the owner, checks: Root reads the Bot's response itself, view only, in its own tab of the owner's logged-in session (the A2 precedent; Root ruling RP6; audit d2 P3-3); `reconciliation.failed_whole_account` is false; `reconciliation.failed_from` is not null; `reconciliation.first_failure.code` is `LEDGER_RULE_UNREPLAYABLE` with `reconciliation.first_failure.reason` `EPOCH_UNKNOWN`; and `failed_from`, by the record's tracked instants, lies in the class interval: class pre-E0 when `failed_from` is before E0 `from`; class gap when `failed_from` is at or after E0 `to` and before E1 `from` (the interval [E0 `to`, E1 `from`) of `epochOfFill`). Root compares it with `scopes[].preE0LastAt` and the BR Q2 `edges[].at` times and records the class only (Root ruling RP3; second opinion d1 P1-3; second opinion d2 P3-4). A null `failed_from` gives no class and is a boundary suspect; a whole-account failure is another failure code (below); (iii) the note shows in English and in Thai; and (iv) every other Bot reconciles OK. The first failure carries the check, code, event key and reason, with no time; the time is `failed_from`. The owner reports the check, the code and the reason only when Root cannot read the response, never the event key into any record, and Root records that relay as such. When Root cannot read `failed_from`, condition (ii) does not pass on the owner's relay alone; Root waits until it can read the field (Root ruling RP8, G2). Any other failure code (`SCALE_DIVERGENCE`, `LOT_BINDING_MISMATCH`, `LOT_EPOCH_UNKNOWN`, a `TARGET_` code, `SELL_EXCEEDS_POSITION`, or `LEDGER_RULE_UNREPLAYABLE` with another reason) is a boundary suspect: stop, Root's review, and the owner decides, likely on GO-RA. Under the accepted BR a01 result (section 2.5; `docs/evidence/S6_QR2_BOUNDARY_READ_1_A01_RESULT_2026-10-30.json`), Bot 2 (slot 2) is the one Bot predicted `EPOCH_UNKNOWN`, class pre-E0: all three of its fills come before E0 `from` (2026-10-01T16:16:00Z), the first at 2026-09-26T11:37:02.310Z and the last at `scopes[].preE0LastAt` 2026-09-27T00:34:02.322Z, it has no `GAP` cell, and the owner chose to keep the proven record rather than move E0 earlier on inference. Bot 3 (slot 3) is predicted to reconcile: all 24 of its fills lie in E0. Condition (b) can therefore pass only for Bot 2, at class pre-E0, with `failed_from` before E0 `from`; `RECONCILIATION_FAILED` on Bot 3, Bot 2 failing with another code or class, and Bot 2 reconciling are each a boundary suspect.
   - **c. Server details and parity rows** (Root ruling RP3; second opinion d1 P1-2; Root ruling RP6; audit d2 P2-4, P2-5, P3-1, P3-2; second opinion d2 P2-3, P2-4, P3-5, P3-6). `marker_counts.DISAGREES` is 0 for a reconciling Bot. Root also reads, view only, each Bot's All Time response, maps the Bot to its BR scope by `scopes[].slot` (section 2.5), and compares the response with the accepted BR result and the A0' expectation. Response paths: `cutoffs.fill_count`, `cutoffs.lot_count`, `cutoffs.last_fill.received_at`, `marker_counts.AGREES`, `marker_counts.DISAGREES`, `marker_counts.ABSENT`, `findings.items[].code`, `findings.capped` and `findings.total`.
     - **Reconciling Bot.** `cutoffs.fill_count` equals `scopes[].fills`, plus only fills after `db.observedAtMs`; any excess is excess AGREES only, so `marker_counts.ABSENT` and `marker_counts.DISAGREES` stay equal to their BR values, and `cutoffs.last_fill.received_at` is then after `db.observedAtMs`. `marker_counts.ABSENT` equals the sum of `cells[].n` with marker ABSENT over every bucket of the scope. `marker_counts.DISAGREES` equals the sum of the cells whose marker disagrees with the bucket epoch (0 under an accepted BR). AGREES + DISAGREES + ABSENT equals `cutoffs.fill_count`. `cutoffs.lot_count` equals `lots[].lots` of the scope plus only lots opened after `db.observedAtMs`: never below it, and an excess only with excess fills. BR Q1 `fills` and the reader's `fill_count`, and BR Q5 `lots` and the reader's `lot_count`, count the same tables in the same scope: the replay scope (`signals.user_id`, `signals.broker`) of one Bot account, with lots from `ledger_position_allocations` by user and broker, any status (Root ruling RP8, G3; audit d3 P3-5); Root re-confirmed this against the BR Q1 and Q5 query text at installation (Root ruling RP10, G3; section 18).
     - **Bot that passes condition (b).** The replay stops at the first `EPOCH_UNKNOWN` fill, and `marker_counts` counts only the replayed fills before `failed_from`. For each marker X, `marker_counts.X` is at least the sum of `cells[].n` over the cells of marker X whose `maxAt` is before `failed_from`; equality is expected only when no cell of marker X straddles `failed_from` (its `minAt` before it and its `maxAt` at or after it), and a straddling cell is recorded "not comparable" (Root ruling RP8; audit d3 P3-4). These marker rows never stop the step on their own. The `cutoffs.fill_count` and `cutoffs.lot_count` rows apply as for a reconciling Bot, without their marker parts.
     - **Clamp row.** No `findings.items[].code` `COST_CLAMPED` (check R14) unless the Q5 price classes predict it, and `findings.capped` false. When `findings.capped` is true, absence is not proved: that is a Root finding and a stop, unless Root's BR expectation covers `findings.total`.
     Any other mismatch is a boundary suspect: stop, Root's review, and the owner decides. Root records counts only. A record whose instants differ from the BR manifest instants (BR Q9) is admissible only when every moved edge lies in an interval that the BR Q2 `edges[].at` and the Q3 `cells[]` `minAt` and `maxAt` prove empty; otherwise a new BR is needed.
   - **d. The note, when a Bot fails.** Heading "Why reconciliation failed" in English and "สาเหตุที่กระทบยอดไม่ผ่าน" in Thai, the `EPOCH_UNKNOWN` sentence in both languages, and the line "Reason code EPOCH_UNKNOWN". Under the accepted BR a01 expectation the note shows on Bot 2 (item 3b). When no Bot fails, the note cannot show on staging: the owner records "note not shown, all reconciled", and the local rehearsal (section 4 item 8) is the English and Thai evidence.
   - **e. Main Bot.** Month answers 200 again (it answered 503 during the window of section 4A); Week shows bar coverage complete; Month and All Time return the report, with bar coverage "not checked" (class C7) where the range is over a coverage budget (the D behaviour of revision 8; no regression).
   - **f. Admin health.** It shows `liveEnabled` false. The owner reports the value at the browser step, and Root records it in the A2 acceptance record (ruling R14 item 14).
   Ruling L1 Q6 applies as in revision 8: the generic 422 hint stays for the remaining 422 cases, which the local rehearsal covers (section 4, item 8).
4. **A2M.** Root runs A2M after steps 2 and 3 (section 6.11).
5. **Record.** Root records the memory numbers, the Week, Month and All Time timings with their status codes (range label, status code and durations only), the owner's report, the per-Bot outcome against Root's BR-based expectation, the parity rows of item 3c (counts only), the class that Root found for each failing Bot (class only) and the A2M result in the A2 result record.

After a market stop code of section 6.4, Root asks the owner first whether steps 2 to 4 run (section 6.4).

Root accepts A2 only after steps 2, 3 and 4. If A2M stops or its receipt is lost, Root reports and the owner decides; a new A2M needs a new GO line.

### 6.10 Receipt export and labels

The A2 receipt keeps hashed post-release API facts (working directory and argument digests) for a later binding update; this packet updates no binding. It records its end code and end row (section 6.6) and carries the gate inventory line and any pre-rename pending digest line (ruling R5 item 1; ruling R6 items 7 and 9). Where they apply it also records `startSent` (run-level, never set back), `startRefused` (`TREE` in A2; `TREE` or, in RA case 5 only, `TREE_UNKNOWN`) with `startRefusedAt` (`PUT_BACK`, `CASE5` or `UNWIND`), `stopProved` and `marketStopProved` (absent, true or false, as section 6.6 defines them; `stopProved` is not recorded on a pass end, and `marketStopProved` only in the A2 market branch), `stopCommandFailed`, `apiFailed`, `apiAtEnd` with `apiAtEndWhy` (`LIMIT` or `READ`), and the API class at a budget stop (ruling R13 items 3, 4, 6 and 9; ruling R19 item 4(b) and (c); ruling R22 item 9; ruling R23 items 2 and 7; ruling R24 item 3, reading 4; section 6.6). The private A2 receipt body is the RA manifest record form (ruling R9 item 11c), and the worst-case A2 receipt body (64 inventory entries, every progress line and the maximum prior records) fits the receipt frame limit, which a test asserts (ruling R9 item 9). It also exports the API main-process digest in the c1 form (section 5.1) at the immediate checks and at the delayed check, as the A2M reference (section 6.11). Every later GO, receipt, result record and handoff names both revisions: the staging API at the pin, the trading worker at `ba1a433`.

### 6.11 A2M: read-only memory job after the owner's browser steps

Ruling R2 O-3. A separate job under GO-A2, with its own job ID (attempt a01), manifest, consumed marker, nonce, receipt and envelope, built and accepted with the A2 and RA tool.
- **When.** After the owner's browser steps and Root's timing record, before the GO-A2 `notAfter`. If the browser steps end after that time, A2M needs a new read-only GO line.
- **Gate, read only.** Host health is recorded and never refused. A2M refuses only on another S6 job in flight or the staging API not active (ruling R9 item 6). The in-flight scan counts a `--input-type=module` process of another Node binary as a payload, and skips an ENOENT on a process executable link only when the process directory is also gone; otherwise the process is unknown and the scan fails closed (ruling R12 items 15 and 17).
- **Returns,** as numbers, booleans and one digest only: the staging API's resident memory; the host's available memory; the API main-process digest in the c1 form (section 5.1); whether the QR-2 worker thread exists (by a method the tool review accepts; an unknown answer, also a throw in that read, is recorded as unknown, never as false; ruling R9 item 6).
- **Process reference** (ruling R3 item 6; A-P3-3, S-P3-4). The A2M manifest is built before A2 and cannot hold the reference. Root compares the returned digest with the API process digest of the reviewed A2 receipt: the delayed-check process, or the immediate-check process when the delayed check did not run (`A2_STOP_MARKET_AFTER_READY`, or `A2_STOP_MARKET_AT_CHECK` at the immediate checks). When that receipt holds no such digest, Root records the answer as unknown.
- **No effect.** No control verb, no reload, no environment read, no database connection. The tool region holds no control-command shape, reachable or not (ruling R9 item 6).
- **Writes.** One record directory with a progress record and a result file in the dedicated protected backup directory, and the shell's temporary here-document file that carries the tool.
- **Bounds.** Host bound 240 seconds (section 11.1). No retry.

### 6.12 AR reference reading (required once before GO-A1; after a drift, at the A1 end reading, or before GO-A2 under the 6-hour rule)

Ruling R3 item 1 (A-P2-2, S-P2-5). AR replaces the c6-form path through the retired collector families. It runs once, required, after the accepted RA(r8) receipt and before GO-A1, as the A1 reference (attempt a01; section 4A; Root ruling RP1, Q-C). After that it runs only after a process-identity drift stopped A1 or A2 at its gate or showed at A1's end reading (section 6.3), or once without a drift before the GO-A2 request when more than 6 hours pass between AR a01 and that request (section 6.1 item 11; Root ruling RP1, Q-C; ruling R4 items 7 and 10), under its own GO-AR, with its own job family, job IDs (attempt a01 first), manifest, consumed marker, nonce, receipt, envelope and record directory. It is built, reviewed and accepted with the A2, A2M and RA tool, not with the A1 tool (ruling R9 item 13).
- **When.** After Root has reviewed the stopped A1 or A2 receipt (or the A1 receipt with a difference at its end reading) and reported the drift and its cost to the owner; or, without a drift, once right before or together with the GO-A2 request, only when more than 6 hours pass between AR a01 and the GO-A2 request (section 6.1 item 11). Before the A1 request, AR a01 runs once after Root accepts the RA(r8) receipt. AR is not run without a drift at any other time, except the 6-hour case of section 6.1 item 11.
- **Reads, read only, in the c6 form of the collector start build** (copied by value, with the "no environment" read lists of section 6.2 for every unit; ruling R4 item 6): host health (recorded); no other S6 job in flight and no pending job (refuses otherwise); the settle wait of section 6.2 for a unit in automatic restart; production identity against the baseline (recorded); the class of the staging API, the trading worker, the research worker, the market stream, the fallback collector service and the fallback collector timer; the main-process digest in the c1 form (section 5.1) and the restart count of the staging API, the trading worker and the stream; the configuration and drop-in list digests of the staging API, the trading worker and the three market units; the timer's trigger count, accuracy, randomized delay, persistence, last trigger and next elapse; the collector service's result.
- **Refuses** only on a read that fails, another S6 job in flight, a pending job, or a unit still transitioning after the settle wait. Every other value is recorded, never compared. Host health is recorded and never refused (ruling R9 item 6). The in-flight scan follows the rule of section 6.11 (ruling R12 item 17).
- **Returns** states, counts, digests and booleans only.
- **No effect.** No control verb, no reload, no drop-in file, no environment read, no database connection, no delete. No file is read beyond unit properties and process start times. The tool region holds no control-command shape, reachable or not (ruling R9 item 6).
- **Writes.** One record directory with a progress record and a result file in the dedicated protected backup directory, and the shell's temporary here-document file that carries the tool.
- **Bounds.** P4 CHECK limits by value: host bound 240 seconds (section 11.1). No retry.
- **Root acceptance as a reference.** Root accepts the AR record as the process reference of A1 attempt a01 (AR a01), of the next A1 or A2 attempt, of A2 after a difference at the A1 end reading, or of A2 after the AR before GO-A2, only when production equals the baseline, the research worker is stopped, the staging API, the trading worker and the stream are running, the timer is active, the collector service is not failed, and the configuration digests equal their records (the staging API's the c1 `pairConfigDigests` value for the staging API, in the c6 form, as for the trading worker (ruling R6 item 1); the trading worker's configuration and drop-in list digests the c1 `pairConfigDigests` and `pairDropInDigests` values for the trading worker, in the c6 form, which c6 recorded as equal, as the A2 gate uses them (ruling R4 item 8; S-P3-4); the stream's the P4M m1 a03 manifest's market configuration digest of the stream, as in section 6.3 and section 6.13). Otherwise Root reports, and the owner decides; a changed configuration is not a drift and needs a packet revision. Under Shape R this configuration rule holds again after RA(r8), because RA(r8) returns the staging API to the post-P5a configuration (change map G5).

### 6.13 Manifest sources (ruling R17 item 1; ruling R16 item 3)

Root builds every A2, A2M, RA and AR manifest and private binding. This subsection names, for each field that ruling R17 item 1 sources, for `api.envDigest` and `api.p5aFlagSha`, and for the stream configuration digest (ruling R21 item 6), the record and the field it comes from; every other field follows the section that names it. It names records and fields only, never a value, a path, a tree name or a digest beyond those already in this packet. Root copies each value by value from the named record into the private manifest or binding, checks the equalities noted here by equality only, and prints no value.

| Field (mode) | Source record and field |
| --- | --- |
| `hb.freshMs` (all) | The heartbeat window `heartbeatMs` of the accepted P5a tool and of the accepted R5a tool (the same window). |
| `hb.intervalMs` (all) | The interval input of sections 8 and 11.1 (the packet default), the same in every mode. |
| `u4Record` (all) | The U-4 record of section 10. |
| `retirementRecord` (A2, A2M, RA) | The retirement inventory record of section 10. |
| `retirementRecord` (AR) | The same record. No AR manifest is built before the inventory record exists; when an AR is needed before GO-A2, Root writes the inventory first (section 10). An AR needs its own GO-AR (section 14). |
| `api.oldTree` (A2, RA) | The working directory that the accepted P5a tool's post-switch configuration function (`postLoadedOf`) sets for the staging API: the P4 stage directory joined with the P1 tree name of `ba1a433`. The stage directory is the P4 x4 a02 binding field `p4.stageDir`, equal to the P1 a02 field `p1.apiStageDir` and the collector c1 field `pm.stageDir`. |
| `api.argvOld`, `api.nodePath` (A2, RA) | The start entry of the same function, path and arguments, computed from the P4 x4 a02 binding field `units.API.loaded` and the working directory above. This is the configuration that the P5a a01 gate passed. |
| `api.newTree` (A2, RA) | The A1 binding field `a1.apiStageDir` joined with the A1 final name for the pin (`jobNames.final`), exactly as the accepted A1 receipt reports it. |
| `api.listDigest`, `api.fileDigests`, `api.admissionOffSha` (A2, RA) | The accepted A1 record fields `dropIns.listDigest`, `dropIns.fileDigests` and `dropIns.admissionOffSha` (ruling R16 item 2(e)). `listDigest` must equal the collector c1 field `ready.pairDropInDigests.API`; a difference is a drift, and Root rules. |
| `api.envDigest`, `api.p5aFlagSha` (A2, RA) | The accepted P5a a01 record: the API environment digest without the QR-2 key, and `export.layerSha`. |
| `adp.apiPort`, `adp.selector` (A2, RA) | The P4 x4 a02 binding fields `p4.apiPort` and `p4.migrationSelector`, without the `form` key of the selector. These are the values that P5a used. |
| `adp.trees.OLD` (A2, RA) | The P5a a01 exec directory: `build-summary.json` fields `treeDigest`, `counts.treeFiles` and `authRoutes`, and `served-list.json` assets as `{url, size, sha256}`. P5a pinned both files (`pins.buildSummary`, `pins.servedList`). |
| `adp.trees.NEW` and `tree` (A2, RA) | The accepted A1 record and the A1 manifest package fields `treeDigest` and `treeFiles`. The served list and the auth routes come from the pin's Git blobs by the rule of section 2.1. The canonical tree form must equal A1's; Root checks the A1 receipt value. |
| `adp.sinceA2Sec` (RA) | The reviewed A2 receipt field `startedUtc`, in epoch seconds. After a lost A2 receipt, the A2 manifest field `dispatchedAt`, which is earlier and so gives a wider read. |
| `ra.records[].startedAt` (RA, PROGRESS record after a lost receipt) | That attempt's manifest field `startBy`: the latest possible start, so the lost-receipt wait of 1335 seconds counted from it is never shorter than the real one. |
| `baseline` (RA) and `ra.review.baseline` | The delayed check r6 a02 with its unit rows, while the latest receipt reports production equal. No accepted record holds per-unit rows of a later reading; after a production change or an `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE`, RA needs a Root ruling and a packet revision that names a source of unit rows (section 12). |
| Binding `channel` (all) | The accepted A1 private binding channel (equal to the c1 channel apart from the known-hosts path), with the known-hosts copy of this exec directory. |
| Binding `ra.recordRoot` and `ra.roles` (all) | The A1 binding fields `a1.recordRoot` and `a1.roles` (equal to the c1 field `pm.backupDir` and the P1 and P4 roles). The order of the four production units in the role list must match `baseline.units`. |
| Stream configuration digest (A2; Root's AR acceptance check, section 6.12) | The P4M m1 a03 manifest's market configuration digest of the stream (the stream's entry under `preconditions.market.configDigests`), which the m1 a03 run found equal (`configEqual` true). The P4M binding holds no digest field (ruling R21 item 6). |

**Sources of this packet** (change map section 2.3). Every field takes the source this table names, re-derived for this packet and never copied from a revision 8 manifest. Under Shape R the fields that describe the `ba1a433` side (`api.oldTree`, `api.argvOld`, `api.nodePath`, `api.envDigest`, `api.p5aFlagSha`, `adp.trees.OLD`, `adp.apiPort`, `adp.selector`, the trading-worker digests, the stream configuration digest and the timer facts) take the same sources as in revision 8, because RA(r8) returns the staging API to that state. `api.newTree`, `adp.trees.NEW`, `tree`, `api.listDigest`, `api.fileDigests` and `api.admissionOffSha` come from the accepted A1 record of this packet (the list digest is expected equal to the c1 value; change map G5). The layer digest comes from the P2 tree and `api.argvOld`. The A1 reference is the AR a01 record (kind `AR_RECORD`). The case 4 memory estimate is 384 unless the A0' measurement exceeds it (section 2.5). `a0Record`, `retirementRecord` and `u4Record` are this packet's records. The revision 8 A2 a01 receipt is a source only of the RA(r8) manifest (section 4A; Root ruling RP1, Q-D). The bindings of A2 and RA carry the P5a layer digest, not its name (ruling R17), as in revision 8.

The A2 manifest has no `argvMax` key, and the runner checks that `manifest.rolesSha` equals the hash of the canonical binding role list before any host contact in every mode (built since the follow-up round; A2RA snapshot r9; ruling R17 item 4; ruling R19 item 4(l); ruling R21 item 5(j); ruling R22 item 9; Root ruling RP2, E2).

**Root-only reads.** The values that only an execution folder holds (section 4A, Root-only reads) are read by Root alone when it builds a manifest; children audit field names only (second opinion d1 P2-5).

## 7. Layer rule

Live API drop-in order after the accepted RA(r8) (state S-0 of section 4A), by role: earlier release layers; the P4 layer (working directory, start command, flag 0); the P5a flag layer (flag 1 only); the admission-off layer (five research flags 0), last.

**Re-pin layer text**, exactly: `[Service]`, `WorkingDirectory=<staged tree>`, `ExecStart=` (reset), `ExecStart=<argv>`. The argv is the P4 argv with only its last element replaced by the staged tree's server entry. The layer has no `Environment=` line.

**Name.** Matches the accepted layer-name form; sorts after every live layer other than the admission-off layer, including the P5a flag layer; sorts before the admission-off layer; collides with no live, pending or archived name. The accepted order rule is applied to the live list read at the gate, never to a constant. A name built from a fixed prefix plus revision hex can sort wrongly (main packet P4 step 2 hazard), so the name is derived from the live list. If no name fits, stop; the order rule changes only by ruling.

**Name candidates** (revision 8; ruling R30 item 1). The function tries the 100 candidates of revision 7 in their order, then one last candidate: the P5a flag layer's recorded name without its `.conf` suffix, then `0-s6-repin-`, then the first eight hex characters of the pin, then `.conf` (section 5.2 has the reason it sorts between the two layers and the checks it passes). When no candidate passes, the function returns null, A1's boolean is false and the A2 gate ends `A2_NO_LAYER_NAME`.

**Two-form layer pattern** (revision 8; ruling R30 item 2). The pattern that recognises the re-pin layer, its pending file and its archived files has two forms:
- the old form: two digits, a dash, `s6-repin-`, the first eight hex characters of the pin, `.conf`;
- the new form: the escaped stem of the P5a flag layer's name, then `0-s6-repin-`, the first eight hex characters of the pin, `.conf`. The tool builds it inside the host process from the P5a layer name, which it finds on disk by the layer's recorded digest (in A2 the one live file with that digest; in RA among every `.conf` file in the drop-in directory, loaded or not); the bindings carry the digest, not the name (ruling R31 item 2, which corrects ruling R30 items 2 and 3). No real name appears in this packet.

Each form takes the same two suffix families: the pending file, and the archived files (the archive suffix forms of the build; this packet names no archive suffix, ruling R10 item 3(g)). A layer of either form is the re-pin layer. Two live re-pin layers refuse before any effect in RA with `PRE_ROW_UNRECOGNISED`. In A2, two live re-pin layers of one of each form refuse, among the layer checks, with `PRE_DROPIN_RECORD` or `A2_NO_LAYER_NAME`; two live old-form layers inside the A1-recorded list that both sort before the P5a flag layer can pass the gate, and only RA refuses them (the section 12 residual; rulings R33 item 2 and R34 item 2); the code is not `PRE_DROPIN_SHAPE`. The name derivation checks only the candidate name and that name followed by a dot. A pending or archived file of the other form is caught by the A2 gate with `PRE_LAYER_FORM_PRESENT`; RA has no collision check. The A2 gate applies the two-form pattern only to files with a suffix (section 6.2): among the layer checks, a live file is refused only when it is outside the A1-recorded drop-in list or makes the derivation fail. The A2 disk rows use the derived name. RA applies the pattern in the archive scan and the end rows (section 8). The AR and A2M reads classify no layer names (ruling R31 item 4, D32: the lane has no layer classifier). A name that matches neither form is not the re-pin layer. The P5a name stays in the host-side process and is never returned.

**The P5a layer found by its digest** (revision 8; ruling R31 item 2). The A2 and RA bindings carry the P5a layer digest, not its name. The tools find the P5a layer name on disk by that digest and build the new form from it. This is sound for these reasons. In A2, the recorded drop-in list digest (it hashes the base names) and the rule that no live layer sorts after the P5a flag layer pin the found name to the layer that A1 recorded. In A2 the lookup is by digest among the live files. In RA it is by digest among every `.conf` file in the drop-in directory, loaded or not. In RA, with no match the new form is not recognised and RA refuses before any effect: `PRE_ROW_UNRECOGNISED` in the host case, where A2 wrote a new-form layer (the row decision, with the records showing the A2 write), otherwise `PRE_DROPIN_RECORD` at the post-list check. With more than one match RA ends `PRE_DROPIN_SHAPE` (open item D35, ruled by R32 item 1).

**Effective values after A2.**
- Working directory and start command: the setters in byte order are earlier layers, the P4 layer, then the re-pin layer, which is last, so the staged tree is in force.
- QR-2 flag: the P4 layer sets 0 and the later P5a flag layer sets 1; the re-pin and admission-off layers do not set it. Effective 1, from the P5a flag layer only.
- Research flags: 0 from the admission-off layer, still last.
- An environment-file value overrides a unit `Environment=` value, so the gate re-checks that no environment file sets the flag.

**Why the re-pin layer must not set the flag.** Setting 0 would turn the panel off. Setting 1 would make the flag depend on two layers, and R5a would no longer turn it off.

**After RA.** The re-pin layer is archived and not loaded; the loaded list equals the post-P5a list; the P4 layer is again the last setter of the working directory and start command, so the `ba1a433` tree is in force; the flag stays effective 1.

**The revision 8 archive** (change map G1). After RA(r8) the drop-in directory also holds one archive of the revision 8 layer (new form, pin prefix `33e5a8c3`, RA archive suffix). It matches neither form of this packet's pattern, because the pin prefix differs, so it is not this packet's re-pin layer: the A2 gate records it among the other files of its inventory line, and this packet's RA compares it there by count and digest.

## 8. RA rollback (its own GO-RA)

Built and accepted together with A2 (rollback readiness). Copied by value from the accepted R5a tool, with named adaptations (ruling R2, P1-1, P2-3, O-7 and O-12; ruling R3 item 2: the layer-state table, the lost-receipt clause, the rollback-family rule and the trading-worker heartbeat; ruling R5: the A2 inventory line, the named end outcomes and the exclusion by end state; ruling R6: the case 5 immediate checks, code precedence, the one production rule, the named terminals, the progress lines, the L0 and LP recognition, the heartbeat by tick time and the crash-looping staging API; rulings R7 and R9: the P3 build requirements, the phase-aware routing, the lost-receipt records, the termination rule, the heartbeat interval and the shared inventory function; rulings R10, R12 and R13: the market-branch allowance, the termination rule without a command, the budget stop in the start phase, the restart-count rule, the two-sided HEALTH_503 rule, the tree walk and the put-back tree check).

- **Gate.** Host, production identity, staging API drop-in layers, environment sources (the same keys as A2, without the database connection key) and the database read only through the migration connection selector, as A2 (section 6.2, without the startup probe), against the Root-reviewed A2 receipt and the last accepted record before it, or under the lost-receipt clause below. Production baseline: the Root-reviewed A2 receipt, or the r6 a02 baseline when that receipt has no post-switch rows, was lost, or reports a production change Root has not reviewed. After a prior `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE`, the baseline is the production reading that Root's recorded review in the RA manifest names (ruling R6 item 4). A production-identity difference at the RA gate ends `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE` before any effect (case 9). The research worker stopped. The staging API and trading-worker journals must be readable, and the R5a 60-second read must not be filled to the line cap; the read since A2 is recorded only (ruling R6 item 11; S-P3-3); their counts are recorded, never a stop. The settle wait of section 6.2 applies before the classification. The staging API is exempt from its refusal: a staging API still in automatic restart, in the activating state with a start sub-state, or with its own pending start, stop or restart job at the bound (for example a pin that crash-loops) is classified restarting and stopped, and its own job is not a pending job (ruling R6 item 11; S-P3-3; ruling R9 item 5); a staging API still in another transitioning state at the bound (for example still stopping) refuses before any effect, and Root rules (ruling R6 item 5). Any state of the re-pin layer that is not a row of the layer-state table below refuses before any effect. The `ba1a433` tree is checked exact with the lane walk (the P5a walk copied by value, with the P5a EXACT rule) before the first database read; its digest and file count come from the on-disk measurement that the accepted P5a tool verified for that tree (ruling R12 item 10; ruling R13 item 10). The walk takes the P5a real-path form of section 6.2 (ruling R19 item 4(g)). The drop-in list digest, the per-file drop-in digests and the admission-off layer digest come from the accepted A1 record, as in section 6.2 (ruling R16 item 3); the P5a a01 record stays the source of the environment digest and the P5a flag layer digest. After a production change that Root reviewed, RA needs a unit-row source and a packet revision (section 6.13 and section 12).
- **Need-reload gate** (ruling R3 item 2; S-P2-1). No pending job of any unit other than the staging API's own start, stop or restart job (ruling R6 item 11; S-P3-3), and no unit needing a reload, except the staging API in a recognised need-reload row of the layer-state table. Any other unit that needs a reload refuses. A need-reload row is recognised only when the A2 record or a prior RA record shows a rename or archive of the live re-pin layer (A2's rename into place, the A2 unwind's archive, an RA archive or an RA put-back) without a completed reload after it in that record or a later one: rows LL-r, LU-r and LR-r (ruling R4 item 3; A-P3-3, S-P3-1). The source of that fact is the Root-reviewed receipt, or, when it was lost, the host progress record. A rename or archive labelled `ATTEMPTED` or `DONE` counts as shown when the disk holds the file in the matching form with the expected digest; a reload counts as completed only when labelled `DONE` (ruling R5 item 4; A-P3-1). A torn progress line counts as `ATTEMPTED` and the disk decides (section 6.4; ruling R9 item 10l). One torn last line with no line ending, at the end of the file, is read as an `ATTEMPTED` step of unknown kind; if the row's facts are missing because of it, row recognition refuses (ruling R12 item 15). The A2 and RA tools write these lines before and after every rename, archive, put-back and reload (sections 6.4 and 6.6 and Effects below; ruling R6 item 7; S-P3-1). L0-r, LP-r and LQ-r, and any "-r" row without such a record, refuse before any effect, because the reload would load a change from outside this packet (section 12).
- **Classification gate for the units RA does not control** (P1-1; R5a unit-class style). The market stream, the fallback collector service, the fallback collector timer and the trading worker are each in a known class: running (with any main process), inactive, or failed. The class, the main-process continuity and the restart count of each are recorded, never compared with the c6, A1, AR or A2 values. Only an unknown state, a state still transitioning after the settle wait, or a pending job of any unit other than the staging API's own start, stop or restart job, refuses. A running stream whose process digest cannot be read refuses before any effect (`PRE_UNIT_SNAPSHOT`), as in A2 (ruling R13 item 13; ruling R19 item 2; ruling R21 item 5(f)).
- **Trading-worker heartbeat** (ruling R3 item 2; A-P2-1; ruling R4 item 5, A-P3-4, S-P3-2). The adaptation of the R5a heartbeat refusal (`PRE_HEARTBEAT_STALE`) and the R5a heartbeat check (`CHK_HEARTBEAT`: the own fresh row, no other fresh row, no Live-mode row): the RA gate records the heartbeat row count and freshness and refuses only on an unreadable queue, a Live-mode row, a Live signal above zero, an empty set of rows of the trading worker role while the trading worker runs, or a fresh row that the tick-time rule does not admit. The queue is also unreadable when any row lacks a string role (`HEARTBEAT_UNREADABLE`). Mode and Live are checked on all rows, unfiltered, and the gate and the immediate checks refuse on a Live signal above zero (`liveSignals` must be a non-negative integer, otherwise it is unreadable). At the RA gate an empty set of rows of the trading worker role refuses when the trading worker runs and is recorded when it does not (ruling R12 item 7). **Tick-time rule** (ruling R6 item 10, which replaces ruling R5 item 8; S-P3-2): a heartbeat row holds a per-instance identifier, a tick and a role, not a process identity, so the rule uses the tick time of the accepted form. A row is "own" when its tick is at or after the classified trading worker's main-process start minus one second (at the immediate checks, the start of its current main process); more than one fresh own row refuses; a fresh non-own row counts as a previous process's row, and is recorded, not refused, only when its tick is before that start or, with no running trading worker, its tick does not advance across two reads at least twice the heartbeat interval plus 250 ms apart; any other fresh non-own row refuses before any effect. Where RA requires the trading worker's own heartbeat (the immediate checks below), the R5a rule of exactly one own row is kept for a running trading worker, so no guard is lowered. A stale heartbeat is recorded and never refused, in every class, also for a running trading worker; this is a named relaxation of R5a `PRE_HEARTBEAT_STALE`, because RA gives the trading worker no command. The RA immediate checks keep "no other fresh row" (under the tick-time rule) and "no Live-mode row" in every class, and require the trading worker's own fresh heartbeat, as exactly one own row, only when it was fresh at RA's gate and the trading worker is still the same process; otherwise they record it. RA admits the states after GO-RC (collector service and timer stopped), after GO-RM (stream, collector service and timer stopped), with a failed collector service, after a stream restart and after a trading-worker restart.
- **Heartbeat reads and interval** (ruling R7 item 4, whose environment override is replaced by ruling R9 items 10m and 11b). The core filters the heartbeat rows to the trading worker role for the own-row and freshness rules only; mode and Live are checked on all rows (ruling R12 item 7). The heartbeat interval is a manifest input from 50 to 5000 ms (the worker interval defaults to 250 ms at the pin, as ruling R7 item 4 set, and the default stays); it replaces only the environment override of ruling R7 item 4 (ruling R10 item 3(f)). The two reads that test for advancing are at least 2 × interval + 250 ms apart, and the timing proof asserts the A2 and RA gate budgets (section 11.1) at the 5000 ms maximum interval.
- **Hooks and links.** The two-way rule of section 6.3, with the same property lists, in every class. Every unit property read of RA other than the host-side environment-source read of the staging API's own environment property (digest only) uses the "no environment" read lists of section 6.2; the build asserts this for the class, process, configuration and hooks read lists, and asserts that the environment property is read only in the host-side process (ruling R4 item 6; ruling R5 item 6, A-P3-3).
- **Timer window, only when the timer is active** (named adaptation, O-12). The rule of section 6.3 with RA's window W_RA = 167 seconds (section 11.1): the next elapse must be later than now plus W_RA plus the accuracy and randomized delay; otherwise a read-only wait of at most 420 seconds, then a stop before any effect (`RA_TIMER_WINDOW`). When the timer is inactive or failed, no timer-window wait applies.
- **Collector run kept out of the reload.** A collector run in progress at the gate is waited out, read only, within the same 420-second bound, otherwise a stop before any effect. After the proved API stop and before the archive, RA re-checks (at most 5 seconds) that no collector run and no job is pending; a run in progress gets a read-only wait of up to 15 seconds.
- **API state (R5a unit-class rule).** The staging API may be running, restarting (also in automatic restart, in the activating state with a start sub-state, or with its own pending start, stop or restart job, at the settle bound, for example a crash loop; ruling R6 item 11; ruling R9 item 5), stopped or failed; only an unknown state, or another state still transitioning at the settle bound, refuses. A failed API is cleared with reset-failed right before its main start only, never before an unwind start, and only under the GO-RA reset-failed clause. The manifest carries a boolean `resetFailedGranted`, true only when the GO-RA carries that optional clause; a failed API with the flag false refuses before any effect with `PRE_RESET_FAILED_NOT_GRANTED` (ruling R9 item 10g).
- **Layer-state table** (ruling R3 item 2; S-P2-1, S-P2-3). RA decides its row from the files on disk and the staging API's need-reload flag, never from an in-memory flag. In every row, before any effect, the staging API drop-in files on disk must equal the row's expected list and digests (the post-P5a list, plus the re-pin layer in rows LL); a need-reload row is recognised only then, and only with the progress-record fact of the need-reload gate above. In a recognised need-reload row, RA's one reload is the reconciling reload. **Two-form pattern** (revision 8; ruling R30 item 2): RA recognises the re-pin layer, its pending file and its archived files with the two-form pattern of section 7, in the drop-in read, the archive scan and the end rows alike. RA takes the P5a layer name for the new form from the disk, by the layer's recorded digest among every `.conf` file in the drop-in directory, loaded or not (section 7). With no match the new form is not recognised and RA refuses before any effect: `PRE_ROW_UNRECOGNISED` in the host case, where A2 wrote a new-form layer, otherwise `PRE_DROPIN_RECORD` at the post-list check; with more than one match RA ends `PRE_DROPIN_SHAPE`. A layer of either form is the re-pin layer, so every row below, the compared file set and the end rows treat a file of either form as the re-pin layer's file in the matching live, pending or archived state. Two live re-pin layers refuse before any effect with `PRE_ROW_UNRECOGNISED`. RA has no collision check. A pending file is row LP, with no record-explanation check; the archive-form rule below covers only archived files. **Compared file set** (ruling R4 item 4; A-P3-2): a row compares the live drop-in list and the files that carry the re-pin layer's derived name in its pending or archive form. Every other file of the drop-in directory (every file other than the live drop-in list and the pending and archive forms of the derived name; for example archives of earlier packets) is compared by count and digest with the A2 gate inventory (section 6.2), read from the Root-reviewed A2 receipt or, under the lost-receipt clause, from the gate line of the A2 host progress record, never from the P5a a01 record (ruling R5 item 1; A-P2-1, S-P2-2); RA reads it with the shared function of section 6.2 (digests sorted non-decreasing, duplicates allowed, at most 64; ruling R9 item 8); any difference refuses before any effect.

  | Row | Files on disk | API needs a reload | RA action after the proved stop and the pre-archive re-check |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | L0-n | none: no re-pin layer, no pending file, no archive (A2 never wrote), admitted only when no listed A2 or RA record shows a write, rename or archive of the re-pin layer, and any listed write, rename or archive line of it, labelled `ATTEMPTED` or `DONE`, refuses (ruling R6 item 8; S-P3-7; ruling R7 item 3, P3-1) | no | no file change, no reload; loaded-configuration gate |
  | L0-r | as L0-n | yes | refuses before any effect (no progress-record fact; revision 4; section 12) |
  | LP-n | pending file only (A2 ended after the pending write and before the rename); any digest; admitted only when no listed A2 or RA record shows a rename of it: a rename counts as shown only when labelled `DONE`, or labelled `ATTEMPTED` with the file renamed on disk, and an `ATTEMPTED` line with the pending file still pending is row LP (ruling R6 item 8; S-P3-7; ruling R7 item 3, P3-1) | no | record the pending file's digest in the progress record, then archive the file by no-clobber rename (no delete; ruling R5 item 5); no reload; loaded-configuration gate |
  | LP-r | as LP-n | yes | refuses before any effect (no progress-record fact; revision 4; section 12) |
  | LL-n | live re-pin layer with the layer digest; no pending file; no archive (A2 ended after the rename, or an earlier RA put the layer back or stopped before it changed the layer) | no | archive the layer by no-clobber rename; one reload; loaded-configuration gate (the normal path) |
  | LL-r | as LL-n (A2 ended after the rename and before its reload, or an RA put-back reload failed or was not reached), admitted only with the progress-record fact | yes | archive the layer by no-clobber rename; one reload, which is also the reconciling reload; loaded-configuration gate |
  | LU-n | archived by the A2 unwind, which the A2 receipt or the A2 progress record shows (archive digest equal to the layer digest for an archived live layer, or to the pre-rename pending digest line, from the reviewed A2 receipt or, when that receipt was lost, from the A2 progress record, for an archived pending file); no live layer; no pending file (ruling R5 item 5; S-P3-1, S-P3-2; ruling R6 item 7, A-P3-6) | no | no file change, no reload; loaded-configuration gate |
  | LU-r | as LU-n (the unwind reload failed or was not reached), admitted only with the progress-record fact | yes | the reconciling reload; loaded-configuration gate |
  | LR-n | archived by an earlier RA attempt (archive digest equal to the layer digest; that attempt's record shows its row LL), admitted under the rollback-family rule; no live layer; no pending file | no | no file change, no reload; loaded-configuration gate |
  | LR-r | as LR-n, admitted only with the progress-record fact | yes | the reconciling reload; loaded-configuration gate |
  | LQ-n | a pending file archived by an earlier RA attempt (archive digest equal to that attempt's pre-rename pending digest line, from its reviewed receipt or, when that receipt was lost, from its progress record (ruling R6 item 7; A-P3-6); that attempt's record shows its row LP), admitted under the rollback-family rule; no live layer; no pending file (revision 4) | no | no file change, no reload; loaded-configuration gate |
  | LQ-r | as LQ-n | yes | refuses before any effect (no progress-record fact; revision 4; section 12) |

  Any other combination (for example a live layer with another digest, a pending file beside a live layer or an archive, two archives within the compared file set, or a drop-in file set that differs from the row) refuses before any effect. An archive-form file of the derived name that no listed A2 or prior-RA record explains refuses before any effect (ruling R5 item 5; S-P3-1). Files that look like row L0 while a listed A2 or RA record shows a write, rename or archive line of the re-pin layer (labelled `ATTEMPTED` or `DONE`), and files that look like row LP while a listed record shows its rename (labelled `DONE`, or labelled `ATTEMPTED` with the file renamed on disk), refuse before any effect, and Root rules (ruling R6 item 8; S-P3-7; ruling R7 item 3, P3-1). Ruling R9 item 11d accepts the archive suffix forms; this packet names no file form, and RA decides the row from the files on disk and the records, never from a suffix alone (ruling R10 item 3(g)). RA archives the layer itself only in rows LL; only there can the put-back of cases 1 and 2 run. The pending-file archive is never put back; rows LP and LQ count as "never written" for the case table (ruling R4 item 2; S-P2-2, A-P3-1). Rows LR and LQ are told apart by the prior attempt's record of its own row (LL or LP), never by the digest alone, because a complete pending file has the layer digest. No job of this packet deletes a file, so a rename or archive of the live re-pin layer always leaves a file of the derived name in its live or archive form; no progress record can therefore show the need-reload fact beside the files of rows L0, LP or LQ, and rows L0-r, LP-r and LQ-r always refuse (ruling R4 item 3). After the file action and any reload, every row continues with the loaded-configuration gate, production identity, the market re-check, the start and readiness, and the immediate checks.
- **Lost A2 receipt** (ruling R3 item 2; S-P2-2). With no Root-reviewed A2 receipt, RA works as R5a does with no P5a receipt: it reads the A2 host progress record on the host; it takes the post-P5a drop-in list digest from the accepted A1 record (ruling R16 item 3) and the environment digest from the accepted P5a a01 record (the re-pin layer has no environment line, so the digest is unchanged), the layer digest from the A2 manifest, the production baseline from the r6 a02 baseline, and the compared file set from the gate inventory line of the A2 host progress record, never from the P5a a01 record; with a readable record that holds no such line, A2 had no effect and no layer exists, so RA refuses before any effect and Root reports (ruling R5 item 1; A-P2-1, S-P2-2); an unreadable or missing A2 progress record refuses before any effect and needs a packet revision (ruling R6 item 5; S-P3-5); and it classifies from the files on disk under the layer-state table. A row whose facts the progress record does not show refuses. RA after a lost A2 receipt is admitted only after the lost-receipt wait of 1335 seconds from the A2 start, so that the A2 host process has ended (section 11.1). **Records** (ruling R9 item 10h): a record whose source is PROGRESS (A2 or RA) carries sha null and the job ID. RA reads the progress record that the job ID names in the dedicated protected record directory, checks the job ID and the form, and records the record's sha256 in its gate line and in its receipt; Root reviews that record afterwards. A record whose source is RECEIPT needs a sha and non-null lines.
- **Rollback-family rule** (ruling R3 item 2; S-P2-3; ruling R4 items 1 and 2, A-P2-1, S-P2-1, S-P2-2; ruling R5 items 2, 3 and 5, S-P2-1, S-P3-1, S-P3-3, S-P3-5, S-P3-6, A-P3-2; ruling R6 items 2, 4 and 5). The five sentences below are used word for word in section 9, the owner summary and GO-RA.
  - **Outcomes.** RA outcomes: `RA_API_READY`, `RA_STOP_MARKET_AFTER_READY` and `RA_STOP_AT_CHECK` end with the API ready on `ba1a433`; `RA_UNWOUND`, `RA_UNWIND_RELOAD_FAILED`, `RA_UNWIND_START_FAILED`, `RA_API_NOT_READY`, `RA_API_STOPPED`, `RA_BUDGET_STOP`, `RA_STOP_NOT_PROVED` and `RA_STOP_MARKET_API_NOT_READY` end in a recognised layer state without the API proved ready on `ba1a433` by RA's immediate checks; `RA_UNWIND_FAILED` and `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE` are excluded.
