  - **Next RA.** After an RA attempt that did not end with the API ready on `ba1a433`, also after a termination, which never counts as an end with the API ready and is followed by the lost-receipt wait of 1335 seconds, Root may request the next RA attempt under a new GO-RA that lists the prior attempts; the tool admits it only when the files on disk form a recognised layer state, the prior records show the facts that state needs, and production equals the baseline.
  - **Exclusion.** Excluded: after `RA_UNWIND_FAILED` (an archived layer could not be put back) Root rules by a packet revision before any next RA; after `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE` (a production-identity difference read at any RA step, at the gate before any effect, the RD-32 state) no next RA is admitted without Root's recorded review of the difference in its manifest, which also sets the production baseline that RA compares against.
  - **Terminal RA and the accepted RA receipt.** An RA that ends with the API ready on `ba1a433`, other than with `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE`, is terminal for the RA family, and no next RA follows it; its receipt is the accepted RA receipt, for the RX tripwire and the return of R5a, R4, R4H, RM and RC, when its end row is L0-n, LU-n, LR-n or LQ-n, the post-P5a configuration is loaded, production is unchanged and the API is ready, after Root accepts it, with a Root review first for a market or check stop; a `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE` receipt with the API ready on `ba1a433` in such an end row may be accepted by Root in the same way after its review of the difference.
  - **A2 receipt that reopens the chain.** A Root-reviewed A2 receipt with end row L0-n or LU-n, no pending file, the API ready on `ba1a433`, the post-P5a configuration loaded and production unchanged reopens that chain in the same way, with no RA.

  The tool refuses before any effect when a prior attempt's record shows `RA_UNWIND_FAILED`, an outcome with the API ready on `ba1a433` other than `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE` (`RA_API_READY`, `RA_STOP_MARKET_AFTER_READY` or `RA_STOP_AT_CHECK`, also after case 5), or `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE` without Root's review of the difference recorded in the manifest. A Root ruling after `RA_UNWIND_FAILED` acts only through a packet revision (ruling R6 item 5; A-P3-3). A terminated attempt has no end code and never counts as an outcome with the API ready, also when its progress record shows readiness; the next RA is admitted after the lost-receipt wait of 1335 seconds from that attempt's start (ruling R6 item 5; S-P3-5). The gate applies this wait to every prior RA record whose end code ends in `_TERMINATED` (`PRE_TERMINATED` and `EFF_TERMINATED`), as it does to a PROGRESS record; a terminated record with no start time refuses (ruling R24 item 4, F3; reading 2). The RA manifest binds the last prior RA attempt (its job ID and the digest of its receipt; for a lost receipt a PROGRESS record with sha null and the job ID, which the tool reads and whose sha256 it records; ruling R9 item 10h), as the R5a manifest binds its prior attempt (ruling R5 item 5; S-P3-1). The next attempt reads the prior attempt's Root-reviewed receipt, or, when that receipt was lost, its host progress record after the lost-receipt wait of 1335 seconds from that attempt's start, and takes its row from the files on disk: row LL when the layer is live (put back, or never archived; the layer digest from the A2 manifest); row LR when an RA attempt archived the live layer; row LQ when an RA attempt archived a pending file; otherwise row LU, LP or L0. Without that prior-attempt fact, an archive by an earlier RA is not a recognised row and refuses. This is the only contingency rule of this packet after an attempt with effects.
- **States with no automatic next step** (ruling R6 item 5; A-P3-3, S-P3-5). Each of these needs Root before any next RA, by the path named: (a) a terminal RA receipt (`RA_API_READY`, `RA_STOP_MARKET_AFTER_READY` or `RA_STOP_AT_CHECK`, also after case 5) that Root does not accept: a Root ruling or a packet revision; (b) `RA_UNWIND_FAILED`: a packet revision; (c) a staging API still transitioning at the settle bound in a state other than automatic restart, or the activating state with a start sub-state (for example still stopping): RA refuses before any effect, and Root rules; (d) a repeated case 4 in an -n row with no progress (the next RA is admissible, but it runs no reload, so a lasting loaded-configuration difference fails it again): a Root ruling; (e) a lost A2 receipt with an unreadable or missing A2 progress record: RA refuses before any effect, and a packet revision decides. After `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE` the next RA waits for Root's recorded review in its manifest, or Root accepts a receipt with the API ready on `ba1a433` after its review (case 9). A terminated RA attempt is not such a state: it never counts as an end with the API ready, and the next RA follows after the lost-receipt wait of 1335 seconds.
- **Effects.** Stop the API and prove the stop when it runs or restarts (100 seconds, no grace; if not proved or the API becomes failed, no further control call; an error in the proof wait other than a termination, also a snapshot fault or a budget stop, counts as not proved and sends no further command, and RA ends `RA_STOP_NOT_PROVED`; a command timeout also ends `RA_STOP_NOT_PROVED`, and any other failure of the stop command gets one snapshot read, with `RA_UNWOUND` (case 5: nothing started, the API on the configuration found, a failed API recorded) when the snapshot shows the API running with no job and the gate's main process or failed with no job, otherwise `RA_STOP_NOT_PROVED` (ruling R14 item 3; a failed API counts as stopped only with an empty job queue, so a failed API with a queued start job is not stopped, the stop poll continues within its bound, sends no further command and the end is not proved: section 6.6; ruling R25 item 7, G1; ruling R26 item 5); if the adapter cannot tell a timeout from another failure, every failure takes the snapshot rule; the receipt records `stopCommandFailed` and `apiAtEnd`; ruling R13 items 3 and 4); the pre-archive re-check; the file action of the layer-state row (archive the live re-pin layer, or a pending file, by no-clobber rename; never a delete); one manager reload when RA archived the live re-pin layer or the row needs the reconciling reload (ruling R4 item 11); loaded-configuration gate (drop-in list equal to the post-P5a list, working directory and start command of the `ba1a433` tree from the P4 layer, QR-2 flag effective 1 from the P5a flag layer, research flags 0, no heap option); production identity; market re-check against RA's own gate reading (each market unit in the same class as at RA's gate; a stream that ran at the gate the same main process with no restart; a timer that was active still active; a collector service that was not failed not failed); start the API (with the restart-count rule of section 6.4); readiness; immediate checks against the `ba1a433` served manifest, with the trading worker's heartbeat required only as stated above. Health at the immediate checks is 200 in Paper mode; the token HEALTH_503 (a 503 with Paper-only mode) is accepted only in RA, and only when the trading worker shows the same not-running class (INACTIVE or FAILED) at the RA gate read and at the immediate read, and A2 never accepts it; both directions are tested, the gate running and now stopped, and the gate stopped and now running, and a different not-running class at the two reads is not accepted (ruling R12 item 8, amended by ruling R13 item 16 and ruling R19 item 4(e)). RA gives the market units and the trading worker no command in every class. Before every rename, archive, put-back and reload, RA writes an fsynced `<step> ATTEMPTED` progress line with the expected source and target form and the expected digest, never a name, and after the step a `<step> DONE` line; the pending-file digest line of row LP is such a line, and the RA receipt carries it (ruling R6 item 7; S-P3-1, A-P3-6). A failed or partial progress write is followed by a fresh line and a `NOTE TORN_PREVIOUS` line, and the reader counts the torn step as `ATTEMPTED` and lets the disk decide (section 6.4; ruling R9 item 10l). **Phases** (ruling R9 item 2): in READY (the API proved ready) RA sends no control command at all: no stop, start, reset-failed, reload, rename, archive, put-back or pending write. In MARKET (a market change seen) the guard admits only a consumable allowance, granted when the change is seen (ruling R9a; ruling R10 item 2): the branch's one start, its readiness poll, after a failed start or readiness one proved stop, and one reset-failed under the optional clause; nothing else in MARKET, and nothing at all in READY. The error routing of cases 8 and 10 and a guard in the control-command layer enforce this, and a termination in any phase sends no command (a progress `NOTE` line). **Termination** (ruling R12 item 5, which withdraws the split of ruling R10 item 3(b)): a graceful termination never sends a command, in any phase, so there is no stop, no put-back and no start; RA ends `PRE_TERMINATED` before the first effect (no effect; ruling R14 item 4) and `EFF_TERMINATED` after it (the first effect is the first host-changing step of the mode: a control command, a rename or archive, a pending-file write or a manager reload; ruling R15 item 3); the RA family treats `EFF_TERMINATED` as "termination (no end code)", and it never counts as the API ready (ruling R14 item 17). Both ends carry the end row from the files on disk and the API state as read, and RA never claims the API stopped; RA writes a `NOTE TERMINATED` progress line and the result says terminated. The effect flag and `effectPhase` read as in section 6.6: only the snapshot, time-budget and termination codes take their `PRE_` or `EFF_` name from the effect flag (step codes such as `EFF_PRE_ARCHIVE`, `EFF_PORT_BUSY`, `EFF_LOADED_CONFIG`, `EFF_PROGRESS_WRITE` and the `EFF_RECORD_` codes are fixed names and can occur with the flag false; ruling R25 item 5), `effectPhase` follows the record directory, and a termination between the two ends `PRE_TERMINATED` with `effectPhase` EFFECT and a made record, which the runner evaluates as a stop with an effect (ruling R24 item 3, reading 1). A step-budget or in-run-limit stop keeps case 7. **Tree check before every start** (ruling R19 item 4(a); ruling R21 item 5(a)): right before each start RA checks the tree that start uses with the lane walk (the P5a walk copied by value, with the P5a EXACT rule) against its bound digest and file count: the put-back start (cases 1 and 2, as below) and the case 5 restart (case 5, as below). A mismatch or an unreadable tree sends no start; each walk is counted in its own path sum (section 11.1: the RA put-back unwind 176 and the case 5 restart path 259 each hold a 4-second walk). **No false stop and the limit read** (ruling R19 item 4(b) with amendment R19a; ruling R21 item 5(b); ruling R22 item 2): no end code claims that the API stopped without a proved stop. While readiness of that start is not proved (the readiness poll and any check before readiness of that start is proved; ruling R23 item 3), any throw after a start command that is not followed by a proved stop ends `RA_STOP_NOT_PROVED`. The scoped rule applies to every start command of the run; the put-back start, the case 5 restart and the market-branch start are examples, and the market-branch rules of case 8 otherwise stand (ruling R23 item 4). After readiness is proved, the existing codes stand: `RA_STOP_AT_CHECK`, the case 5 and case 10 codes, the READY rule (no command at all) and the check phase. After the in-run limit or, while unwinding, the unwind limit (section 11) has passed, RA makes one read-only class read of the API, capped to end before the host deadline minus the receipt-write reserve, and records `apiAtEnd`; when that read cannot run in time, `apiAtEnd` is `UNKNOWN` and `apiAtEndWhy` is `LIMIT`, and no command follows. The receipt-write reserve is the 10-second timing margin, and the read needs more than its 5-second call time before the cap; `apiAtEndWhy` is `LIMIT` when the cap forbids the read, or when the read fails at or after the cap, and `READ` when the read fails otherwise (ruling R24 item 3, reading 4; ruling R25 item 5, audit-r9 P3-4). **Receipt fields** (ruling R19 item 4(c); the fields of section 6.6): `startSent` is run-level and never set back to false; a start that a check refused records `startRefused` (`TREE` or `TREE_UNKNOWN`; `TREE_UNKNOWN` occurs only in RA case 5) and `startRefusedAt` (`PUT_BACK`, `CASE5` or `UNWIND`); the receipt also carries `stopProved` and `marketStopProved` (absent, true or false, as section 6.6 defines them), `apiAtEnd`, `apiAtEndWhy` and `stopCommandFailed` (ruling R25 item 2; ruling R22 item 9). In RA `marketStopProved` is never written: the market-branch stop of that field is the A2 stop. `stopProved` is not recorded on `RA_API_READY`, case 5 restart included. In RA case 5, where the stop command failed and the snapshot read shows the API stopped with no job, the end is `RA_STOP_NOT_PROVED` with `stopProved` false, while the A2 counterpart records true (ruling R25 item 2; section 12).
- **Failure cases (R5a case table, by value with the RA names; end codes named by ruling R5 items 2, 3 and 5; the case 5 checks, code precedence and the one production rule by ruling R6 items 2 to 4).** Before the API is ready, a failure unwinds as follows. The unwind decides from the files on disk, never from a flag (R5a ruling D34 item 3): a rename of the live layer that is on disk counts as archived, also when the step then threw (case 1, or case 7 for a time-budget stop or a failed progress write); a rename of a pending file that is on disk followed by a throw is the row LP sub-case of case 4; an archive step with no rename on disk is case 5.
  1. When RA archived the layer itself (the rename is on disk) and the archive step, the manager reload, the loaded-configuration check or the production-identity check fails, RA renames the layer back, runs one more reload and checks the A2 configuration and the production identity; it starts the API on it only if the API was running when RA began, otherwise the API stays stopped with the re-pin layer in place (`RA_UNWOUND`, row LL-n). If the put-back reload or the A2 configuration check after it fails, RA starts nothing (`RA_UNWIND_RELOAD_FAILED`; row LL-r when the reload is not labelled `DONE`, otherwise row LL-n). If the start or readiness after the put-back fails, RA stops the API, proves the stop and starts nothing more (`RA_UNWIND_START_FAILED`, row LL-n). When a production-identity difference has been read, the put-back and its reload still run and RA then starts nothing (`RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE`, case 9; ruling R6 item 4). Right before the put-back start of cases 1 and 2, RA checks the staged tree with the lane walk (the P5a walk copied by value, with the P5a EXACT rule) against the bound digest and file count of the staged tree; a mismatch or an unreadable tree sends no start, and RA ends `RA_UNWIND_START_FAILED` with why TREE, `startRefused` TREE and `startRefusedAt` `PUT_BACK`, the API staying stopped or failed (`startSent` is run-level and keeps its value; ruling R21 item 5(c); ruling R22 item 9). The walk's worst case enters the budget table and the timing proof (section 11.1; ruling R13 items 10 and 11).
  2. When the API start or readiness fails after RA archived the layer itself and the API was running when RA began, RA stops the API, renames the layer back, reloads and starts the API on the A2 configuration (`RA_UNWOUND`, row LL-n); the put-back sub-outcomes of case 1 apply. If the API becomes failed during that stop, the put-back rename and its reload still run, so the disk and the loaded configuration agree, and no stop and no start follows; RA ends `RA_UNWOUND` with the failed API recorded, which the outcome table allows. The words "no further control call" in packet r6, ruling R6 item 3, mean no command to the API unit; the manager reload is not one (ruling R13 item 5).
  3. In every other case of a failed start or readiness, RA stops the API and leaves it stopped with the re-pin layer archived (or never written, in rows L0, LP and LQ) (`RA_API_NOT_READY`; end row LR-n, LU-n, LQ-n or L0-n); the rollback-family rule applies.
  4. When the re-pin layer was already archived or never written (rows L0, LP, LQ, LU and LR) and the reconciling reload, the loaded-configuration check or the production-identity check fails, RA changes nothing further and starts nothing, so an API that was running stays stopped (`RA_API_STOPPED`; end row LU-r or LR-r when the reconciling reload is not labelled `DONE`, otherwise L0-n, LQ-n, LU-n or LR-n). A time-budget stop or a failed progress write at the reconciling reload in rows LU-r and LR-r counts as a failed reconciling reload (`RA_API_STOPPED`, end row LU-r or LR-r unless the reload is labelled `DONE`), not case 7 (ruling R7 item 3, P3-4). With a production-identity difference this is the RD-32 state (case 9; ruling R5 item 2, S-P3-3). In row LP, when the rename of the pending file is on disk and the step then throws for a reason other than a time-budget stop or a failed progress write (for example the directory fsync or the digest re-read), RA likewise changes nothing further and starts nothing (`RA_API_STOPPED`, end row LQ-n; ruling R6 item 3; S-P3-6).
  5. When RA stops before it has changed the layer (a busy port after the proved stop, as section 6.6 defines it, a pre-archive re-check whose run has not ended after the 15-second wait, or a time-budget stop, a failed progress write or an archive failure at the stop or archive step, with no rename on disk), it starts the API again on the configuration it found, only when the API was running when RA began (`RA_UNWOUND`, end row the row RA found). Right before that restart RA checks the tree it uses with the lane walk against its bound digest and file count; a mismatch or an unreadable tree sends no start, and RA ends `RA_UNWIND_START_FAILED` with why TREE, `startRefused` TREE and `startRefusedAt` `CASE5` (ruling R19 item 4(a); ruling R21 item 5(a); ruling R22 item 9). The same ending holds for a refusal for `TREE_UNKNOWN` below. No start is open then, so RA calls no stop-if-runs check and sends no command after the refusal, and the end code stays `RA_UNWIND_START_FAILED`; the API stays as the case 5 stop left it. The row's disk reads and one capped end class read (`apiAtEnd`) still run after the refusal, as for every end (ruling R24 item 4, F4, as built and accepted by ruling R25 item 4). A time-budget stop of either prefix in the PRECHECK or FILE phases, before the rename, is case 5 and is answered here before the budget branch of case 7 (ruling R25 item 3). No budget stop can occur at the record directory or the progress file: a failure there ends with its own `EFF_RECORD_` or `EFF_PROGRESS_WRITE` code (ruling R27 item 3, P3-5). In rows L0-n, LU-n, LR-n and LQ-n the configuration found is the post-P5a configuration, so RA then runs the immediate checks, like the main path, and ends `RA_API_READY`, `RA_STOP_AT_CHECK` (case 10) or `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE` (case 9) in the row it found, and never `RA_STOP_MARKET_AFTER_READY` (ruling R6 item 2; A-P3-1; ruling R7 item 3, P3-5); in the other rows the restart ends `RA_UNWOUND`. If that start or readiness fails, RA stops the API, proves the stop and starts nothing more (`RA_UNWIND_START_FAILED`, the same row). The case 5 immediate checks run only in rows L0-n, LU-n, LR-n and LQ-n, so `RA_STOP_AT_CHECK` after a case 5 restart is possible only there; in every other row case 5 ends `RA_UNWOUND` with no immediate checks, and an unreadable row there gives `RA_UNWOUND` with `rowNone`. With an unknown loaded tree (the loaded working directory read gives neither tree) case 5 sends no start and ends `RA_UNWIND_START_FAILED` with `treeUnknown` true, `startRefused` TREE_UNKNOWN and `startRefusedAt` `CASE5` (ruling R13 item 12; ruling R21 item 5(c); ruling R22 item 9). `TREE_UNKNOWN` occurs only here.
  6. When RA cannot put an archived layer back (the archive is missing or has another hash), it ends `RA_UNWIND_FAILED` and starts nothing, also when the API was stopped at the start of RA. This is the only use of `RA_UNWIND_FAILED`; the files then form no recognised row (ruling R5 item 2; S-P2-1).
  7. A time-budget stop or a failed progress write later in the effect rows, after a file action, other than at the reconciling reload in rows LU-r and LR-r (case 4; ruling R7 item 3, P3-4), ends `RA_BUDGET_STOP` with nothing started. A `PRE_TIME_BUDGET` stop routes like `EFF_TIME_BUDGET` at the same step (ruling R25 item 3, as built; it replaces the F1 text of ruling R24 item 4). Case 5 answers before the budget branch: a budget stop in the PRECHECK or FILE phases, before the rename, ends `RA_UNWOUND` with no command (case 5); after the port check it ends `RA_BUDGET_STOP`. **No effect, no command** (fix round 6, G2; ruling R25 item 7): when the effect flag is false at a trigger in RA, no command is sent; in particular there is no stop-if-runs check in START with the flag false, also when the API reads running because an outside actor started it. The end codes with the flag false are these (ruling R26 item 3, as built; scope by ruling R27 item 2). Cases 4, 5 and 9 keep their own codes: a budget stop or a failed progress write at the reconciling reload in rows LU-r and LR-r ends `RA_API_STOPPED` (case 4); a budget stop, a failed progress write or an archive failure before the rename ends `RA_UNWOUND` (case 5); a non-budget trigger at the pre-archive check, the loaded-configuration check, the re-check or the production-identity check ends with the case 5, 4 or 9 code of that step. Outside those cases, a non-budget trigger at the start step ends `RA_API_NOT_READY`; a non-budget trigger at the start step of the market branch ends `RA_STOP_MARKET_API_NOT_READY`; any other budget or progress trigger ends `RA_BUDGET_STOP`; a termination ends with the termination code. A non-budget trigger at a start step is a failed read before its command; an argument-shape refusal of a start records `startSent` true, the safe direction, and cannot occur with the bound shapes (ruling R27 item 3, P3-5). These codes, with `startSent` false and no `stopProved`, mean the host is as the gate found it. **Reading a no-command RA end** (ruling R26 item 4): the RA receipt does not record the effect flag, so Root reads a no-command RA end from the trigger, from `startSent` false, from the absence of `stopProved` and from a progress record with no archive or reload line. One exception: a gate that read class FAILED, with the reset-failed grant, can end `RA_API_NOT_READY`, or `RA_STOP_MARKET_API_NOT_READY` in the market branch, with one benign reset-failed command sent and `startSent` false (ruling R27 item 3, P3-1). Such an end needs no unwind. The next step is Root's review of the receipt, then a fresh RA gate under its own GO; this is the RA analogue of the A2 end form in section 6.6. In row LL, after RA's own archive (the rename on disk), RA first runs the case 1 put-back and one more reload, with no start (the R5a deadline behaviour), and the end row is LL-n, or LL-r when that reload is not labelled `DONE` (case 6 when the layer cannot be put back) (ruling R6 item 3; A-P3-2). In the other rows the end row is decided from the files on disk, and a row whose facts the records do not show refuses the next RA under the need-reload gate and the lost-receipt clause (ruling R5 item 2; S-P2-1). A stop that is not proved within 100 seconds, at the first stop or in an unwind, ends `RA_STOP_NOT_PROVED`: no further control call and nothing started; the end row is decided from the files on disk. **Case 7 in START** (ruling R12 item 6; ruling R13 item 9): after the start command is sent and before readiness is proved (phase START), a step-budget or in-run-limit stop first gets one proved stop. The case 7 put-back in row LL runs only after a proved stop, and no start follows. If the stop is not proved, the end is `RA_STOP_NOT_PROVED`. When the in-run limit, not the readiness step, runs out inside the readiness poll, the core throws the budget code and takes this route, and the receipt records `startSent` and the API class. A budget stop during the first stop's proof sends no further command, and RA ends `RA_STOP_NOT_PROVED` (ruling R13 item 3). **Code precedence** (ruling R6 item 3; A-P3-2; ruling R14 item 3): `RA_STOP_NOT_PROVED` is used for exactly these: a command timeout of any stop command (the first stop, the START stop of ruling R12 item 6, or the stop before the case 2 put-back; ruling R15 item 6), a stop not proved within its wait (100 seconds), an error in the proof wait (ruling R13 item 3), a failed stop command whose snapshot shows neither of the two no-job states (the API running with no job and the gate's main process, or the API failed with no job), and a throw after a start command, while readiness of that start is not proved, that is not followed by a proved stop (the scoped throw rule of Effects above; amendment R19a; ruling R23 items 4 and 6). An API that becomes failed keeps the code of the case in which that happens, with no further control call, and the receipt records "API failed"; at the first stop, before any file action, that is case 5 (`RA_UNWOUND`, the row RA found, nothing started).
  8. **Market change at the post-file-action re-check** (A2 rule, O-7; retitled by ruling R5 item 5, because the re-check also runs in rows without a reload): if the market re-check finds a market unit changed from RA's own gate reading, RA does not unwind and runs no extra reload: it gives the market units no command, starts the API on `ba1a433`, runs the immediate checks and ends `RA_STOP_MARKET_AFTER_READY`; if an immediate check then fails, the end stays `RA_STOP_AT_CHECK` (case 10) with `marketChanged` true (ruling R9 item 10f). Root reports; the owner decides on GO-RC or GO-RM. If, in this branch, the API start or readiness fails (also a throw in the start or readiness poll after a market change, with no put-back and no extra reload; ruling R9 item 2), RA runs no unwind and no extra reload and gives the market units no command; it stops the API if it runs and proves the stop (100 seconds, no grace), starts nothing, and ends `RA_STOP_MARKET_API_NOT_READY` with the API stopped (or failed) on the post-P5a configuration, in any row (the row that RA's file action left, for example LR-n from row LL; ruling R5 item 5, S-P3-4) (ruling R3 item 6; S-P3-5). This stop is the one stop of the market allowance, and a reset-failed before the branch's start is its one reset-failed (ruling R9a). When the start leaves the API failed with an empty job queue, RA sends no stop and the failed state counts as stopped (packet r6, ruling R6 item 3; a failed API with a queued start job is not stopped, ruling R25 item 7, G1); the receipt records the failed state (ruling R13 item 8). An unreadable stream at the market re-check is a failure (`EFF_STREAM_UNREADABLE`), not a market change (ruling R12 item 15; ruling R22 item 4: `PRE_UNIT_SNAPSHOT` is the code of the RA gate before any effect only; ruling R25 item 5, audit-r9 P3-1: `STREAM_UNREADABLE` without a prefix is only the A2 immediate-check token). Root reports; the owner decides, and the rollback-family rule applies.
  9. **RD-32 state** (ruling R5 item 2, S-P3-3; one rule by ruling R6 item 4, A-P3-4, S-P3-4): a production-identity difference read at any RA step after the gate means RA starts nothing from that point and ends `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE`, which takes the place of every other end code; in row LL after RA's own archive, the case 1 put-back and its reload still run, with no start. A difference read at the RA gate ends `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE` before any effect. After it, the next RA is admitted only with Root's recorded review of the difference in its manifest, which also sets the production baseline that RA compares against. A difference read at the immediate checks leaves the API running and ready on `ba1a433`; Root may accept that receipt after its review (the terminal sentence above).
  10. **Immediate-check failure after readiness** (ruling R5 item 3; S-P3-5): if an immediate check other than the production identity fails, throws, or hits a step-budget or in-run-limit stop after the API is ready on `ba1a433` (also after the case 5 restart; ruling R6 item 2), RA gives no command to any unit, runs no unwind and no reload, leaves the API running and ends `RA_STOP_AT_CHECK`, also after a market change, with `marketChanged` true (ruling R9 items 2 and 10f); Root reviews it.

  At most two reloads. Root reports every case that leaves the API stopped, and the owner decides. The rollback-family rule above decides the next step after every outcome (ruling R4 item 1; ruling R5 item 2).

  **RA end outcomes** (ruling R5 items 2 and 3; ruling R6 items 2 to 5). Every outcome names its end row; the next step follows the rollback-family rule above. With all immediate checks passed, RA ends `RA_API_READY`. The RA receipt carries `marketChanged` in every end (ruling R9 item 10f).

  | Outcome | Case | End row | API at the end | Next step |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | `RA_API_READY` | none (immediate checks passed), or 5 in rows L0-n, LU-n, LR-n and LQ-n | L0-n, LU-n, LR-n or LQ-n | ready on `ba1a433` | terminal; the accepted RA receipt after Root accepts it |
  | `RA_STOP_MARKET_AFTER_READY` | 8 (never case 5) | L0-n, LU-n, LR-n or LQ-n | ready on `ba1a433` | terminal; Root review first; the owner decides on GO-RC or GO-RM |
  | `RA_STOP_AT_CHECK` | 10 (a failed check, a throw, a step-budget stop or an in-run-limit stop at a check), also after case 5 and after a market change | L0-n, LU-n, LR-n or LQ-n | ready on `ba1a433` | terminal; Root review first |
  | `RA_UNWOUND` | 1, 2, 5 | LL-n (cases 1 and 2) or the row RA found (case 5: a restart in rows other than L0-n, LU-n, LR-n and LQ-n, no restart because the API was not running at RA's start, or a failed API at the first stop, or a failed stop command whose snapshot shows the API running with no job and the gate's main process or failed with no job; a budget stop of either prefix before the rename, which case 5 answers first (no command when the effect flag is false; ruling R25 item 3); case 2 with a failed API, recorded) | running on the configuration found or put back when it was running at RA's start, otherwise stopped, or failed (recorded) | next RA |
  | `RA_UNWIND_RELOAD_FAILED` | 1, 2 | LL-r, or LL-n when the reload is labelled `DONE` | stopped | next RA |
  | `RA_UNWIND_START_FAILED` | 1, 2, 5 (also why TREE at the put-back tree check and at the case 5 restart tree check, and `treeUnknown` in case 5; `startRefused` is `TREE`, or `TREE_UNKNOWN` in case 5 only, and `startRefusedAt` names the start refused, `PUT_BACK` or `CASE5`) | LL-n, or the row RA found (case 5) | stopped or failed | next RA |
  | `RA_API_NOT_READY` | 3; also a non-budget trigger at the start step with the effect flag false (G2: no command; ruling R26 item 3) | LR-n, LU-n, LQ-n or L0-n | stopped or failed; as the gate found it when `startSent` is false and no `stopProved` is recorded | next RA; after a no-command end Root reviews the receipt and a fresh RA gate runs under its own GO, no unwind (ruling R26 item 4) |
  | `RA_API_STOPPED` | 4, also row LP with the rename on disk followed by a throw, and a time-budget stop or failed progress write at the reconciling reload in rows LU-r and LR-r | LU-r or LR-r, otherwise L0-n, LQ-n, LU-n or LR-n; LQ-n after row LP | stopped | next RA; a repeated case 4 in an -n row with no progress: Root rules |
  | `RA_BUDGET_STOP` | 7, with a `PRE_` or an `EFF_` budget-stop code alike, after the port check; before the rename case 5 answers first and the end is `RA_UNWOUND` (ruling R25 item 3); with the effect flag false no command is sent (G2) | LL-n or LL-r after the put-back in row LL; otherwise from the files on disk | stopped or failed | next RA when the records show the row's facts |
  | `RA_STOP_NOT_PROVED` | 7, also the first stop (a command timeout, an error in the proof wait other than a termination, or a failed stop command whose snapshot read shows neither the API running with no job and the gate's main process nor the API failed with no job) and a stop in START or the stop before the case 2 put-back, and any throw after a start command, while readiness of that start is not proved, that is not followed by a proved stop (ruling R19 item 4(b), scoped by amendment R19a; after readiness is proved the check codes `RA_STOP_AT_CHECK` and the case 5 and case 10 codes stand) | the row RA found, or the row its file action left | not proved stopped; after the run limit `apiAtEnd` may be `UNKNOWN` (LIMIT) | next RA (the gate classifies the API) |
  | `RA_STOP_MARKET_API_NOT_READY` | 8; also a non-budget trigger at the start step of the market branch with the effect flag false (G2: no command; ruling R26 item 3) | any row | stopped or failed; as the gate found it when `startSent` is false and no `stopProved` is recorded | next RA; after a no-command end Root reviews the receipt and a fresh RA gate runs under its own GO, no unwind (ruling R26 item 4) |
  | `RA_UNWIND_FAILED` | 6 | no recognised row | stopped | excluded; Root rules by a packet revision |
  | `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE` | 9, at any step, also at the gate (before any effect) and at the immediate checks after the case 5 restart | any | any; ready on `ba1a433` after a difference at the immediate checks | next RA only with Root's recorded review in its manifest, which sets its production baseline; with the API ready on `ba1a433`, Root may accept the receipt after its review |
  | termination before the first effect (`PRE_TERMINATED`; no end code) | the RA gate (no command, no effect; a `NOTE TERMINATED` line; the result says terminated) | the row RA found, from the files on disk | as read | Root review; the next RA follows after the lost-receipt wait of 1335 seconds from that attempt's start; never counts as an end with the API ready (ruling R15 item 2) |
  | termination after the first effect (`EFF_TERMINATED`; no end code) | any later phase (no command: no stop, no put-back, no start; a `NOTE TERMINATED` line; the result says terminated) | from the files on disk | as read; never claimed stopped | next RA after the lost-receipt wait of 1335 seconds; never counts as an end with the API ready, also after readiness |
- **Bounds.** API gap bound 380 seconds; host bound 1200 seconds (section 11.1); from the stop command RA needs at least 478 seconds remaining, measured against the in-run limit of 1140 seconds (the host bound 1200 minus 60), otherwise it stops before any effect (ruling R6 item 12; ruling R7 item 3, P3-3). While unwinding, RA uses the unwind limit of section 11, as A2 does (ruling R22 item 9). The 100 seconds of a stop after a failed unwind start are host time, not API gap, and the gap bound stays 380.
- **Data.** None. QR-2 audit rows written by panel use stay, and `ba1a433` reads them.
- **Record directory.** One record directory with a progress record and a result file in the dedicated protected backup directory, and the shell's temporary here-document file that carries the tool. The worst-case RA receipt body (64 inventory entries, every progress line and the maximum prior records) fits the receipt frame limit, which a test asserts (ruling R9 item 9).

## 9. Rollback, the RX rule and the full-rollback order

| Step | In-run rollback | Later rollback | GO | Data |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| A0 | none | none | none | none |
| A1 | none (inert; a partial stage is left inert and reported) | none | inside GO-A1 | none |
| A2 before readiness | Unwind (section 6.6) | RA; A2 again only under a packet revision or Root ruling that defines the next A2 job ID (ruling R5 item 3); after `A2_UNWIND_FAILED`, RA only when the files form a recognised row and production equals the baseline, otherwise Root rules | inside GO-A2 | none |
| A2 after readiness | none | RA; A2 again only under a packet revision or Root ruling that defines the next A2 job ID (ruling R5 item 3) | GO-RA | QR-2 audit rows stay |
| A2M | none (read only) | none | inside GO-A2 | none |
| AR | none (read only) | none | GO-AR | none |
| RA | Unwind (section 8) | A2 again only under a packet revision or Root ruling that defines the next A2 job ID (ruling R4 item 11); the next RA attempt under the rollback-family rule of section 8 and a new GO-RA, as the RA outcome paragraph below states (ruling R4 item 1; ruling R5 items 2 and 3) | inside GO-RA | none |
| R5a, R4, R4H, RX while the re-pin layer is live | Not requested or admitted | GO-RA first, then the chain below | as below | as those packets |

**Why GO-RA comes first.** R5a, R4 and R4H refuse at their gates, before any effect, on a drop-in list that differs from their expected list, and they require the market units inactive. RX classifies only the P4 and P5a layers. With an extra live layer, RX would stop every running B2 writer (the market stream, the collector timer and service, the API and the trading worker), archive the P4 and P5a layers and reload, then fail its loaded-configuration gate and end `STOPPED_BEFORE_SWAP` with nothing restarted. So, while the re-pin layer is live, Root requests and admits no GO-RX (ruling R1 item 7). This is a Root rule, not a mechanical refusal. Root records it in the A2 receipt review, the handoff memory and every later GO-RX request ("RA receipt first").

**RA is admissible in every market-unit class** (ruling R2, P1-1). GO-RC or GO-RM before GO-RA is admissible: RA classifies the market units and the trading worker, records their class, and gives them no command in every class (section 8).

**RX tripwire** (ruling R2, P3-8). While the re-pin layer is live, Root writes a consumed marker line for the next RX job ID (P4 RQ-17 form), so a mistaken RX dispatch stops at its consume step before any effect. Root issues a fresh RX job ID only after an accepted RA receipt, or a Root-reviewed A2 receipt that reopens the chain (below; ruling R5 item 3). This does not replace the later RX gate hardening.

**Full-rollback order after A2.** GO-RA first; GO-RC or GO-RM may come before it. Then the existing chain:
- to turn the panel off or move the pair back: GO-RM (R5a, R4 and R4H need all three market units inactive), then GO-R5a, then GO-R4 or GO-R4H;
- or a full restore: GO-RX, with GO-RC or GO-RM first only when a market unit is failed.

**RA outcomes, exclusion and the accepted RA receipt** (ruling R5 items 2 and 3; ruling R6 items 2, 4 and 5; the words of section 8). RA outcomes: `RA_API_READY`, `RA_STOP_MARKET_AFTER_READY` and `RA_STOP_AT_CHECK` end with the API ready on `ba1a433`; `RA_UNWOUND`, `RA_UNWIND_RELOAD_FAILED`, `RA_UNWIND_START_FAILED`, `RA_API_NOT_READY`, `RA_API_STOPPED`, `RA_BUDGET_STOP`, `RA_STOP_NOT_PROVED` and `RA_STOP_MARKET_API_NOT_READY` end in a recognised layer state without the API proved ready on `ba1a433` by RA's immediate checks; `RA_UNWIND_FAILED` and `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE` are excluded. After an RA attempt that did not end with the API ready on `ba1a433`, also after a termination, which never counts as an end with the API ready and is followed by the lost-receipt wait of 1335 seconds, Root may request the next RA attempt under a new GO-RA that lists the prior attempts; the tool admits it only when the files on disk form a recognised layer state, the prior records show the facts that state needs, and production equals the baseline. Excluded: after `RA_UNWIND_FAILED` (an archived layer could not be put back) Root rules by a packet revision before any next RA; after `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE` (a production-identity difference read at any RA step, at the gate before any effect, the RD-32 state) no next RA is admitted without Root's recorded review of the difference in its manifest, which also sets the production baseline that RA compares against. An RA that ends with the API ready on `ba1a433`, other than with `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE`, is terminal for the RA family, and no next RA follows it; its receipt is the accepted RA receipt, for the RX tripwire and the return of R5a, R4, R4H, RM and RC, when its end row is L0-n, LU-n, LR-n or LQ-n, the post-P5a configuration is loaded, production is unchanged and the API is ready, after Root accepts it, with a Root review first for a market or check stop; a `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE` receipt with the API ready on `ba1a433` in such an end row may be accepted by Root in the same way after its review of the difference. A Root-reviewed A2 receipt with end row L0-n or LU-n, no pending file, the API ready on `ba1a433`, the post-P5a configuration loaded and production unchanged reopens that chain in the same way, with no RA.

After an accepted RA receipt, or such an A2 receipt, R5a, R4, R4H, RM and RC are valid again with their own rules; RX needs a fresh job ID and manifest (tripwire above); the families retired in section 10 stay retired (ruling R3 item 6; S-P3-7).

**Restore source** (ruling R2 O-10). The full-restore source is the P4 pre-switch backup taken at P4 attempt x4 a02 (2026-10-26T04:49:30Z). It is not refreshed by this packet (ruling R1 item 9). The GO-A2 request shows its UTC time and age. A full restore loses every write since it, including Paper rows, panel audit rows and market bars.

**No RA(r8) after this packet's A2** (Root ruling RP1, Q-B). This packet's RA returns the staging API to `ba1a433` with the post-P5a configuration, the same state as RA(r8). No rebuilt RA of revision 8 is needed, and the revision 8 RA family is terminal after its accepted receipt (section 4A). Root writes the new RX tripwire marker of this packet's layer before the GO-A2 request (section 6.1 item 12; second opinion d1 P3-2).

**Later item.** Harden the RX gate to refuse on any unknown live drop-in before its first effect (a separate packet).

## 10. Retirement inventory (before GO-RA(r8); revision 8 marker lines after its acceptance)

Rule (main packet P4 step 1; ruling D24; P4 RQ-17 form): Root writes one consumed marker line per family namespace for any open contingency job ID, and pins a new inventory record in the A2, A2M and RA manifests and in every AR manifest built after it. The A2, A2M, RA and AR manifests also pin the U-4 record (below). No AR manifest is built before the inventory record exists; when an AR is needed before GO-A2, Root writes the inventory first, which this section allows at any time before GO-A2 (ruling R17 item 1). Reason: these families' gates bind the pre-repin API layout and are not needed after RA.

**Retire** (open contingency IDs): the revision 8 families S6-STAGING-API-REPIN-1 A1, A2, A2M, AR and RA, by marker lines that Root writes after it accepts the RA(r8) receipt (section 4A). Root identifies the revision 8 A2, A2M, RA and AR markers, and those of this packet, by folder plus body `jobFamily`, never by name, because the A2RA marker name is not family-specific (Root ruling RP4, P3-5; audit d2 P2-1). This packet's A1 consumed marker has the form `S6_STAGING_API_REPIN_2_A1_GO_CONSUMED_aNN.json` (`FAMILY_TOKEN` is `JOB_FAMILY` with "-" replaced by "_"); the revision 8 A1 marker keeps the form `S6_STAGING_API_REPIN_A1_GO_CONSUMED_aNN.json` (Root ruling RP4, P2-2). The families that revision 8 retired (S6 P0a, P0b, P0c, P1, P3, P4-X4, P4-R6 and P4-R24, P5A-ON, P4M m1 and m6, collector diag d1, collector c1 and c6) and the families retired by ruling D24 (S07, R7B, DP1S, R3) stay retired by reference; Root confirms from the revision 8 inventory record that no open ID of them remains.

**Keep admissible:** R4, R4H and R5a (they refuse before any effect while the re-pin layer is live); RX (under the rule and the tripwire of section 9); RM and RC (the market-unit stop paths); the families of this packet, including AR, which takes over the c6-form reads after the collector c1 and c6 families are retired (ruling R3 item 1; S-P2-5).

**Inventory (ruling R2 O-5).** Root lists the actual open contingency IDs from the local execution records and writes and pins the inventory record before GO-RA(r8), so that every manifest of this packet can pin it. The record names the revision 8 families with their marker step deferred to the RA(r8) acceptance; Root writes those marker lines after that acceptance and before GO-AR a01, and never before that acceptance: a revision 8 RA-family consumed file written earlier would make RA(r8) refuse `ADMISSION_SCAN` (second opinion d1). They are a local record step with no host effect (Root ruling RP2, E1). The pinned record does not change. The record names this packet's A1 marker form `S6_STAGING_API_REPIN_2_A1_GO_CONSUMED_aNN.json` (Root ruling RP4, P2-2).

**U-4 precondition of GO-A2 (blocker; ruling R2 O-4).** Before GO-A2 Root confirms, from the accepted RM and RC snapshots and not the build sources: (a) that their gates compare no pair digest and no configuration digest of the staging API; (b) whether their GO-record check pins the GO wording. Both GO lines name "pinned revision ba1a433" for the pair. Root records both results. If (a) fails, GO-A2 is not requested until a packet revision. If (b) shows a pinned wording, Root records before GO-A2 how a later GO-RM or GO-RC names both revisions.

**U-4 result** (Root, 28 October 2026; record sha256 `d01105a2119b320f26a9e1b0f504be3865ae38ff036747e95b68adc886b92e93`). Basis: a read-only check of the two accepted snapshots by the architecture auditor (Opus 5.5 / high), and Root's own read of its prep scripts. RM: the build snapshot accepted by ruling D42 (tool hash `7b7ec494`; Root's SHA256SUMS `de3351fc`, 66 of 66 OK). RC: the build snapshot accepted by ruling D51 (tool hash `af4efe23`; Root's SHA256SUMS `ce953a6c`, 75 of 75 OK).
- **(a) PASS for both RM and RC.** Neither gate compares a pair digest or a configuration digest of the staging API. RM reads the API only through state rules (host floors, a pending-job refusal and a need-reload refusal), and its effects are stop and reset-failed on the three market units only. RC does the same for the two collector units; it binds the m1 a03 facts but only shape-checks them, and uses only the stream digest and the restart count. No host region of either tool calls `gPair`, `pairFacts`, `SHOW_TARGET` or the API health URL. U-4 (a) does not block GO-A2.
- **(b) Not pinned in the tools; pinned by the packet templates and Root's GO records.** Tool checks: `ownerGoReference` is any string of 1 to 255 characters and is never compared with GO text; `manifest.pin` and the host binding pin must equal the tool constant `ba1a433`; the runners require the follow-up packet `ebc707c8` (RM) or the collector packet `a296989e` (RC), plus the main packet `e8a18a90`, byte-equal under `docs/`. Root's prep scripts check the GO record's `interpretation.pin` prefix against the tool pin `ba1a433`.
- **Rule for a later GO-RM or GO-RC while the re-pin layer is live.** (1) Keep every template clause from "in one SSH session" onward. (2) Name both revisions in the GO-A2 style: the RM or RC tool pinned to revision `ba1a433`, "while staging runs the mixed-revision pair of packet S6-STAGING-API-REPIN-2 A2 job [ref]: API pin [pin] (75c7a5b336ca6653c0e748cf2b88e1e0ff3b4ac5) (... D: yes; includes the QR-2 boundary record: yes) on the staging API, and trading worker revision ba1a433 (includes QR-2 O7: yes), neither of which this step stops, restarts or changes; reversing [P4M | c1] job [ref]: ...". (3) In the GO record keep `interpretation.pin` as `ba1a433` and add `apiPin` with the first eight hex characters of P2 and the A2 job reference; `manifest.pin` stays `ba1a433`. (4) After an accepted RA receipt, the original templates are true again.
- **Retirement-inventory token rule.** Never write an RM or RC family token into a `manifest*.json` or `GO_CONSUMED*.json` file, and never mark RM or RC consumed. The local record directories of this packet stay inside the glob `s6-staging-api-repin-*` (Root ruling RP1, Q-G) and use the admission skip prefixes (Root ruling RP3; audit d1 P2-7): build directories `s6-staging-api-repin-build-a1-...`, `s6-staging-api-repin-build-a2ra-...` and `s6-staging-api-repin-build-adp-...` (a REPIN-2 marker after the prefix), review directories `s6-staging-api-repin-review-...`, and execution directories `s6-staging-api-repin-exec-...`. A1 skips only `s6-staging-api-repin-build-a1-`, and A2RA skips `s6-staging-api-repin-build-` and `s6-staging-api-repin-review-`; a folder named `s6-staging-api-repin-2-...` is not skipped. So REPIN-2 build fixtures and rehearsal manifests that carry a REPIN-2 family token sit only under those prefixes, and any file of this packet's A1 family (a fixture, a rehearsal marker or a review copy) sits only under `s6-staging-api-repin-build-a1-...`. Until RA(r8) has run, no file named `manifest*.json` or `*GO_CONSUMED*.json` under `.qa-local` outside the skipped directories may carry the revision 8 RA family token (`"jobFamily":"S6-STAGING-API-REPIN-1-RA"`), including the retirement inventory record of this packet and any leftover dry-admission file; such a file makes RA(r8) refuse `ADMISSION_SCAN` before its marker, and the refusal spends the 120-second GO window (second opinion d1 P3-1). The RM and RC admission scans see these directories, at about 16100 and 14400 entries on 28 October 2026 against a cap of 100000. The A1 and A2RA scans (depth 6) refuse `ADMISSION_SCAN` above their limit: about 44,000 entries under `.qa-local` at 2026-10-30, limit 100,000; Root recounts before every admission; test clusters must delete their data dirs (Root ruling RP5, Q4). The design and review folders `s6-repin2-design-2026-10-30` and `s6-repin2-review-2026-10-30` sit under the scanned root in no skip list, so they never hold a `manifest*.json` or `*GO_CONSUMED*.json` file; Root checks this before every admission (Root ruling RP6; audit d2 P3-5).
- **U-4 carry for this packet.** The revision 8 U-4 result carries by reference while the accepted RM and RC snapshots are unchanged since 28 October 2026. Root records that check (the snapshot sums) before GO-A2 of this packet; if a snapshot changed, Root repeats the U-4 check. After the accepted RA(r8) receipt the original GO-RM and GO-RC templates are true again until this packet's layer becomes live; then the rule above applies with this packet's names.

## 11. Durations and bounds

- **RA(r8) (section 4A):** revision 8 RA bounds by value: about 3 to 5 minutes, plus the timer-window wait (at most 420 seconds) and the settle wait (at most 30 seconds). Host bound 1200 seconds. API gap expected 4 to 13 seconds, bound 380 seconds.
- **A0:** local; no host time.
- **A1:** under 1 minute of job time (P1 attempt a02 took about 14 seconds for two trees). Host bound 300 seconds, carried by value from the accepted P1 revision 2 limits.
- **A2:** about 6 to 8 minutes including the delayed check, plus the timer-window wait when needed (at most 420 seconds) and the settle wait when needed (at most 30 seconds). Host bound 1200 seconds. API gap expected 4 to 13 seconds, bound 310 seconds (covers a failed switch at readiness followed by an unwind whose stop needs the read-only wait, with the pre-write check and wait and the market re-check inside the gap). No dump time is added.
- **A2M:** under 1 minute. Host bound 240 seconds. No gap.
- **AR (a01 before GO-A1; after a drift; or before GO-A2 under the 6-hour rule):** under 1 minute, plus the settle wait when needed (at most 30 seconds). Host bound 240 seconds. No gap.
- **RA:** about 3 to 5 minutes, plus the timer-window wait when the timer is active (at most 420 seconds) and the settle wait when needed (at most 30 seconds). Host bound 1200 seconds. API gap expected 4 to 13 seconds, bound 380 seconds.
- **Lost-receipt wait:** 1335 seconds for every family of this packet.
- **Unwind limit** (ruling R19a; ruling R22 item 9): the host bound minus 10 seconds (1190 seconds for the 1200-second bound of A2 and RA), or the host deadline when that is earlier. A2 and RA use it while unwinding. This definition changes no section 11.1 number.
- **Tool payload:** the shell's temporary here-document file that carries the tool has a ceiling of 262144 bytes in each of the four modes (A2, A2M, RA and AR); the build asserts the size with a worst-case manifest, and before each GO request Root runs the payload-size check with the real manifest (ruling R9 item 11a; ruling R10 item 3(i)).
- **Heartbeat interval:** a manifest input from 50 to 5000 ms (ruling R9 item 11b; section 8).
- **Owner browser steps after A2:** about 30 minutes of owner time (Bots 2 and 3 and the Main Bot; Week, Month and All Time; English and Thai), at the owner's convenience, while the port forwarding is up, and before the GO-A2 `notAfter` so that A2M can run under it.
- No gap for the trading worker, the market stream or the collector.

### 11.1 Author check (ruling R2 O-6)

All values in seconds. Values carried by value name their source; new values are marked new. The builds assert the same inequalities over their limits and may tighten a value, never loosen it. No wait is shortened.

**Final sums (revision 8 values, carried by value; ruling R24 item 3, which accepts the fix round 4 sums; the audit of that round checked them by hand).** Each sum shows its terms as the build report does. Fix round 5 (ruling R24) changes no sum, constant, bound, limit or wait.
- A2 normal gap 133 and A2 window 168: unchanged.
- A2 unwind gap: 30 + 70 + 2 + 15 + 10 + 4 + 40 + 5 = 176.
- A2 worst gap (a failed start, then a full unwind): 133 + 176 = 309 of 310 (margin 1).
- A2 reserve: 133 + 265 + 40 = 438. A2 worst in-run path: 240 + 30 + 1 + 420 + 438 = 1129 of 1140.
- A2 stop-grace path 30 + 70 + 4 + 40 + 5 = 149 and A2 pre-write abort path 30 + 5 + 15 + 4 + 40 + 5 = 99: each ends in the walked unwind start and so includes the 4-second walk (draft 10; ruling R25 item 7, G5; they were 145 and 95). No bound, reserve or worst sum changes, and `timingProof` stays ok: its `a2InRun` reads 1129 (the A2 worst in-run path) and the A2 gap sum reads 309, both as before (ruling R26 item 7).
- RA normal path 202 and RA window 167: unchanged.
- RA put-back unwind: 100 + 2 + 15 + 10 + 4 + 40 + 5 = 176.
- RA case 5 restart path: 100 + 5 + 15 + 4 + 40 + 5 + 90 = 259, under the normal plus put-back plus stop path (202 + 176 + 100 = 478).
- RA worst gap: 202 + 176 = 378 of 380 (margin 2).
- RA worst in-run path: 206 + 30 + 1 + 420 + 202 + (176 + 100) = 1135 of 1140.
- RA reserve from the stop command: 202 + 176 + 100 = 478. RA gate step allowance: 206 (rows of the gate: 81.25 of adapter time, 85.25 with the walk, of 176 after the settle wait). A2 gate step allowance: 240 (rows 76 and 80 with the walk, of 210).
- Walk rows of 4 seconds each: the A2 gate and the RA gate (inside the gate steps), the RA put-back, the RA case 5 restart and the A2 unwind start (each in its own path sum).
- Unchanged: host bound 1200, in-run limit 1140, unwind limit 1190, lost-receipt wait 1335.
- The walk cap (4 seconds) and the IMMEDIATE cap (50 seconds) have no host measurement; both stay residuals of section 12.

**Reference formulas from the accepted P5a tool.** P5a switch: normal path 30 + 3 + 15 + 10 + 40 + 5 = 103 (stop, layer write, reload, loaded gate, readiness, overhead); unwind 30 + 70 + 2 + 15 + 10 + 40 + 5 = 172 (stop, grace, archive, reload, loaded gate, readiness, overhead); 103 + 172 = 275 ≤ 280. R5a: normal path 100 + 2 + 15 + 10 + 40 + 5 = 172; unwind 172; 172 + 172 = 344 ≤ 345.

**A1** (accepted P1 revision 2 limits, by value). Remote timeout (host bound) 300; kill-after 10; connect 5; local cutoff 330; termination wait 5; overall 360; stage deadline 220; receive deadline 90; effect budget 120; `node --check` call 10 and total 60.
- 220 + 10 + 5 = 235 ≤ 300 − 60 = 240.
- 90 + 120 = 210 ≤ 220; 60 < 120.
- 300 + 10 + 5 = 315 ≤ 330; 330 + 5 = 335 ≤ 360.
- The added drop-in read, the six-unit state read and the c1-form process reads of three units (the reference export, section 5.1) count against the same stage deadline. A1 has no settle wait (section 5.1), so no A1 value changes.
- Lost-receipt wait 1335 ≥ host-process bound 300 + 10 = 310.

**A2 step budgets** (P5a switch limits by value, plus named additions): fresh gate 240 (P5a gate 150, plus 30 for the startup probe and the staged-tree check, which is the P4 x4 gate of 180 minus the P5a gate, plus 60 for the market-unit gate, the hooks-and-links reads and the layer-name derivation, which is the collector c6 gate); settle wait 30 (new in revision 3, at most once per gate); record 1; timer-window wait 420 (new); stop 30; grace 70; pre-write check 5 (new); pre-write wait 15 (new); layer write 3; archive 2; reload 15; loaded-configuration gate with production identity 10; market re-check 10 (new); readiness 40; gap overhead 5; delayed check starts 265 after readiness; delayed check 40 (P5a 30, plus 10 for the market and memory reads).

**A2 gap.**
- Normal switch path: 30 + 5 + 15 + 3 + 15 + 10 + 10 + 40 + 5 = 133.
- Unwind path (adds the 4-second walk of the old tree before the unwind start; final sums, ruling R24 item 3): 30 + 70 + 2 + 15 + 10 + 4 + 40 + 5 = 176 (172 before the follow-up round).
- Worst gap (the start fails at the end of readiness, then a full unwind whose stop needs the grace): 133 + 176 = 309. This exceeds the P5a bound of 280, so the stated A2 bound is raised to 310 (1 of margin; 305 with 5 of margin before the follow-up round).
- Stop-grace path (ends in the walked unwind start, so it holds the 4-second walk; ruling R25 item 7, G5): 30 + 70 + 4 + 40 + 5 = 149 ≤ 310 (145 before).
- Pre-write abort path (run not ended, no layer written, start on the unchanged configuration after the walk; ruling R25 item 7, G5): 30 + 5 + 15 + 4 + 40 + 5 = 99 ≤ 310 (95 before).
- Market branch, API not ready (`A2_STOP_MARKET_API_NOT_READY`, revision 3): the switch path to the end of readiness 133, then the stop 30, the read-only wait 70 and overhead 5: 133 + 30 + 70 + 5 = 238. The API stays stopped for the owner's decision, so this is host time, not a gap that ends; 238 ≤ 438, the from-stop reserve below.

**A2 switch window W.** 30 + 70 + 5 + 15 + 3 + 15 + 10 + 10 = 158, plus margin 10, W = 168. W + accuracy 1 + randomized delay 0 = 169 < recorded period 300 (131 spare).

**Timer-window wait bound.** Root's criterion: the bound exceeds one collector period plus accuracy, delay and margin: 420 ≥ 300 + 1 + 0 + 119. Expected worst wait under the rule: a next elapse at the window edge (169), the run (under 1 at c1; 624 ms), one poll (1): about 171 ≤ 420. A collector run still in progress at the bound stops A2 before any effect (`A2_TIMER_WINDOW`); with a fire at the window edge, that happens for a run longer than 420 − 169 = 251 (the collector start tool's run bound is 300).

**A2 host bound.** Worst in-run path: 240 + 30 + 1 + 420 + max(133 + 265 + 40 = 438; 309; 238; 149) = 1129 (revision 2: 1099 without the settle wait; the fourth term was 145 before the walk of the stop-grace path, and the maximum is unchanged). Host bound 1200 (P4 x4 limits by value: kill-after 30, connect 5, local cutoff 1270, termination wait 5, overall 1300).
- 1129 ≤ 1200 − 60 = 1140 (11 spare).
- 1200 + 30 + 5 = 1235 ≤ 1270; 1270 + 5 = 1275 ≤ 1300.
- From the stop command the tool needs at least 438 remaining, otherwise a pre-effect stop.
- Lost-receipt wait 1335 ≥ host-process bound 1200 + 30 = 1230.

**A2M** (P4 CHECK limits by value). Remote timeout (host bound) 240; kill-after 10; connect 5; local cutoff 260; termination wait 5; overall 280; gate 90; reads 30.
- 90 + 1 + 30 = 121 ≤ 240 − 60 = 180.
- 240 + 10 + 5 = 255 ≤ 260; 260 + 5 = 265 ≤ 280.
- Lost-receipt wait 1335 ≥ 240 + 10 = 250.

**AR** (revision 3; P4 CHECK limits by value, as A2M). Remote timeout (host bound) 240; kill-after 10; connect 5; local cutoff 260; termination wait 5; overall 280; gate 90 (covers the c6-form gate reads, which are 60 in the collector c6 gate); settle wait 30 (new); record 1; reads 30.
- 90 + 30 + 1 + 30 = 151 ≤ 240 − 60 = 180 (29 spare).
- 240 + 10 + 5 = 255 ≤ 260; 260 + 5 = 265 ≤ 280.
- Lost-receipt wait 1335 ≥ 240 + 10 = 250.

**RA step budgets** (R5a limits by value, plus named additions): fresh gate 206 (210 through draft 8: R5a gate 150, plus 60 for the classification gate and the hooks-and-links reads, which is the collector c6 gate; the layer-state row, the lost-receipt reads of the A2 progress record and the prior-attempt reads count against it, as R5a reads the P5a record and its prior attempt inside its gate of 150; the follow-up round lowers the allowance by 4, to 206, so that the 4-second walk added to the put-back path keeps the worst in-run path at 1135: the rows of the gate are 81.25 of adapter time and 85.25 with the walk, of 176 after the settle wait; ruling R24 item 3, reading 5); settle wait 30 (new in revision 3); record 1; timer-window wait 420 (new; only when the timer is active); stop 100 (no grace); pre-archive check 5 (new); pre-archive wait 15 (new); archive 2; reload 15; loaded-configuration gate with production identity 10; market re-check 10 (new); readiness 40; gap overhead 5; immediate checks 90.

**RA gap.**
- Normal path: 100 + 5 + 15 + 2 + 15 + 10 + 10 + 40 + 5 = 202.
- Put-back unwind (adds the 4-second walk of the new tree before the put-back start; final sums, ruling R24 item 3): 100 + 2 + 15 + 10 + 4 + 40 + 5 = 176 (172 before the follow-up round).
- Case 5 restart path (stop, pre-archive check, walk, start, immediate checks; a new row): 100 + 5 + 15 + 4 + 40 + 5 + 90 = 259, under the normal plus put-back plus stop path (202 + 176 + 100 = 478, below).
- Worst gap: 202 + 176 = 378. This exceeds the R5a bound of 345, so the stated RA bound is raised to 380 (2 of margin; 374 with 6 of margin before the follow-up round).
- Layer-state rows (revision 3): the pending-file archive uses the archive budget 2 and the reconciling reload the reload budget 15; rows other than LL have no put-back, so their paths are at most the normal path 202. The normal path of row LL stays the longest.
- Market branch, API not ready (`RA_STOP_MARKET_API_NOT_READY`, revision 3): 202 + 100 + 5 = 307. The API stays stopped for the owner's decision, so this is host time; 307 ≤ 202 + max(90; 176) = 378.

**RA window W_RA.** 100 + 5 + 15 + 2 + 15 + 10 + 10 = 157, plus margin 10, W_RA = 167. W_RA + 1 + 0 = 168 < 300 (132 spare).

**RA host bound.** Worst in-run path: 206 + 30 + 1 + 420 + 202 + max(90; 176) = 1035 (revision 2: 1005 without the settle wait; the follow-up round changes 210 and 172 to 206 and 176 and keeps the sum). Host bound 1200 (same limits as A2).
- 1035 ≤ 1140 (105 spare).
- Revision 6 (ruling R6 item 12; S-P3-8): the worst path also counts the stop of 100 after a failed unwind start (cases 1, 2 and 5): 206 + 30 + 1 + 420 + 202 + max(90; 176 + 100) = 1135 ≤ 1140 (5 spare); the same sum was 210 + 30 + 1 + 420 + 202 + max(90; 172 + 100) before the follow-up round. This is the RA worst in-run path. The case 5 restart path (259) is shorter than the normal plus put-back path.
- From the stop command RA needs at least 202 + 176 + 100 = 478 remaining (474 = 202 + 172 + 100 before the follow-up round), measured against the in-run limit of 1140 (the host bound 1200 minus 60), otherwise a stop before any effect (ruling R6 item 12; ruling R7 item 3, P3-3). The added 100-second stop is host time, not gap; the gap bound stays 380. The reserve is a check constant of the two gates (`remaining >= reserve`) and rises by 4 with the put-back walk row; at the end of the gate 1140 − (206 + 30 + 1 + 420) = 483 remains against the reserve 478.
- 1235 ≤ 1270; 1275 ≤ 1300.
- RA lost-receipt wait: the revision 1 value of 1035 is below the new host-process bound 1200 + 30 = 1230, so the wait rises to 1335 (105 spare).
- RA after a lost A2 receipt, and the next RA attempt after a lost RA receipt (revision 3): the wait of 1335 from the start of the earlier job covers the A2 and RA host-process bound 1230 (105 spare).

## 12. Residual risks and unknowns

The risks of main packet section 11, follow-up packet section 9 and collector packet section 6 apply. Added or changed by this packet:
- **Manager reload with a running stream and collector timer.** Revision 8 A2 a01 ran one reload while the stream and the calendar timer were active, with no market change; RA(r8) and this packet's A2 each run one under the same rules. A reload may recompute the timer's next elapse. Persistence is off in the c1 gate record and the gate refuses any other value, so the reload cannot start a catch-up run. Mitigations: the timer-window rule, the pre-write re-check, the empty job list at the reload, and the re-checks after the reload, after the start and at the delayed check. A market change after the reload ends the job after readiness for an owner decision (section 6.4).
- **Process identity drift.** A normal stream restart, or a staging API or trading-worker restart, since the process reference (the AR a01 record or a later AR record for A1; the A1 record or an AR record for A2) stops A1 or A2 before any effect and spends that GO (section 6.3). Root's path: GO-AR, then a new GO for the next attempt under a contingency job ID; the GO wording does not change. Cost: one extra read-only GO and the new GO of the next attempt. The stream restarts on a venue tick or step change, so this path is likely; the reference chain through A1 shortens the window to the time between A1 and A2. Revision 4 (ruling R4 items 7 and 10): the A2, A2M and RA tool is accepted before GO-A1 is requested, so that window holds no build or review; Root may request one AR without a drift right before the GO-A2 request, only when more than 6 hours pass between AR a01 and the GO-A2 request (section 6.1 item 11), and GO-AR and GO-A2 may come in one owner message; the GO-A2 request states the observed stream restart rate. A difference of the staging API, the trading worker or the stream at the A1 end reading leads to GO-AR and, after Root's acceptance, to the staged tree with the AR record as the A2 reference, with no new A1. A transitioning collector service, timer or research worker at the A1 gate is a pre-effect stop, not a drift.
- **Mixed-revision pair.** The API runs the pin and the trading worker runs `ba1a433`. The worker's loaded bytes do not change and the neutrality record proves its closure equal, but the scan reads literal specifiers only; a path built at run time would escape it. Every later GO, receipt, binding update and handoff names both revisions; GO lines that name only "pinned revision ba1a433" for the pair need the U-4 rule of section 10 (recorded on 28 October 2026).
- **RM and RC availability while the re-pin layer is live** (U-4 record, 28 October 2026). RM and RC stay admissible, but each of these residuals makes them refuse before any effect; none is a safety risk: a pending staging-API job (RM and RC exempt only their own units); a staging API that needs a reload (after `A2_UNWIND_FAILED`, a lost receipt, or the RA need-reload rows); a production-identity change after the shared reload (a reviewed re-baseline is needed); a layer line with a link, a hook or an `Environment=` entry (the A2 layer must stay the exact four lines of section 7); and any edit of the follow-up, collector or main packet file under `docs/`, which gives `PIN_MISMATCH` (put both-revisions notes only in this packet, in rulings or in the Roadmap).
- **Restore source age.** The P4 pre-switch backup ages regardless of this packet, and two market writers now write continuously. A full restore loses more with each day. A refresh is a separate online-backup decision.
- **Layer-name room.** The revision 8 last candidate is expected to fit again after RA(r8): the drop-in list is the post-P5a list, and the archive of the revision 8 layer is not live and carries another pin prefix (section 5.2). A1 returns the boolean, and the A2 gate re-derives it on every run; a false result ends `A2_NO_LAYER_NAME` before any effect. A change of the order rule needs a ruling and a packet revision.
- **A live re-pin layer without a suffix** (ruling R31 item 3). The A2 gate applies the two-form pattern only to files with a pending or archived suffix (`PRE_LAYER_FORM_PRESENT`). Among the layer checks, a live file of either form is refused only when it is outside the A1-recorded drop-in list (`PRE_DROPIN_RECORD`) or makes the name derivation fail (`A2_NO_LAYER_NAME`). A live leftover of the old form that sorts before the P5a flag layer, inside the A1-recorded list, would pass the gate, and RA would then refuse it; a live leftover of the new form always sorts after the P5a flag layer and ends `A2_NO_LAYER_NAME` (ruling R34 item 2). After the accepted RA(r8) none exists: RA(r8) acceptance requires the post-P5a list (section 4A, check 3), the A1 record of this packet must show the list digest equal to the c1 value, and the rule that no live layer sorts after the P5a flag layer also rules out a live new-form layer. A later round may make the gate refuse any live match.
- **Admission-off layer content** (ruling R16 item 1). The revision 8 A1 a01 record holds these digests for the post-P5a list. This packet's A1 reads them again after RA(r8) and exports them privately (section 5.2); until Root accepts this packet's A1 record, the A2 and RA manifests cannot be filled with them.
- **RA baseline after a production change** (ruling R17 item 1). The only accepted record with per-unit production rows is the delayed check r6 a02, which RA uses while the latest receipt reports production equal. No accepted record holds per-unit rows of a later reading. After a production change or an `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE`, RA needs a Root ruling and a packet revision that names a source of unit rows (section 6.13).
- **RX rule is procedural.** RX itself does not refuse while the re-pin layer is live; the Root rule and the consumed-marker tripwire prevent it. The RX gate hardening is a later packet.
- **Hooks and links through targets.** The two-way rule reads direct links only.
- **RC-Q36 memory with a computation.** The gate and delayed-check readings precede the owner's computing requests; A2M reads the memory after them and whether the QR-2 worker thread exists. The worker bounds hold meanwhile: one computation per API process, a worker thread with 256 MB old generation and a 10-second deadline.
- **D pin reach.** With D, Month beyond the coverage budgets, Year and All Time financial reports compute on the shared host again after the window of section 4A (re-map case 4), and so does a range within 30 days with a data-time stop on a degradable coverage limit (section 2.2); the revision 8 A2 a01 timings (about 100 to 150 ms) are the comparison. The financial read limits and the QR-2 worker bounds still bound them, and the A2 gate checks Root's case 4 memory estimate (section 2.4). With P2 the reconciliation compute of the boundary record adds compute per request for the Bots that halted at `EPOCH_UNKNOWN`, in every range (case 4 by analogy, section 2.5).
- **RA reconciling reload.** In a need-reload row RA loads drop-in files that it has checked on disk against the row; a unit-file change outside the drop-in files is not seen before the reload, and the loaded-configuration gate after the reload is the check (case 4: nothing started). Revision 4 (ruling R4 item 3): a need-reload row is admitted only when the A2 or a prior RA progress record shows a rename or archive of the live re-pin layer without a completed reload. A staging API that needs a reload for any other reason (for example a unit-file change outside this packet, which shows as row L0-r, LP-r or LQ-r) refuses before any effect, and Root decides, by a ruling or a packet revision, how that state is reconciled.
- **Rollback-family rule.** A repeated RA after an RA attempt with effects is admissible only under that rule; each attempt needs its own GO-RA, and each can leave the API stopped. Revision 5 (ruling R5 items 2 and 3) excludes by end state: every RA outcome without the API ready on `ba1a433` that ends in a recognised layer state, with the prior records showing the needed facts and production equal to the baseline, has an admissible next RA, and an RA that ends with the API ready on `ba1a433` is terminal. Revision 6 (ruling R6 item 5; A-P3-3, S-P3-5) names every state with no automatic next step and its path: a terminal RA receipt that Root does not accept (a Root ruling or a packet revision); `RA_UNWIND_FAILED` (case 6; a packet revision); `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE` (the RD-32 state; a next RA only with Root's recorded review in its manifest, which sets its production baseline, or, with the API ready on `ba1a433`, Root's acceptance of the receipt after its review; ruling R6 item 4); a staging API stuck transitioning past the settle wait in a state other than automatic restart, or the activating state with a start sub-state (a Root ruling); a repeated case 4 in an -n row with no progress (a Root ruling); and a lost A2 receipt with an unreadable or missing progress record (a packet revision). A terminated RA attempt never counts as API ready; the next RA follows after the 1335-second wait. The staging API may stay stopped meanwhile. After `A2_UNWIND_FAILED`, RA is admissible only when the files form a recognised row and production equals the baseline; otherwise Root rules (ruling R5 item 3; S-P3-6(c)). Under Shape R, for RA(r8) any such packet revision is a new file and reopens Q-B (section 4A, stop states; second opinion d1 P2-1).
- **Market start after RM or RC before A2** (ruling R4 item 11; A-P3-8, S-P3-7(b)). The start families are retired before GO-A2, and RM and RC only stop. A market start after GO-RC or GO-RM, before A2, therefore needs its own packet or Root ruling, and A2 waits for it.
- **Restart count after a start** (ruling R13 item 2, which amends ruling R12 item 14). Root has no host fact on the reset rule, and the host service manager version is unknown, so the tool must be right under a start that resets the count and under a start that keeps it. The last recorded staging API restart count is 0 (collector start receipt c1-a01, 28 October 2026; for this packet the AR a01 record after RA(r8) gives the current count), which lowers the risk for the A2 main start but does not remove it for RA or the A2 unwind. Residual: in the reset form, a start followed by exactly n0 automatic restarts before the first running snapshot is accepted; the readiness window and its checks still apply.
- **A2 terminated at the pending write** (ruling R13 item 15; tester reading 4). An A2 terminated at the pending-write `ATTEMPTED` line leaves the API stopped in row L0-n, and the next RA refuses `PRE_ROW_UNRECOGNISED`. This fails closed. Recovery needs a Root ruling, a packet revision and its own GO.
- **Budget stop after the RA start, past the deadline** (ruling R13 item 15; tester reading 5). It sends no stop. Reaching it needs one adapter call to overrun its finite limit by about 60 seconds or more.
- **Walk cap and IMMEDIATE cap, unmeasured** (ruling R19 section 3; ruling R21 item 7). The 4-second tree walk cap and the 50-second IMMEDIATE cap are not measured on the host. A slow walk refuses before an effect at a gate and sends no start before a start. Three places fail closed on a false timeout of the walk: the RA put-back start, the RA case 5 restart and the A2 unwind start send no start and leave the API stopped, with why TREE, though the tree may be right (ruling R24 item 6; ruling R25 item 5, audit-r9 P3-7; the run ends `RA_UNWIND_START_FAILED` or `A2_UNWIND_API_STOPPED`, and the next step is the one of the outcome tables). The A2 stop-grace path (149) and the pre-write abort path (99) also end in the walked unwind start and include the walk (section 11.1). A slow IMMEDIATE pass ends in a terminal code and sends no command.
- **RA stop-command asymmetry** (ruling R25 item 2). In RA case 5, when the stop command failed and the snapshot read shows the API stopped with no job, the run ends `RA_STOP_NOT_PROVED` with `stopProved` false; the A2 counterpart (`A2_UNWIND_API_STOPPED`) records `stopProved` true. RA fails closed and follows the list of ruling R14 item 3. A failed API read with no job in the same case ends `RA_UNWOUND` with `stopProved` true.
- **Tool comments predate G1** (ruling R26 item 7; audit-r7 P3-2; second-r7 P3-4). Some generated comments in the A2RA core still say that a failed state counts as stopped, without the job condition. The packet quotes G1, not the comments, and the behaviour follows G1. The comments are corrected in the next code round, if one comes. This is a known cosmetic item.
- **Served-URL rule tightenings** (ruling R24 item 3, reading 6). The rule is the P5a regex plus three tightenings: at most 1024 bytes, no `..` segment and no leading `//`. Reading 6 is accepted on condition that Root's dry admission passes against the real P5a served list (ruling R24 item 7); a served list that a tightening refuses blocks the admission before any GO.
- **API state after the run limit** (ruling R19 item 4(b); ruling R21 item 7). After the in-run limit or, while unwinding, the unwind limit (section 11) is passed, the one read-only class read for `apiAtEnd` can fail to run before the host deadline; `apiAtEnd` is then `UNKNOWN` with `apiAtEndWhy` `LIMIT`, in A2 and in RA. The rule that a throw after a start command that is not followed by a proved stop ends `A2_UNWIND_API_STOPPED` or `RA_STOP_NOT_PROVED` applies only while readiness of that start is not proved; after readiness is proved the check codes stand (amendment R19a; ruling R22 item 2; ruling R23 items 3, 4 and 7).
- **A torn NOTE line** (ruling R13 item 15; second opinion P3-8). A `NOTE` line that is itself torn fails closed.
- **In-flight scan of A2M and AR** (ruling R12 items 15 and 17). The scan fails closed on a process it cannot read, so an unreadable process table refuses, though host health is never a refusal. It is also recorded as an ADP residual (ruling R12 item 15; ruling R14 item 15).
- **Late stderr after a lost probe race** (ADP residual; ruling R12 item 15; ruling R14 item 15). Recorded as a residual; this packet adds no rule for it.
- **Escape form of envMap** (ADP residual; ruling R12 item 15; ruling R14 item 15). Recorded as a residual; this packet adds no rule for it.
- **Functional proof.** The owner's browser check is the only functional proof on staging (ruling D17). The owner's port forwarding drops often; Root never reopens it.
- **Shared reload and production identity.** Each reload is host-wide and shared with the production units. The D30 normalisation held across the P5a reload; any other reload-visible production change stops before the API start (unwind) or after readiness.
- **Webhook gap.** Staging alerts and webhooks are not served during each API gap (bounds 310 seconds for A2 and 380 seconds for RA).
- **Inert trees accumulate** in the stage directory (no delete); A1 re-checks free disk.
- **Reboot.** The staging API does not start at boot; after a reboot the re-pin layer applies at the next manual start.
- **Revision 8 pin drift before GO-RA(r8)** (change map K2). Any edit of the installed revision 8 file or of a pinned record of revision 8 (A0, retirement, U-4) kills RA(r8) at run time (`PIN_MISMATCH` or a pin check). Root runs the pin-drift check and the dry admission right before the GO request (section 4A).
